a book on jurisprudence according to the views of a particular imam, presenting his doctrine as he understands and sees it, even though someone else may know that imam’s doctrine and wording better and understand his intentions more fully. For people’s transmission of the doctrines of the imams may resemble their transmission of the revealed Law.

It is well known that someone may say, “Allah’s ruling is such-and-such,” or, “The ruling of the revealed Law is such-and-such,” according to what he believes the Prophet taught, based on what has reached him and how he has understood it—even though someone else may know the Prophet’s words and deeds better and understand his intent more fully. This, too, is something that occurs frequently among human beings. Thus, reports transmitted from the imams may differ, just as some hadith scholars differ in what they transmit from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. The Prophet, however, is protected from error. He therefore cannot make two statements that genuinely contradict one another, nor issue two genuinely contradictory commands unless one abrogates the other. Anyone other than the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is not protected from error: he may make two contradictory statements or issue two contradictory commands without recognizing the contradiction.

Yet if what is transmitted from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, requires discernment and knowledge—with reports sometimes differing so that some carry greater weight than others, and with considerable disagreement among those who convey his Law through inference—then it is hardly surprising that something similar should occur in what is transmitted from others. Indeed, this is all the more to be expected, because Allah has guaranteed the preservation of the Reminder that He sent down to His Messenger, but has not guaranteed the preservation of what is transmitted from anyone else. For the Book and wisdom with which Allah sent His Messenger constitute Allah’s guidance, which has come from Him, through which His path is known, and which is His proof against His servants;

---

## Page 1491

so if error were to enter it and go unexposed, Allah’s proof in that regard would be nullified, His guidance lost, and His path obscured; for no prophet is awaited after this Prophet to clarify for people the matters over which they have differed. Rather, this Messenger is the last of the messengers, and his community is the best of communities. Hence, by Allah’s permission, a group within it will always stand firm upon the truth, unharmed by those who oppose or abandon it, until the Hour comes.

The second point:

Abū al-Faraj himself is inconsistent on this subject: he has maintained neither a consistent position of denial nor one of affirmation. Indeed, in both verse and prose, he has affirmed many of the attributes he denies in this work. In this respect, he resembles many people of various backgrounds who engage in this subject, sometimes affirming and sometimes denying in numerous matters concerning the attributes. Such is also the case with Abū al-Wafā’ ibn ‘Aqīl and Abū Hāmid al-Ghazālī.

The third point:

Affirmation is not exclusive to the Hanbalīs, nor do they exhibit forms of excess absent from other groups. Rather, anyone who surveys people’s doctrines will find in every group extremists in denial and affirmation whose like are not found among the Hanbalīs, and will find that any of them who inclines toward an erroneous denial or an erroneous affirmation

---

## Page 1492

does not go to the extremes of others who incline toward denial or affirmation. Indeed, among the various groups you will find excesses in erroneous denial and erroneous affirmation whose like are not found among the Hanbalīs. Such transgression in denial and affirmation arose among them only through influences that crept in from others who overstepped Allah’s limits through excessive denial and affirmation. For the Sunnah rests fundamentally upon moderation and balance, not wrongdoing and transgression.

The knowledge possessed by “Imam Ahmad and his followers” was marked by a completeness and comprehensiveness well known to specialists and ordinary people alike who have some familiarity with the Sunnah and its adherents. As for the ignorant and misguided—those who do not know what Allah sent the Messenger with, and cannot distinguish authentic transmitted evidence and clear rational evidence from fabricated reports and confused opinions—they are ignorant of the standing of the Messenger and the earliest forerunners among the Emigrants and Helpers, whose merit the Quran proclaims. They are therefore all the more likely to be ignorant of the standing of the imams who oppose such people, since they more closely resemble those who oppose the Messenger and follow a path other than that of the believers endowed with knowledge and faith. In these circumstances, they are closer to disbelief than to faith.

You find one of them speaking about “the fundamental principles and subsidiary matters of religion” as though he had neither grown up in a Muslim land nor heard the teachings upheld by the people of knowledge and faith; as though he knew nothing of this community’s early generations and the excellence they were granted in beneficial knowledge and righteous deeds, nor knew enough of what Allah sent His Prophet with to distinguish guidance from misguidance, and error from right direction.

---

## Page 1493

You also find that these people’s attacks on “the imams of the Sunnah and the guides of the community” resemble the attacks of the Rāfidīs and their hypocritical allies on Abū Bakr, Umar, and the leading Emigrants and Helpers; the attacks of the Jews, Christians, and their followers among this community’s hypocrites on the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace; and the attacks of the Sabians and polytheists, philosophers and others, on the prophets and messengers. In His Book, Allah has recorded statements made by disbelievers and hypocrites against the prophets and messengers and the people of knowledge and faith. These contain lessons for those who reflect, clear evidence for those who seek insight, and admonition for those who are bewildered and confused.

You find that most speculative theologians and those who have turned away from the path of the early generations—except those whom Allah protects—venerate the leaders of monism even after these men have explicitly expressed monism in their books. They strain to interpret those statements in ways their authors never intended. Allah knows the extent of the reverence and veneration they hold for these men in their hearts, and how readily they attest to their standing as imams and saints, and as masters of spiritual realities.

Here is Ibn Arabī, explicitly declaring in his Fusūs (Bezels) that sainthood is greater than prophethood—indeed, more perfect than messengership. Among his words are:

“Prophethood occupies an intermediate station,
Just above the messenger, yet below the saint.”

Some of his followers interpret this to mean that a prophet’s sainthood is superior to his prophethood, and likewise that a messenger’s sainthood is superior to his messengership. Alternatively, they take his sainthood to be his state in relation to Allah, and his messengership to be his state in relation to creation. This is profound ignorance. For when the messenger addresses creation and conveys the message to them, he does not cease to possess sainthood; rather, he is a friend of

---

## Page 1494

Allah in that state, just as he is a friend of Allah in all his other states. He is Allah’s friend, never His enemy in any state. Nor is his state when conveying the message inferior to his state when he prays, supplicates to Allah, and communes with Him.

Moreover, what does this man who strains to interpret his words make of this venerated man’s statement that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is a silver brick, whereas he himself is two bricks, one gold and one silver? He claims that Muhammad’s brick, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, represents outward knowledge, while his own two bricks represent inward knowledge—the gold—and outward knowledge—the silver—and that he receives this without an intermediary. In his Fusūs (Bezels), he explicitly states that the rank of sainthood is greater than the rank of prophethood because the saint receives without an intermediary, whereas the prophet receives through an intermediary. Thus, the distinction by which he claims superiority over the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is, in his estimation, greater than what he shares with him.

In short, he has not followed the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, in anything. For he claims to have received from Allah even those things in which he outwardly follows him, just as one jurist exercising independent judgment may agree with another, or one messenger with another. In his view, therefore, he neither follows the Messenger nor receives anything from him at all, whether truths conveyed in revealed reports or truths concerning religious law.

Furthermore, he was not content for his relationship with the Messenger to resemble that of Moses with Jesus, or one scholar with another, regarding the religious law in which he agreed with him. Rather, he claimed that he receives inwardly from Allah the religious law that he acknowledges the Messenger to be correct in teaching. His reception of religious law from Allah would thus be greater than the Messenger’s reception of it.

---

## Page 1495

As for that in which he claimed to differ from the Messenger, and which he claimed the Messenger needed—represented by the golden brick—he alleged that he receives it from the very source from which the angel who conveys revelation to the Messenger receives it. Such, as you can see, is this man’s position, yet some later figures venerate him. Al-Ghazālī explicitly stated that killing someone who claims that the rank of sainthood is higher than the rank of prophethood is dearer to him than killing a hundred unbelievers, because the harm such a person does to religion is greater. We shall not dwell on this matter, since it is not our concern here.

Moreover, in their view, the names of Allah and the names of His attributes are established by religious law and transmitted revelation; they may not be applied on the basis of opinion alone. They therefore have greater grounds for being excused for refraining from using these names than those who refrain from calling His attributes “accidents.”

For some of our attributes are accidents, such as knowledge and power, while others are bodies and self-subsisting substances, such as the face and the hand. Calling these “limbs and organs” is more specific than calling them “bodies,” since the former terms imply acquisition, benefit, and action, as well as the possibility of being separated and of constituting parts.

---

## Page 1496

The fourth point:

This question is not confined to these people. Rather, the early generations of the Muslim community and its leading authorities in jurisprudence, hadith, Sufism, and spiritual knowledge agreed on affirming this category of attributes, as did the leading speculative theologians of the Kullābī, Karrāmī, and Ashʿarī schools. All of them affirm for Allah the attributes of the face, the hand, and the like.

Al-Ashʿarī stated in his book al-Maqālāt (The Doctrinal Positions) that this is the position of the scholars of hadith, and declared that he himself held it. In summarizing the position of Ahl al-Sunnah and the scholars of hadith, he wrote:

“The position of Ahl al-Sunnah and the scholars of hadith, in summary, is to affirm such and such; that Allah rose over His Throne; and that He has two hands, without specifying how, as He says:

‘I created with My two hands,’

and as He says:

‘Rather, both His hands are outstretched’;

and that He has two eyes, without specifying how, as He says:

‘Sailing under Our eyes’;

and that He has a face, as He says:

‘And there will remain the Face of your Lord, Owner of Majesty and Honor.’”

We have already explained that all the leading authorities of these groups are among those who affirm the divine attributes. There is nothing that Abū al-Faraj or others have mentioned as occurring among the Hanbalīs—whether the truth lies with the one who affirms it or the one who denies it, or whether the matter requires distinctions—that is not also found among as many as Allah wills

---

## Page 1497

among the scholars of hadith, the Sufis, the Mālikīs, the Shāfiʿīs, the Hanafīs, and others like them. Indeed, this is also found among groups that do not profess adherence to the Sunnah and the community, to hadith, or to the way of the early generations, such as the Shīʿah and others. Among them, both affirmation and negation reach extremes not found among the aforementioned groups.

Likewise, these opposing positions of negation and affirmation are found among the followers of the two Scriptures—the Torah and the Gospel. The Sabians, including philosophers and others, likewise hold opposing positions on negation and affirmation. Some of them even affirm what many speculative theologians who affirm the divine attributes do not affirm. In general, however, affirmation predominates among the followers of the messengers: the believers, the Jews, the Christians, and the rightly guided Sabians. Negation generally predominates among those who do not follow the messengers: the polytheists and the Sabians who introduce religious innovations.

Elsewhere, outside this response, we have set out the position of the early generations of the Muslim community and its leading authorities, quoting their own words and those of the people from all the various groups who transmitted their position. This leaves no group with an exclusive claim to affirming the divine attributes.

One example is what the shaykh of the Two Holy Sanctuaries, Abū al-Hasan Muhammad ibn ʿAbd al-Malik al-Karajī, mentioned in his book, which he entitled “Chapters on the Fundamentals, Drawn from the Eminent Imams, to Confute Those Given to Innovation and Idle Talk.” He was one of the leading Shāfiʿī authorities. In it, he quoted statements by al-Shāfiʿī, Mālik, al-Thawrī, Ahmad ibn Hanbal, and al-Bukhārī—the compiler of the Sahīh—

---

## Page 1498

and Sufyān ibn ʿUyaynah, ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mubārak, al-Awzāʿī, al-Layth ibn Saʿd, and Ishāq ibn Rāhawayh on the fundamentals of the Sunnah, through which their beliefs may be known. In their biographies, he highlighted their rank and standing in Islam. He also stated:

“He confined his quotations to them, rather than others, because they are the authorities whose example is followed and whose schools are consulted in the East and the West. They fulfil the qualifications for exemplary religious leadership more completely than others and possess more fully its requisite means and capabilities: excellent memory, insight, discernment, and knowledge of the Book, the Sunnah, consensus, chains of transmission, narrators and their circumstances, the varieties of Arabic and their contexts of usage, history, what abrogates and what is abrogated, transmitted and rational knowledge, and what is sound and what is flawed. They also surpass others in truthfulness, steadfastness, and manifest trustworthiness and religious devotion.”

He said:

“If any one of them fell short in one of these respects, his chronological proximity to the Companions and those who followed them in righteousness compensated for that shortcoming. In this respect, these men were distinguished from others. Other imams, though they held the position of religious leadership, nevertheless fell short of some of its qualifications, which I have indicated in general terms, since this is not the place to explain them in detail.”

He said:

“There is a third reason that we must explain. We say: quoting these authorities establishes a binding argument against anyone who professes adherence to the school of an imam whose creed he opposes. For one of the two must inevitably judge the other to be misguided, accuse him of innovation, or declare him an unbeliever. Thus, professing adherence to his school—while disagreeing with him

---

## Page 1499

in creed—is, by Allah, reprehensible both according to religious law and by natural disposition. Whoever says, ‘I am Shāfi'ī in law and Ash'arī in creed,’ we tell him: ‘This is a contradiction—indeed, it is apostasy—for al-Shāfi'ī was not Ash'arī in creed.’ Whoever says, ‘I am Hanbalī in subsidiary rulings and Mu'tazilī in fundamentals,’ we tell him: ‘Then, by your own claim, you have strayed from the straight path, for Ahmad was not Mu'tazilī in his religion or his independent reasoning.’”

He said:

“Many Mālikīs have also been seduced by Ash'arī doctrines. By Allah, this is a disgrace, a shame, and a lapse that brings ruin, punishment, and an evil abode upon anyone professing adherence to the schools of these great imams. For their position is what we have transmitted: they declared the Jahmiyyah, the Mu'tazilah, the Qadariyyah, and the Wāqifah unbelievers, and likewise declared the Lafziyyah unbelievers.”

He discussed the question of utterance at length, until he said:

“As for the imams other than those we have mentioned, no one has professed adherence to their schools; therefore, we have not undertaken to quote them.”

He said:

“If it is asked: ‘Why, then, did you not confine your quotations to those scholars of hadith whose schools became widespread and whose preferred positions were adopted—namely, the imams al-Shāfi'ī, Mālik, al-Thawrī, and Ahmad? For we see no one professing adherence to the school of al-Awzā'ī, al-Layth, or the others.’

“We reply: ‘Because the other imams whom we mentioned were also, broadly speaking, leaders of schools, since they were authorities followed in their own times. Their schools were subsequently subsumed under those of the recognized imams. Thus, Ibn 'Uyaynah was an authority whose example was followed, but he did not compile a work on

---

## Page 1500

the legal rulings he preferred. Rather, it was his students—al-Shāfi'ī, Ahmad, and Ishāq—who compiled works, and his school was consequently subsumed under theirs.

“As for al-Layth ibn Sa'd, his students did not preserve and uphold his school. Al-Shāfi'ī said: ‘He was not blessed with students.’ His position, however, agrees with either Mālik’s or al-Thawrī’s, never falling outside the two; thus his school was subsumed under theirs.

“As for al-Awzā'ī, on most questions we find no position of his that does not agree with that of Mālik, al-Thawrī, or al-Shāfi'ī. His preferred positions were therefore also subsumed under theirs. Likewise, Ishāq’s preferred positions fall within Ahmad’s school because the two agree.’”

He said:

“If it is asked, ‘Where did you find this detailed explanation of how these scholars’ schools were subsumed under those of the imams?’ I reply: ‘In al-Ta'līqah (The Commentary) by Shaykh Abū Hāmid al-Isfarāyīnī—a compendium of religious laws and a source of remarkable insights, expounding legal rulings, the schools of eminent scholars, and the foundations of their weighty arguments on matters of disagreement and agreement.’”

He said:

“As for the preferred positions of Abū Zur'ah and Abū Hātim concerning prayer and legal rulings—as I have read them and heard them transmitted from their two compilations—they agree with Ahmad’s position and fall within it. This is well known.

“As for al-Bukhārī, I have not encountered any preferred positions of his, but I heard the hadith scholar Muhammad ibn Tāhir say that al-Bukhārī deduced rulings among his preferred positions that accord with the schools of Ahmad and Ishāq. For these reasons, we quoted the scholars whom we named rather than others, for they are leaders of

---

## Page 1501

the legal schools as a whole, and they are worthy of being followed because they possess the qualifications for such leadership. Others do not hold the same rank, even if they are great imams who have followed in their footsteps.”

He then presented the twelfth chapter, summarizing the imams’ explicit statements after having devoted a separate chapter to each of them. He said:

“When I examined the source works that I could reliably transmit, I found in them the beliefs of the imams that I have already mentioned. I then arranged these according to the sequence of the chapters I included, and prefaced each” chapter “with a number of their praiseworthy qualities, bearing witness to their standing as imams and calling upon people to follow them, affirming the obligation to agree with them and the prohibition against opposing them or breaking ranks with them. For following the imams we have mentioned in matters of foundational belief in our time is equivalent to following the consensus transmitted to us from the Companions and their successors: no Muslim may oppose it, nor is he excused for doing so. The truth does not lie outside their positions, for they are the guides and leaders of this community’s schools, its foremost figures and eminent scholars who lead the way—men of faith and piety, truthfulness and trustworthiness, abundant knowledge and evident scholarly exertion. It is for this reason that people have followed them in practical legal matters, making them intermediaries between themselves and Allah in those matters, until they became the leaders of the legal schools throughout the East and West. Let people likewise accept them in matters of foundational belief concerning their relationship with their Lord, and accept what they explicitly affirmed and called others to follow.”

He said:

“For we know with certainty that they are unquestionably better acquainted with what is authentically established of the beliefs of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and of his Companions after him. This is because of the soundness of their knowledge, their possession of the qualifications for leadership, and the closeness of their time to that of the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and his Companions, as we explained at the beginning of the book.”

---

## Page 1502

He said:

“Then I wished—and a request from some of the brothers coincided with my wish—to present a summary of their explicit statements, incorporating some of their own wording. Such a summary is easier to memorize and contains the essence of the book. So I sought the help of Him upon whom all reliance rests, and said:

“The explicit statements we have selected fall into two chapters: the first explains the sunnah and its merit; the second concerns shunning innovation and its adherents.

“As for the first chapter, know that the ‘sunnah’ is the way of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Following the sunnah means walking that path and conforming to it. It comprises ‘three categories’: sayings, deeds, and beliefs.

“Sayings include the transmitted formulas of remembrance and glorification of Allah. Deeds include the aforementioned sunnah practices of prayer, fasting, and charity, as well as approved ways of conduct and transmitted manners. These two categories comprise practices that are strongly encouraged and recommended, through which reward and recompense are earned. The third category is the sunnah concerning beliefs, which is one of the foundations of faith.”

He said:

“Now, with Allah’s help, I shall present a summary of what I have transmitted from them in scattered passages, adding material recorded in works on foundational belief that has not reached me from them at all. I shall arrange this material in a carefully prepared form, interweaving some of their explicit statements and using the briefest wording I can, so that it may be easy to memorize for anyone wishing to retain it. I therefore say: Let the follower of the sunnah know that the sunnah concerning beliefs falls into ‘three categories’: one concerns Allah’s names, essence, and attributes; another concerns the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, his Companions, and his miracles; and another concerns the people of Islam in this life and the Hereafter.

---

## Page 1503

As for the first category:

Let us believe that Allah has eternal, uncreated names and attributes, conveyed in His Book and reported by the Messenger to his Companions in narrations transmitted by trustworthy narrators and authenticated by reliable hadith critics. The clear Quran and sound, firmly established hadith attest to them.”

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—said:

“They are as follows: Allah Most High is the First, who has always existed, and the Last, who will never cease to exist; the One, the Eternal, the Self-Sufficient, the Generous, the All-Knowing, the Forbearing, the Most High, the Magnificent, the Exalted, the Glorious. His retribution is severe. He originates and restores, and does whatever He wills. He is the Strong, the All-Powerful, the Unassailable, the Helper:

‘There is nothing like unto Him, and He is the All-Hearing, the All-Seeing.’

“And so too His other names and attributes: His Self, face, eye, foot, two hands, knowledge, looking, hearing, sight, will, volition, pleasure, anger, love, laughter, wonder, shyness, protective jealousy, dislike, wrath, grasping, spreading out, nearness, proximity, being above, highness, speech, greeting with peace, utterance, calling, manifestation, meeting, descent, ascent, and rising over the Throne; and that He Most High is in heaven, upon His Throne, distinct from His creation.

“Mālik said: ‘Allah is in heaven, and His knowledge is everywhere.’

“Abd Allāh ibn al-Mubārak said: ‘We know our Lord to be above His seven heavens, upon the Throne, distinct from His creation. We do not say, as the Jahmiyya say, that He is here’—and he pointed to the earth.

“Regarding ‘And He is with you wherever you are,’ Sufyān al-Thawrī said: ‘His knowledge.’

“Al-Shāfiʿī said: ‘He is upon His Throne in His heaven, drawing near to His creation however He wills.’

“Ahmad said: ‘He has risen over the Throne and knows every place.’ He descends every night to the lowest heaven however He wills, and He will come on the Day of Resurrection however He wills

---

## Page 1504

and He rises above His Seat. We believe in the Throne and the Seat, and in the verses and reports concerning them. Good words ascend to Him, and the angels and the Spirit ascend to Him. He created Adam with His two hands, created the Pen, the Garden of Eden, and the tree of Tūbā with His two hands, and wrote the Torah with His two hands. Both His hands are right hands.

Ibn Umar said:

‘Allah created four things with His two hands: Adam, the Throne, the Pen, and the Garden of Eden. To the rest of creation He said, “Be,” and it was.’

He speaks revelation however He wills. Āʾisha—may Allah be pleased with her—said:

‘In my own estimation, my affair was too insignificant for Allah to speak concerning me in revelation that would be recited.’

The Quran, in all its aspects, is the speech of Allah, revealed and uncreated. Not a single letter of it is created. It originated from Him and will return to Him. Abd Allāh ibn al-Mubārak said:

‘Whoever disbelieves in a single letter of the Quran has disbelieved. Whoever says, “I do not believe in this letter lām,” has disbelieved.’

The books revealed to the messengers—one hundred and four books—are the uncreated speech of Allah. Ahmad said:

‘What is in the Preserved Tablet, what is in the written copies of the Quran, and people’s recitation—however it is recited and however it is described—is the uncreated speech of Allah.’

Al-Bukhārī said:

‘I say: The Quran is in the written copy, and the Quran is in men’s hearts. Whoever says otherwise is to be called upon to repent; if he repents, so be it; otherwise, his course is that of disbelief.’”

He said: Al-Shāfiʿī set forth the creed with its proofs, saying:

“Allah has names and attributes conveyed by

---

His Book, and which His Prophet has made known to his community. No one among Allah’s creation against whom the proof has been established may reject them.” He later said:

“Examples include Allah—glorified is He—informing us that He is All-Hearing, All-Seeing; that He has two hands, as He says:

‘Rather, both His hands are outstretched.’

That He has a right hand, as He says:

‘And the heavens will be folded up in His right hand.’

That He has a Face, as He says:

‘Everything will perish except His Face.’

And:

‘And there will remain the Face of your Lord, full of majesty and honour.’

That He has a foot, as stated in the report:

‘Until the Lord places His foot in it’—meaning Hell.

That He laughs at His believing servant, as the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said concerning the man killed in Allah’s cause:

‘He met Allah, who was laughing toward him.’

That He descends every night to the lowest heaven, as reported by the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him; and that He is not one-eyed, as the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, said when mentioning the Dajjāl:

‘He is one-eyed, but your Lord is not one-eyed.’

That the believers will see their Lord on the Day of Resurrection with their own eyes, just as they see the moon when it is full; and that He has a finger, as the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

‘There is no heart except that it is between two of the fingers of the Most Merciful.’”

He said:

“Besides what al-Shāfiʿī transmitted, there are hadiths recorded in the authentic collections and the Musnad collections, which the Muslim community has accepted and affirmed as true. These include the hadith concerning the Divine Essence in the authentic collection, and his saying:

‘No person has greater protective jealousy than Allah.’

And his saying:

‘Are you surprised at Saʿd’s protective jealousy? By Allah, my protective jealousy is greater than Saʿd’s, and Allah’s is greater than mine.’

And his saying:

‘No one loves praise more than Allah, and for that reason He has praised Himself. No one has greater protective jealousy than Allah; for that reason He has forbidden

immoral deeds, both open and concealed.’ And his saying:

‘Allah’s hand is full.’

And his saying:

‘In His other hand is the balance, which He lowers and raises.’

And his saying:

‘On the Day of Resurrection, Allah will grasp the earths, and the heavens will be in His right hand. Then He will say: “I am the King.”’

Similar to this is his saying:

‘Three of the Lord’s handfuls.’

And his saying:

‘When He created Adam, He wiped his back with His right hand.’

And his words in the hadith of Abū Razīn:

Your Lord will then take a handful of water in His hand and sprinkle it toward you. By the life of your God, not one of your faces will be missed by a drop of it.”’

Ahmad recorded it in the Musnad. There is also the hadith concerning:

‘The handful with which He brings out of the Fire people who have never done any good and have been reduced to charcoal. He then casts them into one of the rivers of Paradise, called the River of Life.’

And reports such as the hadith:

‘I saw my Lord in the most beautiful form.’

And his saying:

‘He created Adam in His image.’

And his saying:

‘One of you will draw near to his Lord until He places His shelter over him.’

And his saying:

‘He spoke to your father face to face.’

And his saying:

‘There is not one of you whose Lord will not speak to him, with no interpreter between them to interpret for him.’

And his saying:

‘Our Lord will reveal Himself to us on the Day of Resurrection, laughing.’

In the hadith of the Ascension recorded in the authentic collection:

‘Then the Compeller, the Lord of Might, drew near and came closer, until He was two bow-lengths from him or nearer.’

And his saying:

‘He wrote a book, which is with Him above the Throne:

---

## Page 1507

“My mercy has preceded My wrath.”’ And his saying:

‘Hell will continue to have people cast into it, while saying, “Is there any more?” until the Lord of Might places His foot in it—in another narration, His leg. Then its parts will contract together, and it will say, “Qad, qad (Enough, enough!)”; in another narration, “Qatt, qatt (Enough, enough!), by Your might!”’

And sayings such as:

‘Then Allah will come to them in His form, which they recognize, and say, “I am your Lord.” They will say, “You are our Lord.”’

And his saying:

‘Allah will gather His servants and call to them in a voice heard by those far away just as it is heard by those nearby: “I am the King; I am the Judge.”’

And other such hadiths, whether they alarm us or not, and whether they have reached us or not. Our belief concerning them and the verses pertaining to the divine attributes is that we accept them without distorting them, specifying their manner, denying them, or reinterpreting them. We do not make them conform to reason or liken them to the attributes of created beings. We do not bring our own opinions and speculation to bear upon them, nor do we add to them or subtract from them. Rather, we believe in them and entrust knowledge of them to the One who knows them, as did the righteous predecessors, who are our example in every branch of knowledge.

It has been transmitted to us from Ishāq that he said:

‘We do not divert any attribute with which Allah has described Himself, or with which the Messenger has described Him, from what it is, either in speech or in intent. The Muslim is only required to convey these descriptions and to be certain in his heart that what Allah has described Himself with in the Quran are indeed His attributes. Neither a prophet sent by Allah nor an angel brought near to Him comprehends those attributes except through the names by which the Lord, Mighty and Majestic, has made them known to them. As for any descendant of Adam apprehending those attributes, no one can apprehend them—and so on to the end of the report.

---

## Page 1508

’. Likewise, we have transmitted from Mālik, al-Awzā‘ī, Sufyān, al-Layth, and Ahmad ibn Hanbal that they said concerning the hadiths about seeing Allah and His descent:

‘Leave them as they have been transmitted.’

Likewise, it has been reported from Muhammad ibn al-Hasan—the companion of Abū Hanīfa—that he said concerning hadiths such as “Allah descends to the lowest heaven” and other similar hadiths: These hadiths have been transmitted by trustworthy narrators, so we transmit them and believe in them, but do not explain them.”

Here end the words of al-Karkhī, may Allah Most High have mercy on him.

What is astonishing is that when these speculative theologians are confronted with the divine attributes mentioned in the Quranic verses and hadiths, they say, “The Hanbalīs say that Allah is such-and-such,” thereby vilifying them and promoting their own falsehood. Yet the Hanbalīs, unlike others, have followed in the footsteps of the righteous predecessors, proceeded as they proceeded, and stopped where they stopped. Allah is the One who grants success.

The second point is that this discourse contains no argument or evidence worthy of being addressed to people of knowledge. Anyone can respond with mere insults and alarmist rhetoric. Even someone debating polytheists and the People of the Book would be obliged to present evidence demonstrating the truth he upholds and the falsehood they uphold. For Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said to His Prophet

---

## Page 1509

—may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Invite to the way of your Lord with wisdom and good instruction, and argue with them in a way that is best.”

And the Most High said:

“And do not argue with the People of the Scripture except in a way that is best.”

Even if the opponent of someone who speaks in this manner—whether the speaker is Abū al-Faraj or someone else—belonged to one of the sects most notorious for innovation, such as the Rāfidah, he ought to present evidence and avoid what serves no purpose when undertaking to refute them; let alone (1) when those disputing with him—as he claims—are acknowledged by everyone to be more knowledgeable than he is about fundamentals and subsidiary matters. Yet in his discourse and rebuttal he has presented no evidence whatsoever, whether scriptural or rational. Rather, he has relied on blindly following a group of speculative theologians opposed by a greater number of speculative theologians. He has followed them in what they claimed was rational evidence, just as this objector has done. Anyone who seeks to refute others through reason must set out a rational argument; otherwise, he merely refers people to unknown entities, such as the Rāfidah’s infallible imam and the Sufis’ ghawth.

As for his statement, “Such people are not to be spoken to,” we reply: Allah sent the messengers to all creation to call them to Him. Who among mankind, then, has Allah exempted from being addressed? Let alone those whom you and others know, whom Allah has favored in ways that need not be discussed here. If a foolish person wished to answer this critic with a rebuttal like his own, he would have no difficulty doing so.

Likewise, concerning his statement, “They defy reason,” we say:

Defiance of reason

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) As in the original.

---

## Page 1510

lies either in their affirming what they have affirmed, or in their contradicting themselves by combining (1) the affirmation of these matters with the denial of bodily organs.

As for the first possibility, it is false. No one has ever said that the position of outright corporealists, who explicitly affirm corporeality and take it to extremes, defies reason; nor has anyone said that they are not to be addressed. Rather, those who refuted extreme corporealists—such as Hishām ibn al-Hakam and his followers—used only rational arguments requiring examination and inference. Their opponent, although wrong in much of what he says, has countered them with arguments comparable to their own. Their case against him was no clearer than his against them, for each group holds both truth and falsehood.

And if someone such as “Abū al-Faraj ibn al-Jawzī” relies solely, in denying these matters, on what speculative thinkers who advocate negation put forward, then those thinkers scarcely ever claim that any affirmation or denial amounts to defiance of reason. Even concerning those who deny the Creator—who are the most ignorant, misguided, and unbelieving of all people, and the most opposed to reason—most of those whose support Abū al-Faraj has enlisted do not claim that their position defies reason. Rather, they claim that its falsity can be known only through examination and inference. Although most of these advocates of negation among the speculative theologians maintain this view, it is not a satisfactory approach.

The point, however, is that these advocates of negation do not claim that knowledge of the falsity of

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) As in the original and the printed edition.

---

## Page 1511

the position of those who affirm is self-evident, nor that their position defies reason. Although they denounce them for things that repel many people, they do so to enlist that aversion in opposing them and suppressing their position—not because people’s aversion to them establishes truth or falsehood, nor because their position defies reason or its falsity is known through rational necessity or immediate intuition. I know of no leading speculative thinker among the advocates of negation who makes such a claim about any of his opponent’s affirmative positions, however extreme they may be.

It is well known that mere aversion on the part of those who recoil, or approval on the part of those who agree, establishes neither the soundness nor the falsity of a position, unless it is grounded in guidance from Allah. Rather, appealing to this as evidence amounts to appealing to the pursuit of desires without guidance from Allah.

For a person to follow his desires is to accept the statements and actions he likes and reject those he dislikes, without guidance from Allah. The Most High says:

“And indeed, many lead others astray through their desires without knowledge.”

He also says:

“But if they do not respond to you, then know that they only follow their desires. And who is more astray than one who follows his desire without guidance from Allah?”

The Most High said to David:

“And do not follow desire, lest it lead you astray from the way of Allah.”

The Most High also says:

“If they testify, do not testify with them. And do not follow the desires of those who deny Our signs and those who do not believe in the Hereafter and who set others equal to their Lord.”

The Most High also says:

“Say, ‘O People of the Scripture, do not exceed the bounds of truth in your religion, and do not follow the desires of a people who went astray before, led many astray, and strayed from the right path.’”

The Most High also says:

“The Jews will never be pleased with you

---

## Page 1512

nor the Christians until you follow their religion. Say, ‘Indeed, Allah’s guidance is the guidance.’ And if you were to follow their desires after the knowledge that has come to you, you would have neither protector nor helper against Allah.”

Whoever follows people’s desires after receiving the knowledge with which Allah sent His Messenger, and after the guidance Allah has made clear to His servants, is in this very position. This is why the early generations called the people of innovation and division—those who oppose the Quran and the Sunnah—“followers of desires”: they accepted what they liked and rejected what they disliked according to their desires, without guidance from Allah.

As for the objector’s statement regarding Abū al-Faraj:

“It is as though they were addressing children.”

The Hanbalīs have addressed people only with what has been transmitted from Allah, His Messenger, his Companions, and those who followed them in righteousness. These are the people most knowledgeable about Allah and His rulings. We defer to them in matters of religious law, and they are our examples in what they have conveyed about Allah and His law. Whoever refers to them has acted fairly. Whoever departs from their way has set himself in opposition, claiming that others know more about Allah than they do; or that they knew but concealed their knowledge, and that they did not understand what they conveyed; or that others’ reason in (the subject of knowledge of Allah) is fuller, more complete, and better informed than what they transmitted and understood. We have already presented enough on this subject. Allah alone grants success.

“Whoever Allah does not grant light has no light.”

---

## Page 1513

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him and sanctify his soul—said:

Section

Statements are of two kinds:

The first kind consists of statements authentically transmitted from the prophets. These are free from error, and their meaning is necessarily true, whether people understand it or remain ignorant of it. Inquiry into them concerns only what the prophets intended. Whoever seeks to understand their intent through the means by which it can be known has followed the path of guidance. But whoever seeks to subordinate their words to his own views—accepting them if they agree with him, otherwise rejecting them and contriving distortions that he calls “interpretation,” despite knowing with certainty that much, or most, of this was not intended by the prophets—is distorting words from their proper places, not seeking the interpretation known to those firmly grounded in knowledge.

The second kind consists of statements not transmitted from the prophets. Those other than the prophets are not protected from error, so a person’s words should neither be accepted nor rejected until his intended meaning is understood and its soundness or unsoundness determined.

---

## Page 1514

Thus, if one of the speculative theologians says that Allah does not bring things about through causes, but merely when those causes occur, not through them; that He does not act for a wise purpose; and that commanded acts contain nothing that makes them good, nor forbidden acts anything that makes them evil—this contradicts the texts of the Quran and Sunnah and the consensus of the early generations of the Muslim community.

The first to say this in Islam was Jahm ibn Safwān, whose misguidance the Muslim community unanimously acknowledged. He was the first to deny causes and natural properties, just as he was the first known to advocate denying the divine attributes, and the first to declare Allah’s speech created and deny that He will be seen in the Hereafter.

The texts of the Quran and Sunnah refuting this are very numerous, such as His words:

“We said, ‘O fire, be coolness and safety for Abraham.’”

He thus deprived the fire of its nature. And His words:

“That We may bring forth thereby grain and vegetation.”

And His words:

“Until, when they have carried heavy clouds”

He stated that the winds carry the clouds—that is, bear them—thus describing something inanimate as acting according to its nature. He also said:

“It stirred, swelled, and brought forth vegetation.”

He thus described it as acting according to its nature. And His words:

“So We caused every noble kind to grow therein.”

“Noble” means highly beneficial, and “kind” means a category.

There is abundant evidence in which He states that He creates through causes and for wise purposes. He also states that He upholds justice and does not wrong people in the least. Thus, He puts nothing where it does not belong, neither treats unlike things alike nor distinguishes between like things, as He said:

“Or do those who commit evil deeds think that We will make them”

—and the rest of the verse. And He said:

“Or shall We treat those who believe and do righteous deeds like those who spread corruption on earth?”

—and the rest of the verse. And He said:

“Shall We then treat the Muslims

---

## Page 1515

like the criminals?”

—and the rest of the verse. He also said:

“The blind and the seeing are not equal,”

“Nor is darkness”

—and the rest of the verse. There are many other such passages. And His words:

“Those who follow the Messenger, the unlettered Prophet”

—and the rest of the verse.

This verse and others indicate that what He commands them to do is inherently good, recognized as such by hearts, suited to them, and capable of remedying their corruption. Its being good does not mean merely that it is commanded, since that is a shared characteristic. Thus, what He commands His Messenger to do has a distinctive character; what He forbids is distinguished by being reprehensible and to be avoided; what He permits is distinguished by being wholesome; and what He prohibits is distinguished by being foul. There is much of this in the Quran and in other scriptures, such as the Torah, the Gospel, and the Psalms. And Allah, glorified and exalted is He, knows best.

---

## Page 1516

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—said:

Inferring that something is reprehensible because it is a religious innovation (*) is a great, general principle. A complete treatment of it requires answering the objections raised against it.

Some people say that innovations fall into two categories, citing ʿUmar’s statement, “What an excellent innovation!” They also cite things introduced after the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—which are not reprehensible, as established by evidence from scholarly consensus and analogy. Those who do not apply the principles of knowledge may also invoke customary practices followed by many people. They are like those who, when told:

“Come to what Allah has revealed and to the Messenger,”

reply:

“Sufficient for us is what we found our forefathers following.”

How many people associated with scholarship or worship are cited as authorities on grounds that have no basis in the principles of knowledge! People of learning may offer such a person a justification drawn from scriptural evidence, yet Allah knows that his advocacy of these practices and his observance of them are not founded on that evidence. He cites it only to fend off whoever debates him.

Praiseworthy debate consists only in presenting the grounds on which one’s statements and actions are based.

Some people say that innovations fall into two categories: good and bad. > Those who have not mastered the foundations of knowledge may also invoke the many customary practices followed by many people]
> [They are like those who, when told, ‘Come to what Allah has revealed and to the Messenger,’ reply, ‘Sufficient for us is what we found our forefathers following.’
> How many people associated with scholarship or worship are cited as authorities on grounds that have no basis in the foundations of knowledge!]
> [People of learning may offer a justification for this drawn from scriptural evidence, yet Allah knows that their advocacy and practice of it are not founded on that evidence. They cite it only to fend off those who debate them.
> Praiseworthy debate consists in presenting the grounds and explaining the evidence on which statements and actions are actually based. Presenting anything else is a form of hypocrisy in knowledge and practice.]
> [This is a ‘principle’ established by the sunnah and consensus, together with the Quran. Allah Most High said: ‘Or do they have partners who have prescribed for them a religion that Allah has not authorized?’
> Whoever, through words or actions, recommends or makes obligatory something by which one seeks nearness to Allah, without Allah having prescribed it, has prescribed as religion something Allah has not authorized. Whoever follows him in this has taken him as a partner alongside Allah, prescribing for him a religion that Allah has not authorized. He may be forgiven on account of an interpretation if he is exercising independent scholarly reasoning (ijtihād) of the kind in which an error is excused. Nevertheless, it is not permissible to follow him in this, as Allah Most High said: ‘They have taken their rabbis and monks as lords besides Allah.’ Whoever obeys someone in a religious matter that Allah has not authorized—whether declaring something lawful, forbidden, recommended, or obligatory—incurs a share of this blame, just as the person issuing the commands and prohibitions does. Both may, however, be excused, so that the blame does not apply because a necessary condition is absent or an impediment is present, even though its underlying cause exists. Blame attaches to someone who recognizes the truth but abandons it; who falls short in seeking it, so that it does not become clear to him; or who turns away from seeking it out of desire, laziness, or the like.
> Furthermore, Allah reproached the polytheists for two things:
> First, associating with Him that for which He had sent down no authority.
> Second, forbidding what Allah had not forbidden them, as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, explained in the hadith of ‘Iyād [recorded by] Muslim. Allah Most High said: ‘Those who associate partners with Allah will say: “Had Allah willed, neither we nor our forefathers would have associated partners with Him, nor would we have forbidden anything.”’ Thus, they combined associating partners with Allah (shirk) with declaring things forbidden. Shirk encompasses every act of worship that Allah has not authorized, for the polytheists claim that their worship is either obligatory or recommended. Some worshipped others besides Allah to draw nearer to Him through them; others invented a religion through which they worshipped Allah, as with the forms of worship introduced by the Christians.
> The root of misguidance among the people of the earth arose from these two things: either adopting a religion that Allah did not prescribe or forbidding what Allah did not forbid.
> Accordingly, the foundational principle upon which Ahmad and others built their legal doctrines is that actions comprise acts of worship and customary practices. The default rule for acts of worship is that only what Allah has prescribed may be instituted; the default rule for customary practices is that only what Allah has forbidden may be prohibited. These newly introduced seasonal observances were prohibited only because of the religious practices introduced into them as means of drawing nearer to Allah].

---

## Page 1517

whereas putting forward other grounds is a form of hypocrisy in knowledge and practice. This is a “principle” established by the Sunnah and scholarly consensus, as well as the Book. Allah Most High says:

“Or do they have partners who have ordained for them in religion what Allah has not permitted?”

Whoever recommends something as a means of drawing nearer to Allah, or makes it obligatory through his words or actions, without Allah having prescribed it, has ordained in religion what Allah has not permitted. Whoever follows him in that has taken a partner alongside Allah who has ordained in religion what Allah has not permitted. He may, however, be forgiven on account of an interpretation if he exercises independent scholarly judgment of the kind for which one who errs is excused. Nevertheless, it is not permissible to follow him in that, as Allah Most High says:

“They have taken their rabbis and monks as lords besides Allah.”

Whoever obeys anyone in a religious matter that Allah has not authorized—whether declaring something lawful, unlawful, recommended, or obligatory—incurs a share of this blame, just as the person issuing the commands and prohibitions does. Yet both may be pardoned. The blame may therefore not apply because a condition for it is absent or an impediment to it is present, even though the grounds for it exist. Blame applies to someone to whom the truth has become clear but who abandons it; someone who falls short in seeking it, so that it does not become clear to him; or someone who turns away from seeking it out of desire, laziness, or the like.

Furthermore, Allah reproached the polytheists for two things:

“First”: associating with Him that for which He had sent down no authority.

“Second”: prohibiting what Allah had not prohibited, as he—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—explained in the hadith of

---

## Page 1518

Iyād, as recorded by Muslim. And Allah Most High says:

“Those who associate others with Allah will say, ‘If Allah had willed, neither we nor our forefathers would have associated others with Him, nor would we have forbidden anything.’”

Thus they combined associating partners with Allah with declaring things forbidden. Associating partners with Allah includes every act of worship that Allah has not authorized, for the polytheists claim that their worship is either obligatory or recommended. Some worshipped others besides Allah to draw nearer to Allah through them, while others invented a religion through which they worshipped Allah, as the Christians did when they introduced new acts of worship.

Misguidance among the people of the earth originated in these two things alone: adopting a religion that Allah had not prescribed, or prohibiting what He had not prohibited.

For this reason, the foundational principle upon which Ahmad and others built their legal schools was that actions comprise “acts of worship and customary practices.” The governing rule for acts of worship is that nothing may be prescribed except what Allah has prescribed; the governing rule for customary practices is that nothing may be prohibited except what Allah has prohibited. These newly introduced observances were prohibited only because of the religious practices introduced into them as means of drawing nearer to Allah.

---

## Page 1519

Shaykh al-Islām Ahmad ibn Taymiyyah—may Allah sanctify his soul—was asked about a man who said:

“If Muslims follow the authority of others, and Christians and Jews do likewise, on what grounds can Christians and Jews be refuted and their doctrines invalidated under these circumstances? What is the conclusive proof that establishes the truth of the Muslims’ position and refutes the falsehood of the unbelievers?”

He replied—may Allah be pleased with him:

Praise be to Allah. Whoever says this is lying and misguided in this assertion. Blameworthy imitation consists of accepting another person’s statement without proof, as did those of whom Allah said:

“And when it is said to them, ‘Follow what Allah has revealed,’ they say, ‘Rather, we will follow what we found our forefathers following.’”

Allah Most High says:

“Even though their forefathers understood nothing and were not guided?”

And He says:

“Indeed, they found their forefathers astray.”

“So they hastened to follow in their footsteps.”

There are many similar passages in the Quran. Thus, whoever follows the religion of his forefathers and predecessors out of ingrained habit and abandons following the truth

---

## Page 1520

which he is obliged to follow—is precisely the blameworthy imitator. This is the condition of the Jews and Christians, and indeed of the adherents of innovation and desires within this community who have followed their religious teachers and leaders in falsehood. As Allah Most High says:

“On the Day their faces are turned about in the Fire, they will say, ‘Oh, how we wish we had obeyed Allah and obeyed the Messenger!’”

“And they will say, ‘Our Lord, we obeyed our masters and our leaders, and they led us astray from the path.’”

“‘Our Lord, give them double the punishment and curse them with a mighty curse.’”

Allah Most High also says:

“And on the Day the wrongdoer bites his hands, he will say, ‘Oh, how I wish I had taken a path with the Messenger!’”

“‘Woe to me! I wish I had not taken so-and-so as a close friend!’”

Up to His words: “a deserter.”

Allah Most High also says:

“When those who were followed disown those who followed them, and they see the punishment, and all ties between them are severed.”

Up to His words: “And they will never leave the Fire.”

Allah Most High also says:

“And when they dispute in the Fire, the weak will say to those who were arrogant, ‘We were your followers, so can you relieve us of any portion of the Fire?’”

Up to His words: “Indeed, Allah has judged between His servants.”

These and similar passages make clear that whoever obeys a created being in disobedience to Allah will have a share of this condemnation and punishment.

A person who obeys a created being in disobedience to Allah and His Messenger either follows conjecture or follows his own desires; many follow both. This is the condition of everyone who disobeys the Messenger of Allah: the polytheists; the People of the Book, namely the Jews and Christians; and the people of innovation and wickedness within this community. As Allah Most High says:

---

## Page 1521

“They are nothing but names you and your forefathers have given, for which Allah has sent down no authority.”

Up to His words: “And guidance has indeed come to them from their Lord.”

“Authority” is the Book revealed by Allah; it is the guidance that has come to them from Allah. As Allah Most High says:

“Or have We sent down to them an authority that speaks in support of what they associate with Him?”

He also says:

“Indeed, those who dispute concerning Allah’s signs without any authority having come to them.”

Up to His words: “attain it.”

And He says to the children of Adam:

“If guidance should come to you from Me.”

Up to His words: “And the punishment of the Hereafter is more severe and more lasting.”

The explanation is this: either it is made clear to a person that what Allah sent His Messenger with is the truth, yet he turns away from it to follow his own desires; or he thinks that his abandonment of it is the truth. The latter follows conjecture, while the former follows his own desires . . . (1) the combination of the two: Allah Most High says, describing the former:

“It is not you they deny, but it is Allah’s signs that the wrongdoers reject.”

Allah Most High also says:

“They rejected them, though inwardly convinced of their truth, out of wrongdoing and arrogance. So see what became of those who spread corruption.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Those to whom We have given the Scripture recognize it as they recognize their own sons.”

Up to His words: “conceal the truth knowingly.”

And Allah Most High says, describing those who suffer the greatest loss:

“Say, ‘Shall we inform you of those who lose most through their deeds?’”

## Page 1522

—to the end of the verse. He also says:

“What of one whose evil deeds have been made appealing to him, so that he regards them as good? Indeed, Allah sends astray whom He wills and guides whom He wills.”

The first is the condition of those who have incurred Allah’s wrath: they know the truth but do not follow it, as is found among the Jews. The second is the condition of those who act without knowledge. Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, many lead others astray through their own desires, without knowledge.”

Allah Most High also says:

“And who is more astray than one who follows his own desires without guidance from Allah?”

Everyone who opposes the messengers is an imitator, following someone whom he is not permitted to follow. The same applies to someone who follows the Messenger without insight or understanding: he submits outwardly without faith entering his heart. He is like the person who is asked in the grave: “Who is your Lord? (1) What is your religion? Who is your prophet?” He replies: “Ah, ah! I do not know. I heard people saying something, so I repeated it.” He is an imitator. He is then struck with an iron sledgehammer and lets out a scream that everything except human beings can hear. If a human being heard it, he would be struck down—that is, he would die.

Allah Most High has said:

“The Bedouins say, ‘We have believed.’ Say, ‘You have not believed; rather, say, “We have submitted,” for faith has not yet entered your hearts.’”

Whoever has not had faith enter his heart, but is outwardly a Muslim, is therefore among the blameworthy imitators. Thus, if it is clear that an imitator is blameworthy—meaning someone who follows the desires of a person whom it is not permissible to follow—such as someone who abandons obedience to Allah’s messengers and follows his masters and leaders, or follows the Messenger outwardly

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) The printed edition reads mā rabbuka (“What is your Lord?”). The reading adopted here is from Imam Ahmad’s Musnad, 4:287–288.

---

## Page 1523

without faith in his heart—it becomes clear that all Jews and Christians engage in blameworthy imitation, as do the hypocrites of this community.

As for the adherents of religious innovation, there is both righteousness and wickedness among them. This can be explained in several ways.

First: the Jews and Christians who claim to follow Moses and Jesus—may Allah bless them both and grant them peace—follow them only because they are Allah’s messengers. Every means by which the prophethood of Moses and Jesus is established applies with still greater force to Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

For example, if Jews and Christians say: “It has been established through mass transmission that Moses and Jesus claimed prophethood and that signs demonstrating their truthfulness appeared at their hands. They also brought religious teachings and revealed law that could not, as is evident, have been brought by a lying fabricator—signs demonstrating their truthfulness appeared at their hands—but could only have been brought by a truthful prophet claiming prophethood.”

The reply is: each of these two lines of evidence establishes the prophethood of Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—with even greater force. For it is well known that those who transmitted the religious teachings and revealed law to which Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—called, and who transmitted the miraculous signs he brought, surpass those who transmitted similar accounts about Moses and Jesus. Moreover, what he brought in both these respects is greater than what Moses and Jesus brought.

Indeed, anyone who at the present time rationally considers the beneficial knowledge and righteous deeds found among Muslims, and what is found among Jews and Christians, would recognize that there is between the two

---

## Page 1524

a difference greater than that between a raging flood and sweat.

For what Muslims possess of the affirmation of Allah’s oneness; knowledge of His names and attributes, His angels, prophets, and messengers; and knowledge of the Last Day, the nature of Paradise and Hell, reward and punishment, and promises and warnings is far greater and more sublime than what Jews and Christians possess. This is clear to anyone who investigates the matter.

The outward and inward acts of worship practised by Muslims—such as the five daily prayers and other prayers, remembrance of Allah, and supplications—are greater and more sublime than those practised by the People of the Book.

Their revealed law concerning transactions, marriage, legal rulings, prescribed punishments, and penalties is greater and more sublime than that of the People of the Book. Thus, Muslims surpass them in every kind of beneficial knowledge and righteous deed. This becomes apparent to anyone upon even the slightest consideration and requires little effort.

Muslims agree that whatever guidance and goodness they receive has come to them only through their Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Given this, how could Moses and Jesus be prophets while Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—is not a prophet, and the Jews and Christians be upon the truth?

---

## Page 1525

For the guidance and religion of truth they possess are greater than what the Jews and Christians possess, and they received this only from their Prophet.

Every rational person among the Jews and Christians acknowledges this much: they acknowledge that the religion of the Muslims is true, that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—and that whoever among them obeys him will enter Paradise. Indeed, they acknowledge that Islam is better than their own religion. The philosophers likewise unanimously agree on this, as Ibn Sīnā and others have stated: “The philosophers of the world agree that no religious law greater than this can come to the world.”

Yet those who do not follow him reassure themselves that they are not obliged to follow him, arguing that he is a messenger to the unlettered Arabs, not to the People of the Book: “If his religion is true, ours is also true, and there are various paths to Allah Most High.” They liken this to the legal schools of the imams: although one school may be preferred over another, the followers of the other schools are neither disbelievers nor People of the Book.

This is the specious argument that leads astray those who consider themselves clever among the People of the Book, the philosophizers, and others like them. Its falsity is evident. For just as it is known with indisputable certainty through mass transmission that he called the polytheists to faith, it is known with equal certainty that he called the People of the Book to believe in him, and that he fought the People of the Book just as he fought the polytheists. He fought Banū Qaynuqā‘, Banū al-Nadīr, Qurayzah, and the people of Khaybar—all of whom were Jews—and took their children and women captive and their wealth as spoils. He also campaigned against the Christians in the year of Tabūk, both in person and through his military detachments; indeed, among those killed fighting them was Zayd ibn Muhammad

---

## Page 1526

his freedman, whom he had adopted, as well as Ja‘far and others of his household; and he imposed the jizyah (tribute) on the Christians of Najrān.

Likewise, his rightly guided caliphs after him waged jihad against the People of the Book, fought those who fought them, and levied the jizyah upon those among them who paid it by hand, in a state of submission.

This Quran, which everyone knows to be the Book he brought, is filled with calls to the People of the Book to follow him. It declares those among them who do not follow him to be disbelievers, censures and curses them, and warns them of punishment, just as it declares those polytheists who do not follow him to be disbelievers, censures them, and warns them of punishment. As Allah Most High says:

“O you who were given the Scripture, believe in what We have sent down, confirming what is with you”—the verse.

The Quran’s addresses, “O People of the Book” and “O Children of Israel,” are so numerous that counting them would require considerable effort. Allah Most High also says:

“Those who disbelieved among the People of the Book and the polytheists were not to desist”—the verse, through His words, “the best of creation.”

There are very many passages like this in the Quran. Allah Most High has also said:

“Say, ‘O mankind, I am the Messenger of Allah to you all, to Whom belongs the dominion of the heavens and the earth.’”

And Allah Most High says:

“We have sent you only to all mankind.”

It has been widely transmitted from him—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—that he said, “I have been favored over the prophets in five respects,” including his statement:

“A prophet used to be sent specifically to his own people, whereas I have been sent to all mankind.”

Indeed, it has been established through mass transmission from him—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—that he was sent to both jinn and humankind. Thus, once it is known with indisputable certainty through mass transmission—as widely transmitted as the emergence of his call—that he called the People of the Book to

---

## Page 1527

believe in him, and that he declared those among them who did not believe in him to be disbelievers; that he commanded fighting them until they embraced Islam or paid the jizyah by hand, in a state of submission; that he fought them personally and through his military detachments; and that he imposed the jizyah upon them, killed their fighting men, took their children captive, and seized their wealth as spoils.

He besieged Banū Qaynuqā‘ and then expelled them to Adhri‘āt. He besieged Banū al-Nadīr and then expelled them to Khaybar; concerning this, Allah revealed Sūrat al-Hashr. He then besieged Banū Qurayzah when they broke their covenant, killed their men, took their dependants captive, and seized their wealth. Allah Most High mentioned this in Sūrat al-Ahzāb. He fought the people of Khaybar until he conquered it, killed some of their men, took some of their dependants captive, and divided their land among the believers. Allah Most High mentioned Khaybar in Sūrat al-Fath. He imposed the jizyah upon the Christians, concerning whom Allah revealed Sūrat Āl ‘Imrān. He also campaigned against the Christians in the year of Tabūk, concerning which Allah revealed Sūrat Barā’ah.

Most of the Medinan surahs—such as al-Baqarah, Āl ‘Imrān, al-Nisā’, al-Mā’idah, and others—contain so many calls and addresses to the People of the Book that this fatwa could not accommodate even a tenth of them.

Then his successors, Abū Bakr and ‘Umar, together with the Emigrants and Helpers who accompanied them—who are known to have followed him most closely, obeyed his commands most faithfully, and upheld his covenant most carefully—campaigned against the Romans just as they campaigned against the Persians, and fought the People of the Book just as they fought the Magians. They fought those who fought them and imposed the jizyah upon those among them who paid it by hand, in a state of submission.

---

## Page 1528

Among the authentic hadiths reported from him is his saying, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“By Him in whose hand is my soul, no Jew or Christian among the people to whom I have been sent hears of me and then fails to believe in me without entering the Fire.”

Sa‘īd ibn Jubayr said: “This is confirmed in the Book of Allah Most High:

‘Whoever among the factions disbelieves in it, the Fire is his promised destination.’”

The meaning of this hadith is established through mass transmission from him and is known beyond doubt. Since this is so, it necessarily follows that he is Allah’s Messenger to all groups. For he affirms that he is Allah’s Messenger to the People of the Book and others. Allah’s Messenger does not lie, fight people to compel their obedience without Allah’s command, or deem their blood, wealth, and homes lawful to take without Allah’s permission.

Anyone who claimed that Allah had commanded him to do this and then did it, when Allah had not commanded him to do so, would be a liar, a fabricator, and a wrongdoer:

“Who is more unjust than one who invents a lie about Allah or says, ‘It has been revealed to me,’ when nothing has been revealed to him?”

Besides being a wrongdoer and a fabricator, he would be among the foremost seekers of domination and corruption on earth, and would be more wicked than tyrannical, oppressive kings. For tyrannical kings who fight people to compel their obedience do not say, “We are Allah’s messengers to you; whoever obeys us will enter Paradise, and whoever disobeys us will enter the Fire.” Even Pharaoh and his like do not make such claims. Only a truthful prophet or a lying claimant to prophethood, such as Musaylimah, al-Aswad, and their like, makes such claims.

Thus, once it is known that he is a prophet, in whatever capacity, it necessarily follows that everything he reports from Allah is true. If he is Allah’s Messenger, he must be obeyed in everything he commands, as Allah Most High says:

“We did not send any messenger except to be obeyed by Allah’s permission.”

And if he states that he is Allah’s Messenger to the People of the Book

---

## Page 1529

and that they are obliged to obey him, this must be true. Anyone who acknowledges that he is Allah’s Messenger but denies that he was sent to the People of the Book is like someone who says: “Moses was a messenger, but he was under no obligation to enter the land of Syria or to lead the Children of Israel out of Egypt; Allah did not command him to do so, nor did He command him to observe the Sabbath, reveal the Torah to him, or speak to him on Mount Sinai.” He is also like someone who says that Jesus was Allah’s Messenger but was not sent to the Children of Israel, that they were under no obligation to obey him, and that he wronged the Jews—or makes similar statements expressing the gravest disbelief.

For this reason, Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, those who disbelieve in Allah and His messengers and wish to distinguish between Allah and His messengers and say, ‘We believe in some and disbelieve in others’”

up to His words:

“But those who believe in Allah and His messengers and make no distinction between any of them,”

and the rest of the verse.

He also says to the Children of Israel:

“Do you then believe in part of the Scripture and disbelieve in part?”

up to His words:

“And Allah is not unaware of what you do.”

This clear, manifest, and conclusive line of argument demonstrates to every Muslim, Jew, and Christian that the religion of the Muslims is the truth, not that of the Jews and Christians. It rests on two premises:

- (The first is that the prophethood and mission of Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and the guidance of his community are more evident and clearer. They can be established by every means through which the prophethood of Moses and Jesus, peace and blessings be upon them both, can be established, and by additional means besides. It is therefore impossible to affirm that they were prophets while denying that he was one. If a person wishes, he may establish this through the call itself and what he brought; or, if he wishes, through the Book with which he was sent; or, if he wishes,

---

## Page 1530

through the beliefs and practices of his community; or, if he wishes, through the miracles with which he was sent. Whichever of these approaches establishes the prophethood of Moses and Jesus establishes the prophethood of Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, still more clearly and fully.

(The second premise is that he declared his mission to be universal, addressed to all the inhabitants of the earth, polytheists and People of the Book alike, and that he was not sent to some people to the exclusion of others. This is known self-evidently, through mass-transmitted reports, and through conclusive proofs.

As for the Jews and Christians, their religion was originally true, as Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, those who believe, the Jews, the Christians, and the Sabians—whoever believes in Allah and the Last Day and does righteousness—will have their reward with their Lord, and no fear will there be concerning them, nor will they grieve.”

However, both religions have been altered and abrogated. The Jews altered and distorted their religion; then the remainder of their religious law was abrogated through the Messiah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

The very books in the possession of the Jews and Christians—including the prophecies of the prophets, numbering more than twenty, and other writings—show that they have made alterations, that their religious law is to be abrogated, and that the mission of Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is authentic. These books contain announcements and proofs of the prophethood of the final Messenger about which scholars have written entire works. They also contain contradictions and discrepancies that demonstrate that alteration has occurred, as well as reports concerning what follows them that show that they have been abrogated. Thus, they possess evidence establishing these points. We have debated more than one

---

## Page 1531

of the People of the Book and explained this to them. Groups of their scholars and finest members embraced Islam, then began debating their own co-religionists and explaining the evidence they possessed for the prophethood of Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. But this fatwa does not allow for more than this.

This is part of the wisdom in allowing the People of the Book to remain upon payment of the jizyah tax. They possess testimony and evidence for the prophethood of Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, as well as testimony supporting what he taught concerning belief in Allah and the Last Day. This demonstrates that Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, brought the religion with which the messengers before him were sent, and taught concerning Allah’s oneness and attributes what the prophets before him had taught.

Allah Most High says:

“Say, ‘Have you considered: what if it is from Allah and you disbelieve in it, while a witness from the Children of Israel has testified to something similar?’”

And He says:

“Say, ‘Sufficient as a witness between me and you is Allah, and whoever has knowledge of the Scripture.’”

And Allah Most High says:

“So if you are in doubt about what We have revealed to you, ask those who have been reading the Scripture before you.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, neither doubted nor asked. Rather, this is a ruling contingent upon a condition, and a conditional ruling does not apply when its condition is absent. This leaves scope for anyone who doubts, wishes to present an argument, or seeks greater certainty.

---

## Page 1532

Section:

This method is clear when debating the People of the Book.
