If, however, the person addressed does not acknowledge the prophethood of any prophet—neither Moses nor Jesus nor anyone else—there are several ways to address him. One is to approach the discussion between followers of the revealed religions and others—polytheists, Sabians, philosophers, Brahmans, and others—in a manner analogous to the discussion between Muslims and the People of the Book.

We say: Every rational person who gives even the slightest thought and consideration knows that followers of the revealed religions are more accomplished in beneficial knowledge and righteous deeds than those who do not follow these religions. Whatever good is found among non-Muslim followers of the revealed religions is found in a more perfect form among Muslims. Moreover, followers of the revealed religions possess what others do not. This is because knowledge and practice are of two kinds:

One kind is acquired through reason, such as arithmetic and medicine, and practical occupations such as weaving, tailoring, trade, and the like. Followers of the revealed religions possess these things just as others do; indeed, they are more accomplished in them. For when the philosophical sciences—logic, natural philosophy, astronomy, and other disciplines—from the philosophers of India and Greece, together with the sciences of Persia and Rome, reached the Muslims, they refined and corrected them, owing to the excellence of their intellects and the eloquence of their speech; and it was

---

## Page 1533

their treatment of them that was fuller, more comprehensive, and clearer. Every rational and learned person recognizes this.

As for what cannot be known through reason alone, such as knowledge of divine matters and religious teachings, this belongs exclusively to the followers of revealed religions. Some of this knowledge can be established through rational proofs; the scriptural proofs are derived from the revealed message. The messengers guided people and showed them how reason points to these truths. These proofs are thus both rational and scriptural. An opponent of the Messenger therefore cannot say, “These truths are known only through their reports, so using them to establish the truth of those reports is circular reasoning.” Rather, the reply is that, through their integrity, their guidance, and their explanation of what reason can apprehend, these truths have become known through reason, illustrative examples, and rational analogies. Through this knowledge, the truth of what the Messenger—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—brought, and the falsity of his opponents’ claims, become known.

(The second category: what can be known only through the reports of the messengers. This can be established in several ways:

One is that the messengers agree in reporting it without any collusion or prior agreement among them. A person giving a report is either truthful, with his report corresponding to what he reports, or he is not. If his report does not correspond to what he reports, he is either deliberately lying or mistaken. If both error and deliberate falsehood are ruled out, his report must necessarily be true.

It is well known that if one person reports an extensive body of knowledge containing numerous details concerning which error on their part is ordinarily impossible, and another had previously reported it, while it is known with certainty that the two did not collude, and it cannot be maintained that falsehood is possible in such a case, their reports yield certain knowledge, even if one does not know

---

## Page 1534

their circumstances. For example, if someone were to confide in a man in the presence of others, giving a lengthy account containing secrets concerning himself and a man connected with those confidential matters, and another person then came, whom we knew had not colluded with the first informant, and gave the same account of that confidential conversation and those secrets, we would be absolutely certain that both were truthful.

It is well known that Moses conveyed what he conveyed before Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—was sent, and before the Messiah was sent. Everyone acquainted with the life of Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—also knows that he grew up among an unlettered people who read no scripture and had no knowledge of the teachings of the prophets. There was no one among them who knew the contents of the Torah and the Gospel or the prophethood of the prophets.

Yet Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—conveyed teachings about Allah’s oneness, His attributes and names, His angels, His Throne and Footstool, His prophets and messengers, and the accounts of them and of those who denied them, corresponding to what is found in the books of the prophets, including the Torah and other scriptures.

Anyone who reflects upon the Torah and the Quran will recognize that both proceed from the same source of light, as al-Najāshī observed, and as Waraqah ibn Nawfal said: “This is the bearer of revelation who used to come to Moses.”

For this reason, Allah Most High links the Torah and the Quran in passages of this kind, as in His saying:

“Why not

---

## Page 1535

grant him the like of what Moses was given? Did they not previously disbelieve in what Moses was given?” up to His words:

“If you are truthful.”

The jinn said:

“We have heard a Book revealed after Moses, confirming what came before it”—the verse.

And He said:

“Is one who stands upon clear evidence from his Lord, with a witness from Him following it, and before it the Book of Moses as a guide and a mercy…”

And He said:

“They did not esteem Allah as He deserves when they said, ‘Allah has revealed nothing to any human being.’ Say, ‘Who revealed the Book that Moses brought as a light and guidance for mankind?’” up to His words:

“And this is a blessed Book which We have revealed, confirming what came before it.”

This, then, is one line of evidence: everyone who knows what Moses and the prophets before and after him brought, and what Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—brought, knows with certainty that they all convey information about Allah truthfully, and that any departure from the truth, whether through error or lying, is impossible for them—Allah forbid.

Other lines of evidence are clear and conclusive, and will remain known through widespread transmission until the Hour: the circumstances of the prophets’ followers and of those who denied and disbelieved in them—the circumstances of Noah and his people, Hūd and his people, Sālih and his people, Abraham and his people, Moses and Pharaoh, and Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—and his people.

Allah has explained this line of evidence in several places in His Book, as in His saying:

“Before them, the people of Noah denied, as did the factions after them,” up to His words:

“Then how was My punishment!”

And He said:

“If they deny you, the people of Noah, ‘Ād and Thamūd likewise denied before them,”

“and the people of Abraham and the people of Lot.”

---

## Page 1536

“and the people of Midian; and Moses too was denied,” up to His words:

“How many towns We have destroyed while they were committing wrong,” up to His words:

“Have they not travelled through the land, so that they may have hearts with which to understand or ears with which to hear?”

And His words:

“You pass by them in the morning,”

“and at night. Will you not then understand?”

And He said:

“Indeed, in that are signs for those who discern.”

He thus explained that He has left the remains of the punished peoples to be seen, so that they may serve as evidence of Allah’s punishment of them. And He, Most High, said:

“How many generations We have destroyed”—the two verses.

He therefore mentioned two ways by which this is known. The first is what is witnessed and understood by the heart. The second is what is heard. For the accounts of the prophets and those who believed them and denied them have become known to everyone through widespread transmission. People have also seen remains that show that He, glorified is He, punished those who denied the prophets and exacted retribution from them; that the prophets followed the truth which He loves and approves; that those who denied them followed falsehood whose adherents incur Allah’s wrath; and that obedience to the messengers is obedience to Allah, while disobedience to them is disobedience to Allah.

Another line of evidence is knowledge of their striking miracles and compelling signs, established through widespread transmission, and of the impossibility of a miracle occurring at the hands of someone falsely claiming prophethood without contradiction or conflict, as has been explained at length elsewhere.

---

## Page 1537

Another line of evidence is that the messengers brought beneficial knowledge and righteous deeds that every intelligent, discerning person recognizes, and that only an ignorant, misguided person denies. There is no room in this fatwa for a lengthy exposition. Once their truthfulness has been established, it is obligatory to believe everything they have reported, and to judge anyone who believes in some of it and disbelieves in some of it to be an unbeliever.

Allah, glorified and exalted is He, knows best. All praise is due to Allah, Lord of the worlds. May Allah send blessings upon Muhammad, his family, and all his Companions.

---

## Page 1538

Shaykh al-Islām Abū al-‘Abbās ibn Taymiyyah—may Allah sanctify his soul—was asked:

Is the soul pre-eternal or created? Is someone who affirms its pre-eternity to be deemed an innovator? What is the position of the adherents of the Sunnah concerning it? And what is meant by His saying, mighty and majestic is He:

“Say, ‘The soul is of the affair of my Lord’”?

Is it knowledge of its essence, its attributes, or both that is left to Allah Most High? Explain this using the Quran and Sunnah.

He replied—may Allah be pleased with him:

All praise is due to Allah, Lord of the worlds. The human soul is created and brought into being, according to the agreement of the early generations of the Muslim community, its leading scholars, and all other adherents of the Sunnah. Several leading Muslim scholars have reported scholarly consensus that it is created. Among them is Muhammad ibn Nasr al-Marwazī, the renowned imam who was the most knowledgeable scholar of his time concerning consensus and disagreement, or one of the most knowledgeable.

Likewise, Abū Muhammad ibn Qutaybah, discussing the creation of the soul in his Book of Gleanings, said: “The word nasam means ‘souls.’”

He said: “People are unanimously agreed that Allah is the Creator of the body

---

## Page 1539

and the Maker of the soul—that is, its Creator.”

Abū Ishāq ibn Shāqlā wrote in his answer to this question: “You asked—may Allah have mercy on you—whether the soul is created or uncreated.” He said: “This is something about which anyone guided to the truth has no doubt.” Later he said: “The soul is among the things that are created.”

Groups of eminent scholars and shaykhs have discussed this question and refuted those who claim that the soul is uncreated. The hadith master Abū ‘Abdullāh ibn Mandah compiled a substantial book on “The Spirit and the Soul,” in which he cited numerous hadiths and reports from the early generations. It was accepted by Imam Muhammad ibn Nasr al-Marwazī and others, and by Shaykh Abū Ya‘qūb al-Kharrāz, Abū Ya‘qūb al-Nahrajūrī, Judge Abū Ya‘lā, and others.

The great imams explicitly affirmed this, and strongly condemned those who claimed otherwise concerning the soul of Jesus, son of Mary—all the more so concerning anyone else’s soul. Ahmad discussed this in his book refuting “The Heretics and the Jahmiyyah,” saying at its beginning:

“All praise is due to Allah, who, in every age when no messengers are sent, preserves a remnant of people of knowledge who call those who have strayed back to guidance and patiently endure the harm they inflict. Through Allah’s Book they revive the dead, and through Allah’s light they give sight to the blind. How many a victim of Iblīs have they revived, and how many a lost wanderer have they guided! How good their effect upon people, and how evil people’s treatment of them!

“They purge Allah’s Book of the distortions of extremists, the false claims of those who promote falsehood, and the interpretations of the ignorant, who have raised the banners of innovation and unleashed discord. These people disagree over the Book, oppose the Book, and unite in opposing the Book. They make claims concerning Allah, concerning Allah Himself, and concerning the Book of

---

## Page 1540

Allah without knowledge. They use ambiguous language and deceive ignorant people by confusing them. We therefore seek refuge in Allah from the trials caused by those who lead others astray.”

He then discussed passages of the Quran alleged to contradict one another, until he said:

“Likewise, Jahm and his followers directed people to the ambiguous passages of the Quran and hadith, and led many people astray. Among the accounts that have reached us concerning Jahm, the enemy of Allah, is that he was from Tirmidh in Khurāsān. He was given to disputation and argument, and most of his discussions concerned Allah. He encountered some polytheists called (al-Sumaniyyah). They recognized Jahm and said to him, ‘We will debate with you. If our argument prevails over yours, you will embrace our religion; and if your argument prevails over ours, we will embrace yours.’

“Among the questions they put to Jahm was: ‘Do you not claim that you have a god?’

“Jahm replied, ‘Yes, I do (1).’

“They asked him, ‘Have you seen your god?’

“He replied, ‘No.’

“They asked, ‘Have you heard His speech?’

“He replied, ‘No.’

“They asked, ‘Have you detected any scent from Him?’

“He replied, ‘No.’

“They asked him, ‘Have you perceived Him by touch?’

“He replied, ‘No.’

“They asked, ‘How, then, do you know that He is a god?’

“He continued: Jahm was bewildered and, for forty days, did not know whom he worshipped. Then he came up with an argument like that of the Christian heretics. These Christian heretics claim that the spirit within Jesus is Allah’s spirit, from His very essence. When He wishes to bring something about, He enters one of His creatures and speaks through that creature’s tongue, commanding whatever He wishes and forbidding whatever He wishes, while He is a spirit hidden from sight.

“Jahm then devised a similar argument and said to the Sumani, ‘Do you not claim that there is a spirit within you?’

“He replied, ‘Yes, I do (2).’

“Jahm asked, ‘Have you seen your spirit?’

“He replied, ‘No.’

“Jahm asked, ‘Have you heard

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) The printed edition reads “naʿam” (yes), which is incorrect.
> (2) The printed edition reads “naʿam” (yes), which is incorrect.

---

## Page 1541

it speak?’

“He replied, ‘No.’

“Jahm asked, ‘Have you perceived it through the senses or by touch?’

“He replied, ‘No.’

“Jahm said, ‘Likewise, Allah’s face cannot be seen, His voice cannot be heard, and no scent can be detected from Him. He is hidden from sight and is not in one place to the exclusion of another.’”

Imam Ahmad continued his discussion of “the Quran,” “seeing Allah,” and other matters until he said:

“Then Jahm made a claim, saying, ‘We have found a verse in Allah’s Book indicating that the Quran is created.’ We asked, ‘Which verse?’

“He replied, ‘Allah’s words: “The Messiah, Jesus, son of Mary, was only a messenger of Allah and His word which He conveyed to Mary, and a spirit from Him.” Jesus is created.’

“We said, ‘Allah has deprived you of understanding the Quran. Descriptions apply to Jesus that do not apply to the Quran, for Allah describes him as born, an infant, a child, and a boy. He eats and drinks, is addressed with commands and prohibitions, and is subject to promises and threats. Moreover, he is a descendant of Noah and a descendant of Abraham. We may not say of the Quran what we say of Jesus. Have you heard Allah say of the Quran what He said of Jesus?

“‘Rather, the meaning of Allah’s words, “The Messiah, Jesus, son of Mary, was only a messenger of Allah and His word which He conveyed to Mary,” is that the word He conveyed to Mary was His saying to him, “Be!” Jesus thus came into being through “Be!” Jesus is not himself “Be!”; rather, he came into being through “Be!” The word “Be!” is an utterance from Allah, and it is not created.’

“The Christians and the Jahmiyyah lied about Allah concerning Jesus. The Jahmiyyah said, ‘Jesus is Allah’s spirit and His word, but the word is created,’ while the Christians said:

---

## Page 1542

‘Jesus is Allah’s spirit, from Allah’s own essence, and Allah’s word, from Allah’s own essence—just as one says, “This scrap of cloth is from this garment.”’

We said, ‘Jesus came into being through the word; he is not himself the word.’

He said, ‘Allah’s words, “and a spirit from Him,” mean that the spirit came to be in him by His command, just as in His words:

“And He has subjected to you whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on the earth—all from Him.”

Here, too, He means “by His command.” The meaning of “Allah’s spirit” is that it is a spirit which Allah created through His word, just as one says “Allah’s servant” and “Allah’s heaven.”’”

Imam Ahmad thus stated that the Christian heretics are those who say that Jesus’s spirit is from Allah’s own essence. He explained that attributing the spirit to Allah denotes His ownership and creation of it—as when one says “Allah’s servant” and “Allah’s heaven”—not the attribution of an attribute to the one possessing it. How much more, then, does this apply to the souls of all other human beings? He also explained that these heretics who uphold divine indwelling say that when Allah wishes to bring something about, He enters some of His creation.

Shaykh Abū Sa‘īd al-Kharrāz, one of the foremost spiritual masters and imams, and a contemporary of al-Junayd, wrote a work establishing that souls are created. Among the arguments he advanced was the following:

“Had they not been created, they would not have acknowledged His Lordship. When He took the covenant from them—while they were souls in bodily forms like tiny ants—He said to them:

‘Am I not your Lord?’ They said, ‘Yes, we testify.’

He addressed the soul together with the body. Can there be a Lord without one subject to His Lordship?”

He said:

“Moreover, had souls not been created, the Christians would not have been blameworthy for worshipping Jesus, nor for saying, ‘He is the son of Allah,’ and, ‘He is Allah.’”

---

## Page 1543

He said:

“Moreover, were the soul uncreated, it would not enter the Fire. Were it uncreated, it would not be veiled from Allah, concealed within the body, or taken into the power of the Angel of Death; nor would it have a form that could be described. Were it uncreated, it would neither be called to account nor punished; it would neither worship nor experience fear or hope. Furthermore, the souls of believers shine brightly, whereas the souls of unbelievers are black as charcoal. The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

‘The souls of martyrs are in the crops of green birds that feed in Paradise and take shelter in the precincts of the Throne. The souls of unbelievers are in Barahūt.’”

Shaykh Abū Ya‘qūb al-Nahrajūrī said:

“These souls come into being by Allah’s command and are created. Allah created them from the malakūt, just as He created Adam from dust. Any servant who attributes his soul to Allah’s own essence is thereby led into the negation of divine reality. Those who attribute souls to Allah’s own essence uphold divine indwelling and go on to declare all things permissible. They say, ‘Once our souls are purified of the impurities of our lower selves, we have attained union and become free. Our servitude has been lifted from us, and every pleasure involving women, wealth, and other things has become permissible for us.’ These are the heretics of this community.”

He mentioned several doctrines concerning souls, as well as doctrines held by the heretics.

I say:

Know that those who hold the soul to be beginningless fall into two groups: (A group of Sabian philosophers say, “It is beginningless and eternal, but it is not from

---

## Page 1544

the Lord’s essence”—as they also maintain concerning the intellects and celestial souls. Those adherents of religious traditions who have joined them claim that these are the angels.

Another group consists of the heretics and misguided members of this community among the Sufis, speculative theologians, and hadith scholars. They claim that the soul is from Allah’s essence. Their doctrine is even worse than that of the former group. They divide the human being into two halves: a divine half, which is his soul, and a human half, which is his body. Thus, half of him is a lord and half a servant. Allah declared the Christians unbelievers for saying something similar about the Messiah; what, then, of those who extend this claim to everyone—even Pharaoh, Hāmān, and Qārūn?

Everything that establishes that the human being is a created servant subject to his Lord, and that Allah is his Lord, Creator, Owner, and God, also establishes that his soul is created. For the human being comprises both body and soul; indeed, he is defined more by his soul than by his body. The body is merely a mount for the soul, as Abū al-Dardā’ said: “My body is only my mount. If I treat it gently, it will carry me to my destination; if I do not, it will not.”

Ibn Mandah and others have narrated it from Ibn ‘Abbās, who said:

“Disputes among created beings on the Day of Resurrection will continue until the soul and the body dispute with one another. The soul will say to the body, ‘You committed the evil deeds.’ The body will say to the soul, ‘You commanded me.’ Allah will then send an angel to judge between them.

“He will say, ‘You are just like a man who could not walk and a blind man who entered an orchard. The man who could not walk saw fruit hanging there and said to the blind man, “I can see fruit, but

---

## Page 1545

I cannot get up to reach it.” The blind man said, “I can get up to reach it, but I cannot see it.” The man who could not walk said to him, “Come and carry me so that I can pick it.” So he carried him, and the other began directing him wherever he wished to go, until he picked the fruit.’ The angel said, ‘Which of them should be punished?’ They both replied, ‘Both of them.’ He said, ‘The same applies to you two.’”

Furthermore, numerous hadiths have been transmitted from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, establishing that souls are taken at death, granted bliss, or punished. A soul is told: “Come out, O good soul that dwelt in a good body,” or: “Come out, O wicked soul that dwelt in a wicked body.” The former is told: “Receive glad tidings of rest and provision,” while the latter is told: “Receive tidings of boiling water, ghassāq, and other torments of similar kinds.” These hadiths also establish that the souls of believers ascend to heaven, whereas the gates of heaven are not opened for the souls of unbelievers.

In Sahīh Muslim, ‘Abd Allah ibn Shaqīq narrates from Abū Hurayrah, may Allah be pleased with him, who said:

“When the believer’s soul departs, two angels receive it and ascend with it.” Hammād said: “He mentioned its pleasant fragrance and mentioned musk.” He continued: “The inhabitants of heaven say, ‘A good soul has come from the earth. May Allah bless you and the body you inhabited.’ It is then taken to its Lord, who says, ‘Take it away until the appointed term ends.’

“As for the unbeliever, when his soul departs”—Hammād said that he mentioned its stench and a curse—“the inhabitants of heaven say, ‘A wicked soul has come from the earth.’ Then it is said, ‘Take it away until the appointed term ends.’”

Abū Hurayrah, may Allah be pleased with him, said: “When the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, mentioned the stench, he drew the cloth he was wearing over his nose.”

---

## Page 1546

The authentic hadith of the Ascension also relates that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, saw Adam with the souls of his descendants to his right and left. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“When we ascended to heaven, there was a man with figures to his right and figures to his left. When he looked to his right, he laughed, and when he looked to his left, he wept. He said, ‘Welcome to the righteous Prophet and the righteous son!’ I asked, ‘O Gabriel, who is this?’ He replied, ‘This is Adam, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. These figures to his right and left are the souls of his descendants. Those on his right are the people of Paradise, while the figures on his left are the people of the Fire. When he looks to his right, he laughs, and when he looks to his left, he weeps.’”

It has also been established that the souls of believers, martyrs and others, are in Paradise. Imam Ahmad said, as transmitted by Hanbal:

“The souls of unbelievers are in the Fire, and the souls of believers are in Paradise, while their bodies remain in this world. Allah punishes whom He wills and, through His pardon, shows mercy to whom He wills.”

‘Abd Allah ibn Ahmad said:

“I asked my father about the souls of the dead: ‘Are they in the spaces surrounding their graves, or in the crops of birds, or do they die as bodies die?’ He replied, ‘It has been narrated from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, that he said:

“‘“When a believer dies, his soul is a bird that feeds among the trees of Paradise until Allah returns it to his body on the day He resurrects him.”

“‘It has also been narrated that ‘Abd Allah ibn ‘Amr said: “The souls of believers are inside green birds like starlings. They recognize one another there and are provided with the fruits of Paradise.”’

“He said, ‘Some people said: “The souls of martyrs are inside green birds that take shelter in lamps in Paradise suspended from the Throne.”’”

Muslim also narrated in his Sahīh from Masrūq, who said:

“We asked ‘Abd Allah—meaning Ibn

---

## Page 1547

Mas‘ūd—about this verse:

‘Never think of those who have been killed in the cause of Allah as dead. Rather, they are alive with their Lord, receiving provision.’

He replied, ‘We asked the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, about this, and he said:

“Their souls are inside green birds that have lamps suspended from the Throne. They roam freely in Paradise wherever they wish, then return to those lamps. Your Lord looked upon them and said, ‘Do you desire anything?’ They replied, ‘What could we desire when we roam freely in Paradise wherever we wish?’ He asked them this three times. When they realized that they would not be left without being asked, they said, ‘Our Lord, we want You to return our souls to our bodies so that we may be killed in Your cause once again.’ When He saw that they had no need, they were left alone.”’”

Allah Most High has also said:

“O reassured soul,”
“Return to your Lord, well-pleased and pleasing to Him,”
“Enter among My servants,”
“And enter My Paradise.”

He thus addresses it, commanding it to return to its Lord, to enter among His servants, and to enter His Paradise. This explicitly states that it is subject to His lordship. The soul (nafs) here is the spirit (rūh) that is taken away; only its attributes vary. Thus, in the authentic hadith, when the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and his companions slept through the dawn prayer while travelling, he said:

“Allah took our spirits wherever He willed and returned them wherever He willed.” In another version: “He took our souls wherever He willed.”

Allah Most High says:

“Allah takes the souls at the time of their death, and those that do not die during their sleep. Then He keeps those for which He has decreed death.”

It is the spirit that is taken away and received, as related in Sahīh Muslim from Umm Salamah, who said:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, entered upon Abū Salamah, whose eyes were fixed open. He closed them and then said, ‘When the spirit is taken, the gaze follows it.’ Some of his family cried out, so he said:

---

## Page 1548

‘Do not pray for anything but good for yourselves, for the angels say “Amen” to what you say.’ Then he said:

‘O Allah, forgive Abū Salamah, raise his rank among the rightly guided, and take care of the descendants he leaves behind. Forgive us and him, O Lord of the worlds; make his grave spacious for him and fill it with light.’”

Muslim also narrated from Abū Hurayrah that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Have you not seen how a person’s eyes become fixed when he dies?” They replied, “Yes.” He said, “That is what happens when his gaze follows his soul.”

Thus, he called it a spirit (rūh) on one occasion and a soul (nafs) on another. Ahmad ibn Hanbal and Ibn Mājah narrated from Shaddād ibn Aws that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“When you are present with your dead, close their eyes, for the gaze follows the spirit. Say only good things, for ‘Amen’ is said to whatever the deceased’s family says.”

The evidence for this principle, the explanation of what the terms “spirit” and “soul” denote, and the different senses in which they are used are too extensive for this response. We have discussed them at length elsewhere.

What we have mentioned therefore makes it clear that anyone who says that the spirits of the children of Adam are eternal and uncreated is among the gravest innovators who advocate divine indwelling. Their position leads to negation of the Creator by identifying the servant with the Lord, and to other false and misleading innovations.

As for the words of Allah Most High:

“Say, ‘The Spirit is of the command of my Lord,’”

it has been said that the spirit here is not the human spirit, but rather an angel mentioned in His words:

“On the Day when the Spirit and the angels stand in a row”

---

## Page 1549

and His words:

“The angels and the Spirit ascend to Him,”

and His words:

“The angels and the Spirit descend therein by permission of their Lord.”

It has also been said that it is, rather, the human spirit. Both views are well known. Whether the verse encompasses both or refers to only one of them, it contains nothing indicating that the spirit is uncreated, for two reasons.

The first is that, in the Quran, “command” sometimes denotes the act of commanding and sometimes denotes its object—that which is commanded—as in His words, Most High:

“The command of Allah has come, so do not seek to hasten it,”

and His words:

“And Allah’s command is a decree determined.”

This usage also occurs with words other than “command,” such as “creation,” “power,” “mercy,” “word,” and others. Even if it were said that the spirit is some of Allah’s command, or a part of Allah’s command, or something similar explicitly identifying it as part of Allah’s command, “command” could only mean that which is commanded, not the act of commanding. For the spirit is a self-subsisting entity: it comes and goes, experiences bliss, and suffers punishment. Such an entity cannot conceivably be what is denoted by the verbal noun in *amara, ya’muru, amran* (“he commanded, he commands, commanding”). This is the position of the early generations of the Muslim community, its leading scholars, and its majority. Even a speculative theologian who says that the spirit is an accident inhering in the body does not regard it as the act denoted by the verbal noun in *amara, ya’muru, amran* (“he commanded, he commands, commanding”).

When the Quran is called Allah’s command, the Quran is the speech of “Allah.” The word *kalām* (“speech”) is a verbal noun of *kallama, yukallimu* (“he spoke, he speaks”), with the verbal nouns *taklīm* and *kalām* (“speaking”), and of *takallama* (“he spoke”), with the verbal nouns *takallum* and *kalām* (“speaking”). Thus, calling it a command in the sense of the verbal noun is appropriate, especially since speech is of two kinds: commands and statements.

---

## Page 1550

As for self-subsisting entities, they are not called “a command,” not in the sense of the object—that which is commanded—just as the Messiah was called a word because he was brought into being by the word, and just as that which is brought about by power is called “power,” Paradise is called “mercy,” and rain is called “mercy,” as in His words:

“So look at the effects of Allah’s mercy—how He gives life to the earth after its death.”

And in the statement of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, reporting from his Lord that He said to Paradise:

“You are My mercy; through you I show mercy to whomever I will.”

And his statement:

“When Allah created mercy, He created it as a hundred mercies.”

There are many similar examples. This was the answer given by Abū Saʿīd al-Kharrāz. He said: If someone objects that the Most High has said:

“Say, ‘The spirit is of my Lord’s command,’”

and that His command is from Him, the reply is that His command, Most High, means that which is commanded, brought into being by the creative act of the One who brings it into being.

Similarly, Ibn Qutayba discussed the categories of the spirit in (Kitāb al-Mushkil—The Book of Difficult Passages), saying: It is the spirit of bodies, which Allah takes at death; and “the Spirit” is Gabriel. The Most High said:

“The Trustworthy Spirit brought it down.”

And He said:

“And We supported him with the Holy Spirit,”

meaning Gabriel. According to what the Quran commentators have mentioned, the Spirit is also a mighty angel among the angels of Allah Most High, who stands alone, forming a row, while the angels stand in another row. The Most High said:

“They ask you about the spirit. Say, ‘The spirit is of my Lord’s command.’”

He said: The spirit is attributed to Allah because it comes into being by His command, or because it comes into being by His word.

The second reason is that the word (min), meaning “of” or “from,” may indicate a kind or substance in Arabic, as when one says, “a door of iron.” It may also indicate a point of departure, as when one says, “I departed from Mecca.” Thus, His words, Most High:

“Say, ‘The spirit is of my Lord’s command,’”

do not explicitly state that the spirit is part of the command and is of

---

## Page 1551

the same kind as it. Rather, “from” may indicate a point of origin, since the spirit was brought into being by the command and proceeded from it. This is the meaning of Imam Ahmad’s answer concerning His words, “and a spirit from Him,” where he said:

“‘And a spirit from Him’ means that the spirit came from Him by His command, as in His words: ‘And He has subjected to you whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on the earth—all from Him.’”

Another parallel is His saying:

“Whatever blessing you have is from Allah.”

Since the things subjected to us and the blessings are from Allah, yet are not part of His essence but originate from Him, it does not follow that His description of the Messiah as “a spirit from Him” means that the spirit is part of Allah’s essence.

It is well known that His words, “and a spirit from Him,” are stronger than His words, “The spirit is of my Lord’s command.” If “and a spirit from Him” does not preclude the spirit’s being created, nor entail its being part of Him, then “The spirit is of my Lord’s command” is still less grounds for precluding its being created or requiring that it be part of Him—or even part of His command.

This line of reasoning applies if “the command” means the command that is one of Allah’s attributes. Each of these two answers stands independently, and they can also be combined into a single answer, as follows:

In His words, “The spirit is of my Lord’s command,” “command” means either that which is commanded or an attribute of Allah Most High. If the former is intended, the spirit may be part of that which is commanded, and would therefore be created. If “command” means an attribute of (Allah), then His words, “The spirit is of my Lord’s command,” are like His words, “and a spirit from Him,” and “all from Him,” and similar expressions. The misconception arose only because someone supposed that the command is an eternal attribute of Allah and that the spirit of

---

## Page 1552

the children of Adam is part of that attribute. Yet the verse establishes neither of these two premises. And Allah, glorified be He, knows best.

The word “spirit” may also occur in the Quran with another meaning, as in His words:

“And thus We have revealed to you a spirit by Our command.”

And His words:

“He has inscribed faith in their hearts and strengthened them with a spirit from Him.”

And other similar passages. The Quran that Allah sent down is His speech, but discussion of this falls outside the question.

As for the questioner’s words, “Does what is left to Allah concern the spirit’s essence, its attributes, or both?”—this is not peculiar to discussion of the spirit. Rather, no one may pursue that of which they have no knowledge, or say about Allah what they do not know.

Allah Most High says:

“Do not pursue that of which you have no knowledge. Indeed, the hearing, the sight, and the heart—all of these will be questioned.”

And Allah Most High says:

“Say: My Lord has only forbidden shameful deeds, whether open or hidden, sin, unjust aggression, associating with Allah that for which He has sent down no authority, and saying about Allah what you do not know.”

And Allah Most High says:

“Was not the covenant of the Scripture taken from them, that they would say nothing about Allah except the truth?”

When He said to the angels:

“Tell Me the names of these, if you are truthful,”

they said:

“Glory be to You! We have no knowledge except what You have taught us. Indeed, You are the All-Knowing, the All-Wise.”

Moses said to al-Khidr:

“May I follow you so that you teach me some of the sound guidance you have been taught?”

And when the sparrow dipped its beak into the sea, al-Khidr said to Moses: “Compared with Allah’s knowledge, my knowledge and yours are no more than what this sparrow has taken from this sea.”

---

## Page 1553

There is nothing in the Quran or Sunnah to indicate that Muslims have been forbidden to discuss the spirit on the basis of what the Quran and Sunnah establish, whether concerning its essence or its attributes. Speaking without knowledge, however, is forbidden in every matter.

Yet it is established in the two Sahīh collections, on the authority of Ibn Mas‘ūd, that:

“The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, was in one of the lanes of Medina. Some of them said, ‘Ask him about the spirit.’ Others said, ‘Do not ask him, lest he tell you something you dislike.’ He said: They asked him while he was leaning on a palm stalk, and Allah revealed this verse.”

He thereby made clear that the Lord’s dominion is immense, and that His hosts, the nature of all this, and His power are too great for human beings to comprehend fully. They have been given only a little knowledge. Thus, no one who claims knowledge should imagine that he can know everything he is asked about, or everything that exists. For none knows the hosts of your Lord except Him.

---

## Page 1554

The shaykh—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked about someone who says:

“Unless the true essence of the jinn and the full reality of their attributes become clear to me, I will not follow the scholars in anything.”

He replied:

As for his not understanding the nature and essence of the jinn, this is merely an admission of his lack of knowledge; he has not denied their existence. Their existence is established in many ways besides the evidence of the Quran and Sunnah. Some people have seen them; others have seen people who have seen them, and their existence has been established for them through reports and certain knowledge.

Some people have spoken to them, and they have spoken in return. Others command them, forbid them, and exercise control over them. This occurs among both the righteous and those who are not righteous. Were I to recount what my companions and I have experienced with them, the discussion would be lengthy. The same is true of what others have experienced.

However, scholarly answers rest on knowledge that people share, not on knowledge possessed exclusively by the person giving the answer—unless the answer is addressed to someone who believes what he reports.

---

## Page 1555

The shaykh—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked about believing jinn:

Are they required to observe the “practical ordinances of Islam,” such as fasting, prayer, and other acts of worship? Or are they required only to affirm the truth, and nothing more?

He replied:

There is no doubt that they are commanded to perform deeds beyond affirming the truth, and forbidden to perform deeds other than denying it. They are therefore required to observe both the fundamental principles and the practical ordinances, in a manner appropriate to their nature. Since they are not identical to humans in their defining characteristics and essential nature, the precise requirements of their commands and prohibitions are not identical to those imposed on humans. Nevertheless, they share with humans the same general obligation to observe commands and prohibitions, and to abide by what is lawful and unlawful. I know of no disagreement among Muslims concerning this.

Likewise, Muslims have not disputed that those among the jinn who are guilty of disbelief, wickedness, and disobedience deserve punishment in the Fire, just as humans enter it. They have, however, differed concerning the believers among them. The majority of the followers of Mālik, al-Shāfiʿī, Ahmad, Abū Yūsuf, and Muhammad hold that they will enter Paradise. It is also reported in a hadith narrated by al-Tabarānī:

“They will be on the outskirts of Paradise. Humans will see them from where they cannot see the humans.”

---

## Page 1556

A group, including Abū Hanīfah—as reported from him—held that the obedient among the jinn will become dust like animals, and that their reward will be deliverance from the Fire.

Are there messengers among them, or only warners? There are two views.

One view holds that there are messengers among them, based on Allah’s words:

“O company of jinn and humans, did messengers from among you not come to you?”

The other holds that the messengers are human, while the jinn have warners. This is the better-known view, for Allah tells us that they follow the religion of Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—and that they:

“Returned to their people as warners.”

“They said, ‘O our people, we have heard a Book revealed after Moses’”—and the rest of the verse.

Those who hold this view say that His words, “Did messengers from among you not come to you?” are like His words, “From both emerge pearls and coral,” although these emerge only from salt water; and like His words, “He made the moon a light within them and made the sun a lamp,” although the moon is in only one of them.

As for their obligation to observe commands and prohibitions, and what is lawful and unlawful, the evidence is abundant. One example is the report in Muslim from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Masʿūd, from the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“‘An emissary from the jinn came to me, so I went with him and recited the Quran to them.’ They then set out, and he showed us their traces and the traces of their fires. They asked him for provisions, and he said, ‘You may have every bone over which Allah’s name has been mentioned: when it comes into your hands, it will be in its fullest state. Every dropping is fodder for your animals.’ The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—then said, ‘Do not cleanse yourselves after relieving yourselves with bones or dung.’”

This was to prevent their food and fodder from being spoiled. Here he makes clear that he permitted them only that over which Allah’s name had been mentioned, not that over which His name had not been mentioned.

---

## Page 1557

And Allah Most High says:

“And when Satan made their deeds seem fair to them”

through to His words:

“Indeed, I fear Allah, and Allah is severe in punishment.”

Thus, He tells us that Satan fears Allah. Punishment is imposed only for neglecting a command or committing something forbidden; the command at issue here is not assent to the truth.

Furthermore, the disobedience of Iblīs, the father of the jinn, did not consist in denying the truth. Allah commanded him to prostrate, and he knew that Allah had commanded him. There was no messenger between him and Allah whom he could accuse of falsehood. When he refused to prostrate before Adam, Allah punished him severely. Hence the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“When the son of Adam prostrates, Satan withdraws, weeping”—and the rest of the hadith.

Allah Most High also says in the account of Solomon:

“And for Solomon We subjected the wind, whose morning journey was a month and whose evening journey was a month”

through to His words:

“the punishment of the blazing Fire.”

This includes His command that they obey Solomon. Allah Most High says of Iblīs that he disobeyed; He does not say that he denied the truth. He also says concerning the jinn:

“O our people, we have heard a Book revealed after Moses”

through to His words:

“And whoever does not respond to Allah’s caller cannot escape Him on earth”—and the rest of the verse.

They were therefore commanded to respond to Allah’s caller, who is the Messenger. Responding and answering the call mean obeying the commands and prohibitions. This is the worship for which jinn and humans were created, as Allah Most High says:

“I did not create jinn and humans except to worship Me.”

As for the claim that “worship” is the innate knowledge present within them, that this constitutes faith, and that it is found in jinn and humans alone: if this were so, there would be no unbeliever among jinn or humans. Yet Allah has declared the unbelief of Iblīs and others among the jinn and humans. Allah Most High says:

---

## Page 1558

“I will surely fill Hell with you and all those among them who follow you.”

He states that He will fill it with him and his followers. This shows that none will enter it except those who follow him. Thus, the unbelievers and sinful people who enter it are followers of Iblīs. It is well known that unbelievers are neither believers nor possessed of a knowledge of Allah that makes them believers.

Rather, the particle lām indicates the religious statement connected with His legislative will, as in His words, Most High:

“Allah intends ease for you and does not intend hardship for you.”

And His words:

“Allah intends to make things clear to you”—and the rest of the verse.

It may also indicate the outcome ordained in creation, as in His words:

“Whoever Allah wills to guide, He opens their heart to Islam”—and the rest of the verse.

This is like His words, Most High:

“They will continue to differ,”

“except those upon whom your Lord has mercy; and for that He created them.”

That is, He created some people for differing and others for mercy. He also says:

“We have certainly created for Hell many of the jinn and humans.”

Thus, the particle lām in His words, Most High,

“I did not create jinn and humans except to worship Me,”

even if it is the same lām as in this verse, signifies the agent’s will and purpose. Hence, in the Book of Allah, this will is divided into religious will and will governing creation. Likewise, in the Book of Allah Most High, words, commands, judgments, decrees, prohibitions, permissions, and other such expressions fall into these two categories.

Furthermore, Allah Most High says:

“O assembly of jinn and humans, did there not come to you messengers from among yourselves, relating My verses to you and warning you of the meeting of this Day of yours?”

through to His words:

“And they testified against themselves that they

---

## Page 1559

were unbelievers.”

He thus makes clear that the messengers recited Allah’s verses to both communities, jinn and humans. Hence, when the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, recited a surah to the Companions, he said, “The jinn were indeed…”—and the rest of the hadith. He called them to obey Allah through its commands and prohibitions, not merely to discourse unaccompanied by obedience. Such assent was possessed by Iblīs, yet it availed him nothing against Allah.

The evidence establishing this principle is abundant, as are the hadiths and reports describing how the jinn perform Hajj, pray, engage in jihad, and are punished for sin. Allah Most High says, reporting their words:

“Some of us are righteous, and some are otherwise; we have followed divergent paths.”

They explained this as meaning different religious persuasions: Muslims, Jews, Christians, Shiites, and Sunnis.

He thus states that some of them are righteous and others fall short of righteousness. One would therefore either be obedient in that respect and thus a believer, or disobedient in that respect and thus an unbeliever; believers would not be divided into righteous and unrighteous. Someone who is unrighteous is not regarded as righteous, owing to his neglect of acts of obedience. The righteous person is one who fulfils what is obligatory upon him. Someone who falls short of righteousness must disobey some of what he has been commanded to do, yet belongs to a category distinct from unbelievers, for an unbeliever is not described in such terms. This shows that some of them neglect certain obligations. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1560

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked about the hadith of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“The drop of fluid remains a drop for forty days, then a clinging mass for forty days, then a lump of flesh for forty days; then it is fashioned, its features delineated, and its shape formed.”

Yet it is reported from Hudhayfa ibn Asīd:

“When forty-two nights have passed over the drop of fluid, Allah Most High sends an angel to it, who fashions it and creates its hearing, sight, skin, flesh, and bones. Then he says, ‘My Lord, male or female? Wretched or blessed? What will its provision be, and what will its lifespan be?’”

The questioner cited the remainder of the hadith, then asked: How can these two hadiths be reconciled?

He replied:

Praise be to Allah, Lord of all worlds.

The first hadith is recorded in the two Sahīh collections, from ‘Abd Allah ibn Mas‘ūd, who said:

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace—the truthful one whose words are confirmed as true—told us:

“The creation of each of you is brought together in his mother’s womb for forty days as a drop of fluid. Then he becomes a clinging mass for a similar period, then a lump of flesh for a similar period. Then the angel is sent to him and breathes the soul into him. He is commanded to record four things: his provision, his lifespan, his deeds, and whether he will be wretched or blessed. By Him besides whom there is no god, one of you may perform the deeds of the people of Paradise until there is only a cubit between him and it

---

## Page 1561

but what has been written for him overtakes him, so he performs the deeds of the people of the Fire and enters it. And one of you may perform the deeds of the people of the Fire until there is only a cubit between him and it, but what has been written for him overtakes him, so he performs the deeds of the people of Paradise and enters it.”

Through another chain of transmission, and in another version:

“Then Allah sends an angel, who is commanded to record four things. He is told, ‘Write down his deeds, his lifespan, his provision, and whether he will be wretched or blessed.’ Then the soul is breathed into him.”

This authentic hadith does not mention when the body is fashioned. It does, however, state that the angel records his provision, his lifespan, his deeds, and whether he will be wretched or blessed before the soul is breathed into him and after he has become a lump of flesh.

The hadith of Anas ibn Mālik recorded in the Sahīh agrees with this. It is traced back to the Prophet, who said:

“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has appointed an angel over the womb. The angel says, ‘My Lord, a drop of fluid! My Lord, a clinging mass! My Lord, a lump of flesh!’ When Allah wishes to complete its creation, the angel says, ‘My Lord, male or female? Wretched or blessed? What will its provision be? What will its lifespan be?’ These things are thus recorded while it is in its mother’s womb.”

This makes clear that the recording takes place after it has become a lump of flesh. As for the hadith of Hudhayfa ibn Asīd, it is recorded by Muslim but not al-Bukhārī. Its wording is:

“I heard the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, say:

‘When forty-two nights have passed over the drop of fluid, Allah sends an angel to it, who fashions it and creates its hearing, sight, skin, flesh, and bones. Then he says, “My Lord, male or female?” Your Lord decrees whatever He wills, and the angel records it. Then he says, “My Lord, what will his provision be?” Your Lord decrees whatever He wills, and the angel records it. Then he says, “My Lord, what will his lifespan be?” Then He decrees

---

## Page 1562

whatever your Lord wills, and the angel records it. Then the angel departs with the record in his hand, neither adding to nor omitting anything he was commanded to record.’”

This hadith states that the drop of fluid is fashioned after forty-two nights, and that, after it has been fashioned and its hearing, sight, skin, flesh, and bones have been created, the angel says, “My Lord, male or female?” It is known that it does not become flesh and bone until it has become a lump of flesh.

This therefore agrees with that hadith in placing the angel’s recording after this stage—unless it is argued that what is meant is the predetermination of the flesh and bones. This hadith has also been transmitted in wordings that are not fully explicit, some clearer than others. Among these is what Muslim also narrated from Hudhayfa:

“I heard Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, say:

‘The drop of fluid remains in the womb for forty nights. Then the angel who fashions it descends to it and says, “My Lord, male or female?” Allah makes him male or female. Then he says, “My Lord, well-formed or not?” Allah Most High makes him well-formed or otherwise. Then he says, “My Lord, what will his lifespan and physical form be?” Then Allah makes him wretched or blessed.’”

This makes clear that his provision, his lifespan, and whether he will be wretched or blessed are recorded after He has made him male or female and well-formed or otherwise.

Another wording recorded by Muslim states:

“The angel comes to the drop of fluid forty or forty-five nights after it has settled in the womb. He says, ‘My Lord, wretched or blessed?’ Then he records it. ‘My Lord, male or female?’ Then he records his provision, and records his deeds, the traces he leaves, and his lifespan;

---

## Page 1563

then the records are folded up, and nothing is added to them or taken away.”

This wording places the recording of whether a person will be blessed or wretched first, but suggests that this is recorded once the forty nights have elapsed. However, its narrators did not preserve this wording as accurately as the other versions. Hence the uncertainty: was it after forty nights or forty-five? Other narrators mention only forty or forty-two, which is correct: whoever mentioned forty-two included both endpoints of the period, whereas whoever said forty omitted them. This is common when specifying periods of time. Thus, in this wording, what comes later was placed first, and what comes first was placed later.

Alternatively, it may be said that he did not mention this using the conjunction thumma (“then”), so the wording does not necessarily imply a sequence. He merely intended to say that these things occur after the forty.

In that case, one of two explanations must apply. Either these things occur immediately after the forty and then again after the one hundred and twenty—and there is nothing objectionable about their being recorded twice—with the first record including whether the child is male or female;

or it may be said that the wordings of this hadith were not preserved with complete accuracy. This is why its narrators differed over its wording, and why al-Bukhārī did not include it in his collection. A hadith may be authentic in its substance while some of its wordings contain inconsistencies. In that case, those wordings cannot properly be used to contradict what is established in the agreed-upon authentic hadith, whose wording does not vary and which is, moreover, corroborated by other authentic hadiths.

The answer may therefore be summarized as follows: whatever conflicts with the agreed-upon hadith either agrees with it in reality, or is

---

## Page 1564

not accurately preserved, in which case there is no contradiction. There is no doubt that its wordings were not recorded precisely, as shown by the differences already mentioned. This is acknowledged by the wording that places the giving of form before the determination of lifespan, deeds, wretchedness and blessedness. At most, this wording implies that the embryo may take shape during the second forty-day period, before entering the third. This does not contradict the authentic hadith, nor do we know it to be false.

Indeed, women have reported that the embryo takes shape after forty days, and that the male takes shape before the female. This is to be given precedence over the statement of those jurists who say that the embryo cannot take shape in fewer than eighty-one days. They based this solely on the assumption that it takes shape only once it has become a lump of flesh, and that it does not become a lump of flesh until after eighty days. Yet it may take shape before that, as some women have reported; even the clot itself may be given form.

Allah knows best. May Allah send blessings and peace upon Muhammad, his family and his Companions.

---

## Page 1565

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—said, refuting the claim:

“Every newborn is born in the state that Allah already knew he would ultimately attain.”

It is well known that this applies to all created beings. All animals are born according to Allah’s prior knowledge concerning them. On that interpretation, every created being would therefore be created upon the fiṭrah (innate disposition).

Moreover, if that were the intended meaning, his statement, “His parents then make him a Jew, a Christian or a Magian,” would have no meaning. The parents would merely have brought about the very innate disposition upon which he was born, so there would be no distinction between making him a Jew and making him a Christian.

He then said:

The comparison made by the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, with an animal born intact and subsequently mutilated shows that the child’s parents changed the state in which he was born.

He then said:

As for your claim that they were created devoid of recognition and denial, without their innate disposition inclining them towards either—that the heart is instead like a tablet upon which faith or disbelief may be inscribed, being no more receptive to one than to the other—this is a profoundly unsound view.

---

## Page 1566

On that view, then, the innate disposition would make no distinction between recognition and denial, being made a Jew or a Christian, and Islam; these differences would depend solely on their causes. It should therefore have been said: “His parents then make him a Muslim, a Jew or a Christian.” Yet he stated that the parents make their child a disbeliever, mentioning the false religions but not Islam. It is thus clear that Islam differs from disbelief with regard to the presence of a specific cause.

He then said:

In short, whatever is equally receptive to what merits praise and what merits blame deserves neither praise nor blame. Yet Allah Most High says:

“So set your face towards the religion, inclining to truth—the innate disposition upon which Allah created mankind.”

Furthermore, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, compared this innate disposition to an animal born physically intact, and the disbelief that subsequently affects it to cutting off its nose. Clearly, its wholeness is praiseworthy and its mutilation blameworthy. How, then, could it have been neither praiseworthy nor blameworthy before being mutilated? And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1567

He was asked:

What did the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, mean when he said, “Every child is born upon the innate disposition”? Did he mean one’s natural constitution, or the innate disposition of Islam?

And regarding his statement, “The wretched is the one who is wretched in his mother’s womb,” and the remainder of the hadith: is this specific or general in its application? As for domestic and wild animals, will Allah bring them back to life on the Day of Resurrection or not?

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. Regarding the Prophet’s statement, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Every child is born upon the innate disposition; then his parents make him a Jew, a Christian or a Magian.”

The correct view is that this is the innate disposition upon which Allah created mankind: the innate disposition of Islam. It is the disposition upon which He created them on the day when He said:

“‘Am I not your Lord?’ They said, ‘Yes.’”

It consists in freedom from false beliefs and receptiveness to sound beliefs. The essence of “Islam” is to submit to Allah, and to none other. This is the meaning of “There is no god but Allah.” The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, illustrated this by saying:

“Just as an animal gives birth to an animal that is physically intact: do you see any mutilation in it?”

He explained that the heart’s freedom from defect is like the body’s soundness, and that the defect arises subsequently.

In Sahīh Muslim, it is narrated from ‘Iyād ibn Himār that he said: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, relating from Allah:

“I created My servants upright in faith, but the devils led them astray and declared unlawful

---

## Page 1568

for them what I had made lawful for them, and commanded them to associate with Me that for which I had sent down no authority.”

For this reason, according to the well-known view reported from him, Imam Ahmad, may Allah be pleased with him, held that whenever either of a child’s two unbelieving parents dies, the child is legally deemed a Muslim, since the cause of departure from the original innate disposition has ceased to exist.

It has also been related from him, from Ibn al-Mubārak, and from both of them that they said:

“He is born according to the wretchedness or blessedness upon which he was created.”

This statement does not contradict the first view. A child is born free from defect, although Allah already knows that he will disbelieve. He must therefore ultimately reach the outcome already recorded for him in the Mother of the Book, just as an animal is born physically intact, although Allah already knows that it will be mutilated.

This is the meaning of the report in Sahīh Muslim from Ibn ‘Abbās, may Allah be pleased with them both, who said:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said concerning the boy whom al-Khidr killed: ‘He was inscribed as a disbeliever when his fate was inscribed; had he been left alive, he would have overwhelmed his parents with transgression and disbelief.’”

That is, Allah inscribed him in the Mother of the Book: He wrote and recorded him as a disbeliever, meaning that if he lived, he would actually disbelieve. For this reason:

“When the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was asked about the children of polytheists who die while still young, he said: ‘Allah knows best what they would have done.’”

That is, Allah knows which of them would believe and which would disbelieve if they reached maturity.

Furthermore, a hadith with a chain of transmission approaching soundness has been narrated from Abū Hurayra, may Allah be pleased with him, from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

“On the Day of Resurrection, Allah will test them and send them a messenger on the plain of Resurrection. Whoever responds to him, He will admit into Paradise; whoever disobeys him, He will admit into the Fire.”

At that point, what Allah, glorified be He, knew about them will become manifest. He will recompense them for what has become manifest of His knowledge—their belief or disbelief—not merely on the basis of His knowledge.

---

## Page 1569

This is the best view expressed concerning the children of polytheists, and all the hadiths can be understood in accordance with it.

The innate disposition stands in relation to the truth as eyesight does to the sun: anyone with eyes would see the sun if no veil obstructed his vision. The false beliefs acquired through adopting Judaism, Christianity, or Zoroastrianism are like a veil that prevents the eyes from seeing the sun. Likewise, anyone with sound senses enjoys sweetness, unless some corruption affects his constitution and distorts it, making what is sweet taste bitter in his mouth.

Their being born upon the innate disposition does not mean that they actually believe in Islam at birth, for Allah brought us forth from our mothers’ wombs knowing nothing. Rather, it means that the heart is sound, receptive to the truth—which is Islam—and desirous of it, such that, if a person were left without anything altering him, he would be nothing other than a Muslim. This capacity for knowledge and action, which inherently leads to Islam unless something prevents it, is the innate disposition upon which Allah created humankind.

As for the hadith mentioned, it is authentically reported that Ibn Mas‘ūd used to say:

“The wretched person is the one whose wretchedness was determined in his mother’s womb, and the blessed person is the one who takes warning from others.”

In the two Sahīh collections, ‘Abd Allāh ibn Mas‘ūd said: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace—who is truthful and whose truthfulness is confirmed—told us:

“The creation of each of you is brought together in his mother’s womb for forty days as a drop of fluid, then he becomes a blood clot for a similar period,

---

## Page 1570

then he becomes a lump of flesh for a similar period. Then the angel is sent to him and commanded to record four matters. He is told: ‘Write down his provision, his lifespan, his deeds, and whether he will be wretched or blessed.’ Then the spirit is breathed into him.”

This applies to every person who is born. Allah, glorified be He, knows—through His knowledge, which is an attribute of His—which of His servants are wretched and which are blessed. He has written this in the Preserved Tablet, and He commands the angel to record the condition of every child between the formation of his body and the breathing of the spirit into him. There are also other records that Allah writes, which are not discussed here. Whoever denies His eternal knowledge of this is an unbeliever.

As for animals, Allah, glorified be He, will gather them all, as the Quran and Sunnah indicate. The Most High says:

“There is no creature on earth, nor any bird flying with its two wings, but they are communities like you. We have neglected nothing in the Book. Then to their Lord they will be gathered.”

The Most High also says:

“And when the wild beasts are gathered.”

And the Most High says:

“Among His signs is the creation of the heavens and the earth, and the creatures He has dispersed throughout them. He has the power to gather them whenever He wills.”

The particle idhā (“when”) is used only for what will inevitably occur.

The hadiths concerning this are well known. On the Day of Resurrection, Allah, mighty and majestic, will gather the animals and exact retribution for some of them from others. Then He will say to them, “Become dust,” and they will become dust. At that moment, the unbeliever will say:

“If only I were dust!”

Whoever says that they will not be brought back to life commits a most grievous error; indeed, he is astray or an unbeliever. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1571

He also said—may Allah have mercy on him:

“Every child is born with the natural disposition.”

For Allah, glorified be He, has created hearts such that, among all they love and desire, none but Allah can give them rest and be their ultimate end. Whenever a person loves anything else, he finds within himself that his heart seeks something beyond it and loves something else to worship, to turn to, and in which to find rest; and he sees other things of its kind that resemble it.

For this reason, He said:

“Surely, in the remembrance of Allah do hearts find rest.”

---

## Page 1572

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah sanctify his soul—said:

Section

Allah mentions in several places in His Book the guardian angels entrusted with the children of Adam, who protect them and record their deeds. He, Most High, says:

“It is He who takes your souls by night and knows what you have done by day, then awakens you therein so that an appointed term may be fulfilled. Then to Him is your return.”

“He is the Subduer above His servants, and He sends guardians over you until, when death comes to one of you, Our messengers take his soul, and they never neglect their duty.”

He, Most High, also says:

“It is the same whether any of you conceals his speech or declares it openly, whether he hides by night or goes about openly by day.”

“For each person there are angels in succession, before him and behind him, guarding him by Allah’s command.”

He, Most High, also says:

“No! Rather, you deny the Judgment.”

“Yet over you are watchers,”

“noble recorders,”

“who know whatever you do.”

He, Most High, also says:

“By the heaven and the night visitor!”

“And what will make you know what the night visitor is?”

“It is the piercing star.”

“There is no soul without a guardian over it.”

He, Most High, also says:

“We certainly created man and know what his soul whispers to him, and We are nearer to him than his jugular vein.”

“As the two recording angels take down his deeds, one seated on the right and the other on the left.”

“He utters no word without a vigilant observer ready beside him.”

And He, Most High, says:

---

## Page 1573

“We have bound every person’s deeds to his neck, and on the Day of Resurrection We will bring forth for him a record which he will find spread open. ‘Read your record. You yourself are sufficient this day to reckon your own account.’”

He, Most High, also says:

“You will see every nation kneeling. Every nation will be summoned to its record: ‘Today you will be recompensed for what you used to do.’”

“This record of Ours speaks truthfully against you. Indeed, We had all that you did recorded.”

He, Most High, also says:

“They will say, ‘Woe to us! What is this record that leaves nothing, small or great, without counting it?’ They will find all that they did before them, and your Lord wrongs no one.”

He, Most High, also says:

“Everything they did is in the records.”

“Every small and great thing is written down.”

And He, Most High, says … (1).

---

## Page 1574

Shaykh al-Islām was asked:

Are the angels assigned to a servant always the same, or does Allah send down two different angels to him each day? Are some angels assigned to the servant by night and others by day? And what is the meaning of His saying, Mighty and Majestic:

“He is the Subduer above His servants, and He sends guardians over you until, when death comes to one of you, Our messengers take his soul, and they never neglect their duty.”

He replied:

All praise is due to Allah. There are different categories of angels. Some are assigned to the servant permanently. Others alternate by night and day and gather at the dawn and afternoon prayers. He asks them—though He knows best about them—“How did you leave My servants?” They reply, “We came to them while they were praying, and we left them while they were praying.” There are also angels, additional to those who record people’s deeds, who seek out gatherings where Allah is remembered.

(The deeds of the servants are collected both collectively and in detail. The deeds of the night are raised before those of the day, and the deeds of the day before those of the night. Deeds are presented to Allah every Monday and Thursday. All of this is reported in authentic hadiths. As for the claim that the two angels assigned to a person are replaced each day, no report concerning this has reached us. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1575

He was asked about the saying of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“When a servant intends to do a good deed but does not do it, it is recorded for him as one good deed”—and the rest of the hadith.

If this intention is a secret between the servant and his Lord, how do the angels become aware of it?

He replied:

All praise is due to Allah. It is reported that Sufyān ibn ‘Uyaynah answered this question by saying:

“When he intends to do a good deed, the angel smells a pleasant fragrance; and when he intends to do an evil deed, the angel smells a foul odour.”

The truth of the matter is that Allah is able to inform the angels of what is within the servant’s soul in whatever way He wills, just as He is able to enable some human beings to perceive what is within another person. If Allah may grant some people insight through which they sometimes know what is in a person’s heart, then the angel assigned to the servant is all the more deserving of being informed of this by Allah.

Regarding His words, Exalted is He:

“We are closer to him than his jugular vein,”

it has been said that what is meant is the angels. Allah has caused the angels to place thoughts within the servant’s soul, as ‘Abdullāh ibn Mas‘ūd said: “There is a prompting from the angel and a prompting from Satan. The angel’s prompting is an affirmation of the truth and a promise

---

## Page 1576

of good, whereas Satan’s prompting is a denial of the truth and a threat of evil.”

It is also authentically reported from him in the Sahīh that he said:

“Each of you has been assigned a companion from among the angels and a companion from among the jinn.” They asked, “Even you, O Messenger of Allah?” He replied, “I too, but Allah has helped me against him, so he commands me only to do good.”

Thus, if the evil deed that a servant intends comes from Satan’s prompting, Satan knows of it. Likewise, if the good deed that a servant intends comes from an angel’s prompting, then that angel knows of it—all the more so. If this angel knows of it, it is possible for the angels who keep watch over the deeds of the children of Adam to know of it as well.

---

## Page 1577

He was asked:

Concerning the presentation of religions at the time of death: does this have any basis in the Quran and Sunnah? What is meant by the trial in the Prophet’s words (peace and blessings be upon him), “You will surely be tried in your graves”? If a servant apostatizes—Allah’s protection is sought—will he be rewarded for the righteous deeds he performed before his apostasy, or not? Give us a ruling, and may you be rewarded.

He replied:

All praise is due to Allah, Lord of all worlds. As for religions being presented to a servant at the time of death, this neither happens to everyone nor is it absent from everyone’s experience. Rather, some people are presented with religions before their death, while others are not. This has indeed happened to some people.

All of this falls within the trials of life and death from which we have been commanded to seek refuge in our prayers. These include what is mentioned in the authentic hadith:

“The Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) commanded us to seek refuge in our prayers from four things: the punishment of Hell, the punishment of the grave, the trials of life and death, and the trial of the False Messiah.”

Yet it is at the time of death that Satan is most eager to lead the children of Adam astray, for it is a time of need.

---

## Page 1578

The Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) said in an authentic hadith:

“Deeds are judged by how they end.”

He also said (peace and blessings be upon him):

“A servant may perform the deeds of the people of Paradise until only a cubit separates him from it; then what has been decreed overtakes him, and he performs the deeds of the people of Hell and enters it. And a servant may perform the deeds of the people of Hell until only a cubit separates him from it; then what has been decreed overtakes him, and he performs the deeds of the people of Paradise and enters it.”

For this reason, it has been reported:

“Satan is most relentless against the son of Adam at the time of death. He says to his helpers, ‘Seize this one! If he escapes you, you will never get hold of him again.’”

The account of Abd Allāh ibn Ahmad ibn Hanbal with his father, who was saying, “No, not yet. No, not yet,” is well known.

For this reason, it is said that this is feared for someone who has not performed Hajj, because Anas ibn Mālik (may Allah be pleased with him) narrated that the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) said:

“Whoever possesses provisions or a mount that can convey him to Allah’s Sacred House, yet does not perform Hajj, let him die as a Jew if he wishes, or as a Christian if he wishes.”

Allah Most High said:

“Pilgrimage to the House is a duty owed to Allah by all people who are able to make their way to it. And whoever disbelieves—Allah has no need of any of His creation.”

Ikrimah said: When this verse was revealed:

“Whoever seeks a religion other than Islam, it will never be accepted from him, and in the Hereafter he will be among the losers,”

the Jews and Christians said, “We are Muslims.” So Allah said to them:

“Pilgrimage to the House is a duty owed to Allah by all people.”

They said, “We will not make pilgrimage to it.” Then Allah Most High said:

“And whoever disbelieves—Allah has no need of any of His creation.”

---

## Page 1579

As for the trial in the graves, it is the examination and testing of the deceased when the two angels question him, asking:

“What did you used to say about this man who was sent among you—Muhammad?”

Allah makes those who believe steadfast with the firm word, so the believer says:

“Allah is my Lord, Islam is my religion, and Muhammad is my Prophet.”

He also says:

“He is Muhammad, the Messenger of Allah. He came to us with clear proofs and guidance, so we believed in him and followed him.”

The two angels then sternly rebuke him—this being the last of the trials to which the believer is subjected—and repeat their original question.

Hadiths concerning this trial have been mass-transmitted from the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him), through the reports of al-Barā’ ibn Āzib, Anas ibn Mālik, Abū Hurayrah, and others (may Allah be pleased with them). This trial applies to all those subject to religious obligations, except that scholars have differed concerning the prophets.
