They have likewise differed concerning those not subject to religious obligations, such as children and the insane.

One view is that they are not subjected to this trial, because such testing concerns only those subject to religious obligations. This is the view of al-Qādī and Ibn Aqīl. According to this view, they are not prompted with the answers after death.

Another view is that they are prompted and are also subjected to the trial. This is the view of Abū Hakīm and Abū al-Hasan ibn Abdūs, who also reported it from his fellow scholars. It accords with the view that they will be charged with obligations on the Day of Resurrection, which is the position of most scholars and of most of Ahl al-Sunnah among hadith scholars and theologians. This is the position that Abū al-Hasan al-Asharī (may Allah be pleased with him) reported from Ahl al-Sunnah and adopted himself, and it is what Imam Ahmad’s explicit statements imply.

As for “apostasy from Islam,” whereby a man becomes a disbeliever—either a polytheist or one of the People of the Book—

---

## Page 1580

if he dies in that state, his deeds are rendered void by scholarly consensus, as the Quran explicitly states in several places. For example, Allah says:

“Whoever among you turns back from his religion and dies as a disbeliever—their deeds are rendered void in this world and the Hereafter.”

And He says:

“Whoever rejects faith, his deeds are rendered void.”

And He says:

“Had they associated others with Allah, all that they used to do would have been rendered void.”

And He says:

“If you associate others with Allah, your deeds will surely be rendered void.”

However, scholars have differed concerning someone who apostatizes and then returns to Islam: are the deeds he performed before his apostasy rendered void, or are they rendered void only if he dies an apostate? There are two well-known views, both of which are held within the school of Imam Ahmad. The view that the deeds are rendered void is that of Abū Hanīfah and Mālik, while the view that their nullification remains in suspense is that of al-Shāfiʿī.

People have also differed concerning the apostate: should it be said that he had sound faith which his apostasy rendered void? Or should it instead be said that his apostasy revealed to us that his faith had been unsound, and that sound faith can never disappear? Different groups hold these two views.

On this rests the question concerning someone who qualifies his statement by saying, “I am a believer—if Allah wills”: does this qualification refer to the completeness of faith, or to the state in which one ultimately meets one’s end? Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1581

He was asked:

“Do all creatures—even the angels—die?”

He answered:

The view held by most people is that all creatures die (*), even the angels and even Azrael, the Angel of Death. A hadith concerning this has been reported and attributed to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Muslims, Jews, and Christians agree that this is possible and that Allah has the power to bring it about. The only dissenters are groups of would-be philosophers who follow “Aristotle” and others like them, together with those who have joined them while professing affiliation with Islam, Judaism, or Christianity. These include the authors of the “Epistles of the Brethren of Purity” and others like them, who claim that “the angels” are intellects and souls and that they cannot possibly die under any circumstances. Indeed, they regard them as gods and lords of this world.

The Quran and the other scriptures declare that the angels are servants subject to His rule, as He, glorified is He, says:

“The Messiah would never disdain to be a servant of Allah, nor would the angels brought near to Him. Whoever disdains His worship and acts arrogantly—He will gather them all to Himself.”

And He, exalted is He, says:

“They say, ‘The Most Merciful has taken offspring.’ Glory be to Him! Rather, they are honoured servants.”

“They do not precede Him in speech, and they act by His command.”

“He knows what lies before them and what lies behind them, and they intercede only for those with whom He is pleased.”

And He says: “And how many

> **Footnotes:**
> (*) Shaykh Nāsir ibn Hamad al-Fahd stated (p. 33):
> This fatwa appears again in (16/33–36), but the version there differs from the one here for two reasons:
> First: the question there includes details not mentioned here.
> Second: there are differences between the two versions, which I have noted in my discussion of volume sixteen.

---

## Page 1582

angels there are in the heavens whose intercession avails nothing except after Allah has given permission for whomever He wills and is pleased with.”

Allah, glorified is He, has the power to cause them to die and then bring them back to life, just as He has the power to cause humans and jinn to die and then bring them back to life.

He, glorified is He, has said:

“He is the One who originates creation, then repeats it, and that is easier for Him.”

It is established in authentic hadith, transmitted through several routes and from more than one Companion, that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“When Allah speaks revelation, something like fainting overtakes the angels.”

In another narration:

“When the angels hear His speech, they fall unconscious.”

And in another narration:

“The angels hear something like a chain being dragged over smooth rock, and they fall unconscious. Then, when fear is removed from their hearts—that is, when terror is lifted from their hearts—they say, ‘What did your Lord say?’ They reply, ‘The truth,’ and call out, ‘The truth! The truth!’”

In these authentic hadiths, he thus tells us that they fall unconscious in a swoon. If it is possible for them to fall unconscious, it is also possible for them to fall dead. These would-be philosophers, however, admit neither possibility.

Such a swoon is like that of Moses, peace be upon him. Allah, exalted is He, says:

“When his Lord manifested Himself to the mountain, He reduced it to dust, and Moses fell unconscious.”

The Quran speaks of three blasts of the Trumpet. The blast of terror is mentioned in Sūrat al-Naml (The Ants), in His words:

“On the Day the Trumpet is blown, all those in the heavens and all those on earth will be terrified, except those whom Allah wills.”

---

## Page 1583

And He mentions the blasts of death and resurrection in His words:

“The Trumpet will be blown, and all those in the heavens and all those on earth will fall dead, except those whom Allah wills. Then it will be blown again, and at once they will be standing, looking on.”

As for the exception, it includes the wide-eyed maidens in Paradise, for there is no death in Paradise, and it includes others as well. We cannot identify with certainty everyone whom Allah has excepted, for Allah has left this unspecified in His Book.

It is established in an authentic hadith that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“People will fall unconscious on the Day of Resurrection, and I will be the first to regain consciousness. I will find Moses holding on to a leg of the Throne, and I do not know whether he regained consciousness before me or was among those whom Allah excepted.”

Some have said that this swoon is a fourth occurrence, while others have said that it is one of those mentioned in the Quran.

In any case, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, withheld judgment concerning Moses: was he among those whom Allah excepted or not?

Since the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, did not tell us everyone whom Allah excepted, we cannot assert this with certainty. This is therefore like knowing the time of the Hour, the identities of the prophets, and other such matters about which he did not inform us. Such knowledge can only be obtained through transmitted reports.

And Allah knows best. May Allah bless Muhammad, his family, and his Companions, and grant them peace.

---

## Page 1584

Shaykh al-Islām Taqī al-Dīn Abū al-‘Abbās Ahmad ibn Taymiyyah—may Allah have mercy on him—said:

Section

The position of all Muslims—indeed, of all religious communities—is to affirm the “Greater Resurrection,” people’s rising from their graves, and reward and punishment then. They also affirm reward and punishment in the intermediate state between death and the Day of Resurrection. This is the unanimous position of the early Muslim generations and of the people of the Sunnah and the community. Only a few adherents of religious innovations have denied reward and punishment in the intermediate state.

Some scholastic theologians, however, say that this affects the body alone, as though, in their view, there were no soul that separates from the body. This is the position of those among the Mu‘tazilites and Ash‘arites who hold this view.

Others say that it affects the soul alone, on the premise that the body experiences neither punishment nor bliss in the intermediate state. This is the view held by Ibn Maysarah and Ibn Hazm.

---

## Page 1585

Still others maintain that the body, rather, experiences bliss and punishment without having any life in it. This is the view of a group of hadith scholars, and Ibn al-Zāghūnī inclines towards it in his work on the life of the prophets in their graves. This question has been discussed at length in several places.

The point here is that many scholastic theologians deny that the soul exists after death or receives reward or punishment. They claim that neither the Quran nor hadith provides evidence for this, just as those who deny punishment in the grave and the intermediate state altogether claim that the Quran provides no evidence for it. This is mistaken. Rather, the Quran explains in several places that the soul continues to exist after leaving the body, and it describes bliss and punishment in the intermediate state.

He—glorified and exalted be He—mentions both the “Greater Resurrection” and the “Lesser Resurrection” within a single surah, as in Sūrat al-Wāqiʿah. At its beginning, He mentions the Greater Resurrection and states that people will fall into three groups, saying:

“When the Inevitable Event occurs—
none can deny its occurrence—
bringing some low and raising others high;
when the earth is violently shaken,
and the mountains are utterly crushed,
becoming scattered dust,
and you are divided into three groups.”

Then, at its end, He mentions the Lesser Resurrection, which occurs at death, and states that people fall into three categories after death, saying:

“Why, then, when the soul reaches the throat,
and you are looking on,
while We are nearer to him than you, though you do not see—
why, if you are not subject to judgment,
do you not bring it back, if you are truthful?
Then, if he is one of those brought near,
there will be rest, bounty, and a Garden of Bliss.
And if he is one of the companions of the right,
then, ‘Peace be upon you,’ from the companions of the right.
But if he is one of those who denied the truth and went astray,

---

## Page 1586

then he will be welcomed with scalding water
and burned in Hellfire.”

This states that the soul reaches the throat and that they cannot bring it back. It also explains the condition, at that moment, of those brought near, the companions of the right, and those who denied the truth.

In Sūrat al-Qiyāmah, He likewise mentions both resurrections, saying:

“I swear by the Day of Resurrection.”

Then He says:

“And I swear by the self-reproaching soul.”

This is the human soul. It has been said that the soul may be self-reproaching or otherwise, but this is not so. Rather, every human soul is self-reproaching, for there is no human being who does not reproach himself and feel regret, whether in this world or in the Hereafter. This affirms the existence of the soul.

Then He mentions the resurrection of the body, saying:

“Does man think that We will not gather his bones?
Yes indeed, We are able even to restore his fingertips.
Yet man wishes to persist in sin in the time ahead of him.
He asks, ‘When is the Day of Resurrection?’”

He describes the circumstances of the Resurrection up to His words:

“expecting a crushing calamity to befall them.”

Then He mentions death, saying:

“No! When the soul reaches the collarbones.”

This affirms the existence of the soul and that it reaches the collarbones, just as He says in the other passage:

“it reaches the throat.”

The collarbones are connected to the throat. Then He says:

“And it is said, ‘Who can recite a healing invocation?’”

That is, someone to recite over the soul. It has also been interpreted as: “Who will ascend with it to Allah?” The first interpretation is more evident, because this occurs before death. For He says:

“And he realizes that it is the parting.”

This indicates that they hope for his recovery and seek someone to recite healing invocations over him. Moreover, the soul’s ascent does not require someone to be sought to ascend with it, for Allah has angels who do whatever they are commanded. Healing recitation is the greatest of remedies, for it is a remedy

---

## Page 1587

of a spiritual nature. Hence the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said when describing those who place their trust in Allah:

“They do not ask others to recite healing invocations over them.”

The point is that he fears death and hopes to remain alive through the efforts of someone who recites healing invocations. Hence Allah says:

“And he realizes that it is the parting.”

Then He says:

“And one leg is entwined with the other.
To your Lord that Day will be the driving.”

This indicates that there is a soul which exists and subsists in its own right and is driven to its Lord. An accident inhering in something else cannot be driven, nor is the dead person’s body driven. This, then, is an explicit affirmation of a soul that leaves the body and is driven to its Lord, as stated in the widely transmitted hadiths concerning the taking of the believer’s soul and the disbeliever’s soul.

After this, alongside the warning He has already given, He describes the disbeliever, saying:

“He neither believed nor prayed.”

This does not mean that every one of these souls is like that. Likewise, Sūrat Qāf discusses the warning of the Resurrection, yet He also says in it:

“And the agony of death has come with the truth. That is what you were trying to evade.”

Then He says:

“And the Trumpet will be blown. That is the Day of the Warning.”

Thus He mentions both resurrections: the minor and the major. His words, “And the agony of death has come with the truth,” mean that it has brought the reward and punishment that follow death—the truth proclaimed by the messengers. He does not mean that it has brought the truth which is death itself, for death is common knowledge and has never been disputed. No one has ever said, “Death is unreal,” such that one would need to say, “It has come with the truth.”

As for His words, “That is what you were trying to evade,” although a person dislikes death, he knows that its angels will meet him. This is like His saying:

“And worship your Lord until certainty comes to you.”

And “certainty” is

---

## Page 1588

what comes after death, as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“As for ‘Uthmān ibn Maz‘ūn, certainty has come to him from his Lord.”

Otherwise, death itself—considered apart from what follows it—is common knowledge and has never been disputed, so there would be no reason to call it “certainty.”

Allah has mentioned the punishment of the Resurrection and that of the intermediate state after death together in several places. He mentioned them in the account of Pharaoh’s people, saying:

“And a terrible punishment engulfed Pharaoh’s people: the Fire, to which they are exposed morning and evening. And on the Day the Hour is established: ‘Admit Pharaoh’s people to the severest punishment.’”

He said in the account of Noah’s people:

“Because of their sins they were drowned and then admitted into a Fire, and they found no helpers against Allah.”

This is alongside Noah’s warning to them about the Resurrection in his words:

“And Allah has caused you to grow from the earth. Then He will return you to it and bring you forth again.”

We have explained elsewhere, in several places, that the messengers before Muhammad warned of the major Resurrection, refuting those speculative philosophers who deny this. Allah said of the hypocrites:

“We will punish them twice; then they will be returned to a tremendous punishment.”

Several scholars have said that the first punishment is in this world and the second in the intermediate state after death; “then they will be returned to a tremendous punishment” in the Hereafter.

Allah, Most High, said in Sūrat al-An‘ām:

“If only you could see the wrongdoers in the throes of death, while the angels stretch out their hands, saying: ‘Give up your souls! Today you will be repaid with humiliating punishment for saying things about Allah that were not true and for being arrogant toward His signs.’”

“And you have come to Us alone, just as We created you the first time, leaving behind you all that We bestowed upon you.”

This describes the state at death. His words:

---

## Page 1589

“Give up your souls!”

indicate the existence of the soul that leaves the body. His words:

“Today you will be repaid with humiliating punishment”

indicate that recompense follows immediately after death.

Allah, Most High, said in Sūrat al-Anfāl:

“If only you could see when the angels take the disbelievers in death, striking their faces and their backs, saying: ‘Taste the punishment of the burning Fire.’”

“That is for what your hands have sent ahead, and Allah is never unjust to His servants.”

This is their tasting that punishment after death. It is established in both authentic hadith collections through several chains of transmission:

“When the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, came to the pit containing the polytheists on the day of Badr, he called out to them: ‘O so-and-so! O so-and-so! Have you found what your Lord promised to be true? I have indeed found what my Lord promised me to be true.’”

This proves their continued existence, their hearing, and their having encountered the punishment they were promised after death. As for the mere fact that they had been killed, those of their people who remained alive already knew it.

Allah, Most High, said in Sūrat al-Nisā’:

“When the angels take in death those who have wronged themselves, they ask: ‘What circumstances were you in?’ They reply: ‘We were oppressed in the land.’ The angels say: ‘Was Allah’s earth not spacious enough for you to migrate elsewhere?’ Their refuge will be Hell—what an evil destination!”

They are addressed in this way when the angels take them in death. They do not behold the angels until they have lost all hope of remaining in this world. It is known that the body’s tongue has not spoken; rather, it bears witness. One knows that it is the soul that addresses the angels, and an entity being addressed cannot be an accident.

Allah, Most High, said in Sūrat al-Nahl:

“Those whom the angels take in death while they are wronging themselves will offer submission

---

## Page 1590

, saying: ‘We did no evil.’ But yes, you did! Allah knows what you used to do.”

“So enter the gates of Hell, to abide therein forever. How evil is the abode of the arrogant!”

This is an offer of submission at the time of death, and their saying to the angels, “We did no evil.” This can only come from the soul.

He also said in Sūrat al-Nahl:

“Those whom the angels take in death while they are pure, saying to them: ‘Peace be upon you. Enter Paradise for what you used to do.’”

And He said in Sūrat al-Sajdah:

“Indeed, those who say, ‘Our Lord is Allah,’ and then remain steadfast—the angels descend upon them, saying: ‘Do not fear or grieve, but receive glad tidings of the Paradise you were promised.’”

“‘We are your allies in the life of this world and in the Hereafter. There you will have whatever your souls desire, and there you will have whatever you ask for.’”

It has been stated that this descent takes place at death.

Allah, Most High, said in Sūrat Āl ‘Imrān:

“Do not think of those killed in Allah’s cause as dead. Rather, they are alive with their Lord, receiving provision.”

“Rejoicing in what Allah has bestowed upon them of His bounty, and rejoicing for those left behind who have not yet joined them—that they will have no fear, nor will they grieve.”

“They rejoice in Allah’s favour and bounty, and that Allah does not let the reward of the believers go to waste.”

Before that, He said in Sūrat al-Baqarah:

“Do not say of those killed in Allah’s cause, ‘They are dead.’ Rather, they are alive, but you do not perceive it.”

Allah, Most High, also said:

“Allah takes souls at the time of death, and during sleep He takes those that have not died. He then retains those for whom He has decreed death and releases the others until an appointed term.”

And this

---

## Page 1591

makes clear that the soul is taken at the time of death. Some souls He retains and does not return to their bodies—those for whom He has decreed death—while others He releases until an appointed term. This can only apply to something that exists in its own right, not to an accident inhering in something else. Thus, it establishes the existence of the soul that departs from the body at death. Authentic hadiths accord with this, such as the Prophet’s saying, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“In Your name, my Lord, I lay down upon my side, and through You I rise. If You retain my soul, have mercy on it; and if You release it, protect it as You protect Your righteous servants.”

When they slept through the dawn prayer, he said:

“Allah took our souls where He willed.”

Allah, Most High, said:

“It is He who takes your souls by night and knows what you have done by day, then awakens you during it so that an appointed term may be fulfilled. Then to Him is your return, and He will inform you of what you used to do.”

“He is the Subduer over His servants, and He sends guardians over you until, when death comes to one of you, Our messengers take him, and they never neglect their duty.”

“Then they are returned to Allah, their true Lord. Surely His is the judgment, and He is the swiftest of reckoners.”

This describes the taking of souls during sleep until the appointed time of death, when they return to Allah. It also states that the angels take them at death, after which they are returned to Allah. Neither the body nor the accidents inhering in it are returned; it is only the soul that is returned.

This is like His saying in Sūrat Yūnus:

“And they are returned to Allah.”

Allah, Most High, also said:

“Indeed, to your Lord is the return.”

And Allah, Most High, said:

“O tranquil soul!”

“Return to your Lord

---

## Page 1592

well-pleased and pleasing to Him.”

“Then enter among My servants.”

“And enter My Paradise.”

Allah, Most High, also said:

“Say, ‘The Angel of Death entrusted with you will take you; then to your Lord you will be returned.’”

The angel can take only something that exists in its own right; an accident inhering in something else cannot be taken. Thus, the life inhering in the body is not taken; rather, it vanishes and ceases to exist, just as the body’s movement and perception cease to exist.

Allah, Most High, said concerning the believers:

“Until, when death comes to one of them, he says, ‘My Lord, send me back, so that I may do righteous deeds in what I left behind.’ No! It is only a word he is saying; and behind them is a barrier until the Day they are resurrected.”

His words, “Send me back,” are a request for the soul to be returned to the body, as He said in Sūrat al-Wāqiʿah:

“Then why, if you are not subject to recompense, do you not bring it back, if you are truthful?”

This makes clear that the soul exists and departs from the body at death. Allah, Most High, said:

“It is only a word he is saying; and behind them is a barrier until the Day they are resurrected.”

Here it ends. All praise is due to Allah, Lord of the worlds. May Allah bless Muhammad, his family, and his Companions, and grant them peace.

---

## Page 1593

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked about “the believing soul,” which the angels receive and carry up to the heaven in which Allah is.

He replied:

As for the hadith mentioned concerning “the taking of a believer’s soul and its being carried up to the heaven in which Allah is,” this is a well-known hadith with a good chain of transmission. The words “in which Allah is” are equivalent to His words, Most High:

“Do you feel secure that He who is in heaven will not cause the earth to swallow you, and suddenly it would quake?”

“Or do you feel secure that He who is in heaven will not send against you a storm of stones? Then you will know how severe My warning is.”

They are also equivalent to what is established in al-Sahīh:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, asked the slave girl of Mu'āwiyah ibn al-Hakam, ‘Where is Allah?’ She replied, ‘In heaven.’ He asked, ‘Who am I?’ She replied, ‘You are the Messenger of Allah.’ He said, ‘Free her, for she is a believer.’”

This does not mean that heaven confines and contains the Lord as it contains the sun, the moon, and other things. No Muslim says this, nor does any rational person believe it. Indeed, He, glorified and exalted, said:

“His Kursī encompasses the heavens and the earth.”

The heavens, in relation to the Kursī, are like a ring cast into a vast desert; the Kursī, in relation to the Throne, is like a ring cast into a vast desert; and the Lord

---

## Page 1594

—glorified is He—is above His heavens, upon His Throne, distinct from His creation. Nothing of His Essence is within His creation, and nothing of His creation is within His Essence.

He, Most High, said:

“I will surely crucify you on the trunks of palm trees.”

And He said:

“Travel freely throughout the land.”

And He said:

“They will wander in the land.”

This does not mean that they are inside the palm trees or inside the earth. Rather, the meaning is that He is above the heavens and over them, distinct from created things, just as He stated concerning Himself in His Book: He created the heavens and the earth in six days, then rose over the Throne.

And He said:

“O Jesus, I will take you and raise you to Myself.”

He, Most High, also said:

“The angels and the Spirit ascend to Him.”

And He said:

“Rather, Allah raised him to Himself.”

There are other passages of this kind in the Quran and Sunnah. The answer to this question is set out at length elsewhere.

---

## Page 1595

He was asked:

“Does the deceased speak in his grave?”

He replied:

As for the question of whether the deceased speaks in his grave, the answer is that he does speak, and he may also hear whoever addresses him. It is established in an authentic hadith that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“They hear the sound of their sandals.”

It is also established from him in an authentic report:

“The deceased is questioned in his grave. He is asked, ‘Who is your Lord? What is your religion? Who is your prophet?’ Allah keeps those who believe firm with the firm word, and he says, ‘Allah is my Lord, Islam is my religion, and Muhammad is my prophet.’ He is also asked, ‘What do you say about this man who was sent among you?’ The believer says, ‘He is Allah’s servant and Messenger. He brought us clear proofs and guidance, so we believed in him and followed him.’ This is the meaning of His words, Most High: ‘Allah keeps those who believe firm with the firm word in this life and in the Hereafter.’”

It is authentically reported from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, that this verse was revealed concerning the punishment of the grave. Likewise, the hypocrite speaks, saying, “Ah, ah! I do not know. I heard people saying something, so I said it.” He is then struck with an iron sledgehammer and lets out a scream that everything except human beings can hear.

It is also established in an authentic hadith that he said:

“Were it not that you might stop burying one another, I would ask Allah to let you hear the punishment of the grave as I hear it.”

It is further established in an authentic report that he called out to the polytheists on the day of Badr, after casting them into the well, and said:

“You do not hear what I say any better than they do.”

The reports concerning this are numerous and widespread. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1596

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked about Munkar and Nakīr questioning the deceased:

“When a person dies, his soul enters his body, and he sits up and answers Munkar and Nakīr. Would this require him to die a second time?”

He replied:

The soul’s return to the body of the deceased in the grave is not like its return to the body in this worldly life, although that return may be more complete in certain respects. Likewise, existence in the Hereafter is not like existence in this world, although it is more complete. Indeed, each state—in this world, in barzakh (the intermediate realm), and at the Resurrection—is governed by rules of its own.

Thus, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, explained that the deceased’s grave is made spacious for him, that he is questioned, and other such matters, even though the soil may remain unchanged. Souls are returned to the bodies of the deceased and depart from them.

Is this called death? There are two views. Some have said that it is called death, and have interpreted the words of Allah Most High accordingly:

“Our Lord, You made us die twice and gave us life twice.”

It has been said that the first life is in this world and the second life is in the grave.

---

## Page 1597

and the second death is in the grave. The correct view, however, is that this verse is like His saying:

“You were lifeless, and He gave you life; then He will cause you to die, then He will give you life.”

The first death thus precedes this life, and the second death follows it. His words, “then He will give you life,” refer to life after death.

Allah Most High says:

“From it We created you, into it We shall return you, and from it We shall bring you forth once again.”

He also says:

“He said: ‘There you will live, there you will die, and from there you will be brought forth.’”

The soul connects with the body whenever Allah Most High wills and departs from it whenever Allah Most High wills. This is not limited to occurring once or twice. Sleep is the brother of death. Hence:

“When the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, retired to bed, he would say: ‘In Your name, O Allah, I die and I live.’”

“When he awoke, he would say: ‘All praise is due to Allah, who has given us life after causing us to die, and to Him is the resurrection.’”

He thus called sleep death and waking life.

Allah Most High has said:

“Allah takes the souls at the time of their death, and during sleep those that have not died. He then retains those for whom He has decreed death and releases the others until an appointed term.”

He has thus explained that He takes souls in two ways: He takes them at death, and He takes through sleep the souls of those who have not died. When people sleep, He retains the soul of anyone who dies in sleep and releases the soul of anyone who does not die. Hence:

“When the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, retired to bed, he would say: ‘In Your name, my Lord, I lay down my side, and by You I raise it. If You retain my soul, have mercy on it; and if You release it, protect it as You protect Your righteous servants.’”

A sleeping person experiences pleasure and pain in sleep, and this affects both the soul and the body, to the point that

---

## Page 1598

he may dream that someone is striking him and wake with pain in his body. He may also dream that he has been fed something delicious and wake with its taste in his mouth. This does occur. If, then, a sleeping person experiences bliss and torment in both soul and body, while the person beside him feels none of it—so much so that the sleeper may cry out from intense pain or fright, and someone awake hears his cries—how can the state of a person in the grave be denied? The sleeper may even speak, reciting the Quran, remembering Allah, or answering someone. A person who is awake hears this, although the sleeper’s eyes are closed and he would not hear if addressed. Yet the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, told us that the person in the grave hears the sound of their sandals.

He also said:

“You hear what I say no better than they do.”

The heart resembles the grave. Thus, when he missed the afternoon prayer on the day of the Battle of the Trench, he said, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“May Allah fill their insides and their graves with fire.”

In another wording:

“their hearts and their graves with fire.”

Allah distinguished between the two in His words:

“what is in the graves is scattered forth”

“and what is within the breasts is brought to light.”

This analogy makes the matter easier to understand and establishes its possibility.

It must not be said that the bliss and torment experienced by the dead are like those experienced by a sleeper in his dreams. Rather, that bliss and torment are more complete, more intense, and more perfect. They are real bliss and real torment. This example is cited only to explain how this is possible when a questioner says, “The dead person does not move in his grave, and the soil does not change,” or something similar. This matter nevertheless calls for a lengthy discussion and an explanation beyond the scope of this sheet. Allah knows best. May Allah bless Muhammad, his family, and his Companions, and grant them peace.

---

## Page 1599

He was asked:

Concerning a young child and an infant: if he dies, will he be tested? And so forth…

(1)
Withholding judgment concerning them and saying, “Allah knows best what they would have done.” A fuller discussion belongs elsewhere.

When a child dies, is he tested in his grave and questioned by Munkar and Nakīr? There are two views within the school of Ahmad and among others.

The first is that he is not tested, and that this trial applies only to those who were subject to religious obligations in this world. A group of scholars held this view, including al-Qādī Abū Yaʿlā and Ibn ʿAqīl.

The second is that children are tested. This was mentioned by Abū Hakīm al-Hamdānī and Abū al-Hasan ibn ʿAbdūs, who also transmitted it from the scholars of al-Shāfiʿī’s school. The question of “prompting the young child and the mentally unsound after burial” follows this distinction: whoever holds that the deceased is tested in the grave prompts him, while whoever holds that he is not tested does not prompt him.

Mālik and others narrated from Abū Hurayrah, may Allah be pleased with him:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, offered the funeral prayer over a child and said, ‘O Allah, protect him from the torment of the grave and the trial of the grave.’”

This view accords with the position that they will be tested in the Hereafter and will be subject to religious obligations on the Day of Resurrection, as is the view of most scholars

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) The beginning of the answer is missing.

---

## Page 1600

and of Ahl al-Sunnah among the scholars of hadith and speculative theology. This is the view that Abū al-Hasan al-Ashʿarī reported from Ahl al-Sunnah and himself adopted, and it is what Imam Ahmad’s statements imply. And Allah knows best.

When the children of believers enter Paradise, their souls and those of other believers are in Paradise, although their ranks differ. Children differ in rank according to the ranks of their fathers and the merits of their own deeds—if they have any deeds. Ibrāhīm, the son of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is not like others. Young children are rewarded for the good deeds they perform, although their evil deeds are not recorded against them. As established in an authentic hadith:

“A woman lifted up a child from a litter to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and asked, ‘Is Hajj valid for this child?’ He replied, ‘Yes, and you will have a reward.’”

Muslim narrated this in his Sahīh. The Sunan collections also report that he said:

“Instruct them to pray at seven, strike them for not doing so at ten, and separate them in their beds.”

They would have young children fast on the day of ʿĀshūrāʾ and on other days. Thus, a child is rewarded for his prayer, fasting, Hajj, and other deeds, and thereby receives a higher rank than one who has not performed comparable deeds. This is distinct from what is done for him in honour of his parents. Likewise, among worldly blessings, he may benefit both from what he earns and from what his parents give him, and thereby enjoy an advantage over someone who does not have these benefits.

The souls of believers are in Paradise, as the transmitted reports state. This accords with the Prophet’s words, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“The believer’s soul feeds from Paradise.”

That is, it eats. No particular time before the Day of Resurrection was specified for this.

---

## Page 1601

Souls are undoubtedly created. They do not cease to exist or perish; rather, their death consists in their separation from the bodies. At the second blast of the Trumpet, the souls will be returned to the bodies. Those who enter Paradise will have the form of their father Adam, peace be upon him, each standing sixty cubits tall, as established in authentic hadiths.

Some people have said that the children of unbelievers will be servants of the people of Paradise, but this view has no foundation. It is established in the two Sahīh collections that Paradise will have space left beyond what is occupied by the people of this world. Allah will therefore create other beings for it and settle them in Paradise. If He settles those whom He creates from Paradise, who are not descendants of Adam, in its remaining spaces, then what of Adam’s descendants who enter it and are settled elsewhere within it? They are therefore no more entitled to be among the people of Paradise than those who are created afterward and inhabit its remaining spaces.

As for the coming to Hell mentioned in the words of Allah Most High:

“There is not one of you who will not come to it,”

the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, explained it in the authentic hadith narrated by Muslim in his Sahīh from Jābir:

“It means passing over the Sirāt.”

The Sirāt is the bridge; everyone who enters Paradise must cross it, whether they were young children in this world or not.

(The youths who attend to the people of Paradise are beings created from Paradise, not children from this world. Rather, when the children of the people of this world enter Paradise, their physical form will reach its perfection, like that of the other people of Paradise: in the form of Adam, aged thirty-three and standing sixty cubits tall, as mentioned earlier. It has also been reported that their width is seven cubits. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1602

The Shaykh—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

Will a young child be brought back to life and questioned, or brought back to life without being questioned? What will he be questioned about? Are those who are subject to religious obligations and those who are not treated alike with regard to being brought back to life and questioned?

He replied:

All praise is due to Allah, Lord of the worlds. Regarding someone who is not subject to religious obligations, such as a young child or a person of unsound mind, scholars hold two views on whether he will be tested in his grave and questioned by Munkar and Nakīr.

The first view is that he will be tested. This is the view of the majority of Ahl al-Sunnah, as reported from them by Abū al-Hasan ibn Abdūs and also mentioned by Abū Hakīm al-Nahrawānī and others.

The second view is that he will not be tested in his grave, as mentioned by Judge Abū Ya‘lā, Ibn ‘Aqīl, and others. They argue that this test applies only to those who were subject to religious obligations in this world.

Those who hold the first view cite as evidence the report in al-Muwatta’ from Abū Hurayrah, may Allah be pleased with him:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, offered the funeral prayer for a young child who had never committed a sin and said: ‘O Allah, protect him from the punishment of the grave and the trial of the grave.’”

This indicates that he will undergo the trial.

---

## Page 1603

Moreover, this rests on the view that the children of disbelievers, who were not subject to religious obligations in this world, will be subject to them in the Hereafter, as reported in several hadiths. This is the view that Abū al-Hasan al-Ashʿarī attributed to Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamāʿah. For the position transmitted from the imams, such as Imam Ahmad and others, is to withhold judgement regarding the children of polytheists, as established in the two Sahīh collections: when the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was asked about them, he said:

“Allah knows best what they would have done.”

It is also established in Sahīh al-Bukhārī, in the hadith of Samurah, that some of them will enter Paradise. And it is established in Sahīh Muslim that the boy whom al-Khidr killed was formed as a disbeliever on the day he was formed.

If, among children and others, there are both the wretched and the blessed, and if that is due to their being tested in this world, then their being tested in their graves is not precluded. This, however, rests on the view that one does not testify that each particular child of the believers is in Paradise, even if this is affirmed of them in general. Even if it is affirmed of them in general, a child born among Muslims may be a hypocrite among believers. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1604

While in Egypt, Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah sanctify his soul—was asked about “punishment in the grave”:

“Does it affect both the soul and the body, or the soul alone, without the body? Is the deceased punished in his grave while alive or while dead? Whether the soul returns to the body or not, do they both share in punishment and bliss, or does this affect one without the other?”

He replied—may Allah be pleased with him and make the Garden of Firdaws his final abode and dwelling place. Amen:

All praise is due to Allah, Lord of the worlds. Rather, punishment and bliss affect both the soul and the body, according to the agreement of Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamāʿah. The soul experiences bliss and punishment independently of the body, and it is punished while joined to the body. The body is joined to it, so in this state both experience bliss and punishment together, just as the soul experiences them independently of the body. As for whether the body experiences punishment and bliss without the soul, there are two well-known views

---

## Page 1605

among the scholars of hadith and the Sunnah and the practitioners of speculative theology. There are also aberrant views on this matter that are not among the positions of the people of the Sunnah and hadith.

One is the view of those who say that bliss and punishment affect only the soul, and that the body experiences neither bliss nor punishment. This is maintained by the “philosophers” who deny bodily resurrection; they are unbelievers by the consensus of the Muslims. It is also maintained by many “speculative theologians” among the Muʿtazilites and others, who say that this does not occur during the intermediate state between death and resurrection, but only upon rising from the graves.

Another is the view of those who say that the soul, on its own, experiences neither bliss nor punishment, and that the soul is simply life itself. This is maintained by groups of speculative theologians among the Muʿtazilites and the followers of Abū al-Hasan al-Ashʿarī, such as Judge Abū Bakr and others. They deny that the soul continues to exist after departing from the body. This is a false view, opposed by the scholar Abū al-Maʿālī al-Juwaynī and others. Indeed, the Quran, the Sunnah, and the agreement of the early generations of this community establish that the soul continues to exist after departing from the body, and that it experiences either bliss or punishment.

The theistic “philosophers” affirm this, but deny bodily resurrection. Those theologians acknowledge bodily resurrection, but deny the resurrection of souls and their bliss and punishment independently of bodies. Both views are erroneous and misguided. Yet the philosophers’ view is further removed from the positions of the people of Islam, even though they may be joined in it by someone who believes that he adheres to the religion of Islam—indeed, someone who considers himself versed in spiritual knowledge, Sufism, rigorous inquiry, and speculative theology.

---

## Page 1606

The third aberrant view is that there is neither bliss nor punishment in the intermediate state between death and resurrection; rather, neither occurs until the great Resurrection takes place. This is maintained by some Muʿtazilites and others who deny punishment and bliss in the grave, on the grounds that the soul does not continue to exist after departing from the body, and that the body experiences neither bliss nor punishment.

All those belonging to these two groups are misguided concerning the intermediate state, but they are better than the philosophers because they acknowledge the great Resurrection.

Once you have understood these three false views, know that the position of “the early generations of this community and its leading scholars” is that, when a person dies, he experiences bliss or punishment, and that this affects both his soul and his body. The soul continues to exist after departing from the body, experiencing bliss or punishment, and at times it reconnects with the body, which then shares in its bliss and punishment. Then, on the day of the great Resurrection, souls will be restored to their bodies, and people will rise from their graves before the Lord of all worlds. Bodily resurrection is agreed upon by Muslims, Jews, and Christians. All of this is agreed upon by the scholars of hadith and the Sunnah.

But can the body experience bliss or punishment without the soul? Some of these scholars affirm this, while most deny it.

---

## Page 1607

We shall now present what clarifies what we have said. The hadiths concerning punishment in the grave and the questioning by Munkar and Nakīr are numerous and mass-transmitted from the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace). Among them is the report in the two Sahīh collections from Ibn ‘Abbās (may Allah be pleased with them both):

“The Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) passed by two graves and said, ‘Both of them are being punished, yet they are not being punished for anything great. One of them used to go about spreading malicious gossip, while the other did not protect himself from his urine.’ Then he asked for a fresh palm frond, split it in two, and planted one half in each grave. They asked, ‘O Messenger of Allah, why have you done this?’ He replied, ‘Perhaps their punishment will be lightened so long as these two do not dry out.’”

In Sahīh Muslim, Zayd ibn Thābit said:

“While the Messenger of Allah (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) was riding a mule in a garden belonging to Banū al-Najjār, with us accompanying him, it suddenly swerved and nearly threw him off. There were six, five, or four graves there. He asked, ‘Who knows about these graves?’ A man replied, ‘I do.’ He asked, ‘When did these people die?’ The man replied, ‘They died in a state of polytheism.’ He said, ‘This community is indeed put to trial in its graves. Were it not that you might cease burying one another, I would ask Allah to let you hear some of the punishment of the grave that I hear.’

“Then he turned to face us and said, ‘Seek refuge in Allah from the punishment of the grave.’ They said, ‘We seek refuge in Allah from the punishment of the grave.’

“He said, ‘Seek refuge in Allah from the punishment of the Fire.’ They said, ‘We seek refuge in Allah from the punishment of the Fire.’

“He said, ‘Seek refuge in Allah from trials, both manifest and hidden.’ They said, ‘We seek refuge in Allah from trials, both manifest and hidden.’

“He said, ‘Seek refuge in Allah from the trial of the Dajjāl.’ They said, ‘We seek refuge in Allah from the trial of the Dajjāl.’”

In Sahīh Muslim and the other Sunan collections, Abū Hurayrah (may Allah be pleased with him) reports that the Prophet

---

## Page 1608

(may Allah bless him and grant him peace) said:

“When any of you has finished the final tashahhud (testimony of faith), let him say, ‘I seek refuge in Allah from four things: the punishment of Hell, the punishment of the grave, the trials of life and death, and the trial of the false messiah, the Dajjāl.’”

In Sahīh Muslim and elsewhere, Ibn ‘Abbās (may Allah be pleased with them both) reports that the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) used to teach them this supplication just as he would teach them a surah of the Quran:

“O Allah, I seek refuge in You from the punishment of Hell; I seek refuge in You from the punishment of the grave; I seek refuge in You from the trial of the false messiah, the Dajjāl; and I seek refuge in You from the trials of life and death.”

In Sahīh al-Bukhārī and Sahīh Muslim, Abū Ayyūb al-Ansārī said:

“The Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) went out after the sun had set and said, ‘Some Jews are being punished in their graves.’”

In the two Sahīh collections, ‘Ā’ishah (may Allah be pleased with her) said:

“An elderly Jewish woman from Medina came to me and said, ‘The people in the graves are being punished in their graves.’ I rejected what she said and could not bring myself to believe her. She left, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) came to me. I said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, an elderly woman from Medina came to me and claimed that the people in the graves are being punished in their graves.’

“He replied, ‘She spoke the truth. They are indeed suffering a punishment that all animals can hear.’ After that, I never saw him pray without seeking refuge from the punishment of the grave.”

In the Sahīh of Abū Hātim al-Bustī, Umm Mubashshir (may Allah be pleased with her) said:

“The Messenger of Allah (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) came to me while I was in a garden, saying, ‘Seek refuge in Allah from the punishment of

---

## Page 1609

the grave.’ I said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, is there punishment in the grave?’ He replied, ‘They are indeed being punished in their graves with a punishment that animals can hear.’”

Some have said: This is why people take their animals, when they suffer from colic, to the graves of Jews, Christians and hypocrites, such as the Ismā‘īlīs, the Nusayrīs and the other Qarmatians, including Banū ‘Ubayd and others in Egypt, Syria and elsewhere. Horse owners deliberately visit their graves for this purpose, just as they visit the graves of Jews and Christians. The ignorant suppose that they are descendants of Fātimah and among Allah’s righteous allies, whereas this is merely an instance of what has just been described. For it has been said that when horses hear the punishment of the grave, they develop a heat that dispels their colic.

There are many hadiths on this, more than the scope of this question permits us to discuss. There are also many hadiths concerning this matter, including the report in Sahīh al-Bukhārī, Sahīh Muslim and the Sunan collections from al-Barā’ ibn ‘Āzib (may Allah be pleased with him), who reported that the Messenger of Allah (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) said:

“When a Muslim is questioned in his grave, he testifies that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah and that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah. This is what Allah Most High means by His words: ‘Allah keeps firm those who believe, with the firm word, in worldly life and in the Hereafter.’”

Another wording states:

“This was revealed concerning the punishment of the grave. He is asked, ‘Who is your Lord?’ He replies, ‘My Lord is Allah, my religion is Islam, and my prophet is Muhammad.’ This is what Allah Most High means by His words: ‘Allah keeps firm those who believe, with the firm word, in worldly life and in the Hereafter. And Allah sends astray the wrongdoers. And Allah does what He wills.’”

The compilers of the Sunan and Musnad collections have narrated this hadith at length, as in Sunan Abī Dāwūd

---

## Page 1610

and elsewhere, on the authority of al-Barā’ ibn ‘Āzib (may Allah be pleased with him), who said:

“We accompanied the Messenger of Allah (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) to the funeral of a man from the Ansār. We reached the grave before its burial niche had been prepared. The Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) sat down, and we sat around him, as still as though birds were perched on our heads. He held a stick with which he scratched the ground. Then he raised his head and said, ‘Seek refuge in Allah from the punishment of the grave,’ repeating this twice or three times. He described how the soul is taken, how it ascends to heaven, and then how it returns to the deceased, until he said, ‘He certainly hears the sound of their sandals as they turn and walk away, when he is asked, “Who is your Lord? What is your religion? Who is your prophet?”’”

Another wording states:

“Two angels come to him, sit him up and ask him, ‘Who is your Lord?’ He replies, ‘My Lord is Allah.’ They ask him, ‘What is your religion?’ He replies, ‘My religion is Islam.’ They ask, ‘Who is this man who was sent among you?’ He replies, ‘He is the Messenger of Allah.’ They ask, ‘How do you know?’ He replies, ‘I read the Book of Allah, believed in it and affirmed its truth.’ This is what Allah means by His words:

‘Allah keeps firm those who believe, with the firm word, in worldly life and in the Hereafter. And Allah sends astray the wrongdoers. And Allah does what He wills.’”

He said:

“Then a caller from heaven proclaims, ‘My servant has spoken the truth. Prepare bedding for him in Paradise, clothe him from Paradise, and open a door for him to Paradise.’”

He said:

“Some of its refreshing breeze and fragrance reach him.”

He said:

“His grave is expanded for him as far as his eye can see.”

He said, “As for the disbeliever…” and described his death. Then he said:

“His soul is returned to his body, and two angels come to him and sit him up. They ask him, ‘Who is your Lord?’ He replies, ‘Hāh, hāh! I do not know.’ They ask him, ‘What is your religion?’ He replies, ‘Hāh, hāh! I do not know.’ Then a caller from heaven proclaims, ‘My servant has lied. Prepare bedding for him from the Fire, and clothe him from the Fire

---

## Page 1611

and open a door for him to the Fire.’” He said:

“Some of its heat and scorching wind reach him.” He said: “His grave is constricted around him until his ribs interlock.” He said: “Then someone blind and mute is assigned to him, carrying an iron sledgehammer which, if used to strike a mountain, would reduce it to dust.” He said: “He strikes him with it once, a blow heard by all between the east and the west except humans and jinn, reducing him to dust. Then his soul is restored to him.”

The hadith thus explicitly states that the soul is restored to the body and that his ribs interlock. This clearly indicates that the punishment afflicts both the soul and the body together.

A report similar to the hadith of al-Barā’, concerning the taking of the soul, the questioning, the bliss and the punishment, has also been narrated by Abū Hurayrah. His hadith appears in al-Musnad and elsewhere. Abū Hātim ibn Hibbān also narrated it in his Sahīh, on the authority of Abū Hurayrah (may Allah be pleased with him), from the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace), who said:

“When the deceased is placed in his grave, he hears the sound of their sandals as they turn and walk away. If he is a believer, prayer stands at his head, fasting to his right, charity to his left, and good deeds—charity, maintaining ties of kinship, kindness and benevolence—at his feet. The two angels approach him from the direction of his head, and prayer says, ‘There is no entrance on my side.’

“Then he is approached from his right, and fasting says, ‘There is no entrance on my side.’ Then he is approached from his left, and obligatory almsgiving says, ‘There is no entrance on my side.’ Then he is approached from the direction of his feet, and good deeds—charity, maintaining ties of kinship, kindness and benevolence—say, ‘There is no entrance on my side.’

“Then he is told, ‘Sit up.’ He sits up, and the sun is made to appear to him as though it is nearing sunset. He says, ‘Let me pray first.’ They say, ‘You will pray. Tell us about what we ask you: this man who was among you—what do you say about him? And what

---

## Page 1612

testimony do you bear concerning him?’

He replies, ‘Muhammad. We bear witness that he is the Messenger of Allah, who brought the truth from Allah.’

He is told, ‘Upon this belief you lived, and upon it you will be resurrected, if Allah wills.’ Then a door to Paradise is opened for him, and he is told, ‘This is your dwelling and what Allah has prepared for you there.’ His joy and happiness increase. Then his grave is widened for him by seventy cubits and illuminated. His body is returned to that from which it originated, and his soul becomes the spirit of a bird feeding among the trees of Paradise. He said: This is what Allah Most High says:

‘Allah keeps those who believe firm with the firm word, in the life of this world and in the Hereafter. And Allah leaves the wrongdoers astray, and Allah does what He wills.’”

Regarding disbelief, he mentioned the opposite, saying: “His grave is constricted around him until his ribs interlock. This is the life of hardship of which Allah Most High says:

‘He will have a life of hardship, and We will raise him blind on the Day of Resurrection.’”

This hadith is shorter. The previously cited hadith of al-Barā’ is the longest in the Sunan collections; their compilers abridged it to present what it contains concerning the punishment of the grave. It appears in full in al-Musnad and elsewhere.

It is a sound, established hadith, in which the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) says:

“When the believing servant is approaching the Hereafter and departing from this world, angels with radiant faces, as though their faces were the sun, descend to him. They bring a shroud from the shrouds of Paradise and embalming perfume from the perfumes of Paradise. They sit at a distance from him as far as the eye can see. Then the Angel of Death comes and sits at his head, saying, ‘O pure soul, come out to forgiveness and approval.’

He said: “It flows out as a drop flows from the mouth of a waterskin, and he takes it. Once he has taken it, they do not leave it in his hand even for the blink of an eye

---

## Page 1613

before they take it and place it in that shroud with that embalming perfume. From it comes a fragrance like the finest scent of musk ever found on the face of the earth. He said: They ascend with it, and whenever they pass a company of angels, they ask, ‘What is this pure soul?’ They reply, ‘So-and-so, son of so-and-so,’ using the finest names by which he was known in this world. They bring him to the lowest heaven and ask for it to be opened for him, and it is opened. He said: The angels nearest to Allah in each heaven escort him to the next, until they bring the soul to the seventh heaven.

Then He says, ‘Enter My servant’s record in Illiyyīn and return him to the earth, for from it I created them, to it I return them, and from it I shall bring them forth once again.’ He said: His soul is returned to his body, and two angels come to him and sit him up.” He then mentioned the questioning as related above.

He said: “A man with a handsome face and a pleasant fragrance comes to him and says, ‘Receive glad tidings of what will delight you! This is the day you were promised.’ He asks him, ‘Who are you? Yours is a face that brings good.’ He replies, ‘I am your righteous deeds.’

Then he says, ‘My Lord, bring on the Hour! My Lord, bring on the Hour! My Lord, bring on the Hour, so that I may return to my family and my wealth!’”

He said: “When the disbelieving servant is approaching the Hereafter and departing from this world, angels with black faces descend to him from heaven, bringing cloths of hair. They sit at a distance from him as far as the eye can see. Then the Angel of Death comes and sits at his head, saying, ‘O wicked soul, come out to Allah’s displeasure and wrath.’ It then disperses throughout all his limbs, and he wrenches it out as a skewer is pulled from wet wool, tearing the veins and sinews along with it. He said: He takes it, and once he has taken it, they do not leave it in his hand even for the blink of an eye before taking it and placing it in those cloths of hair. He said: From it comes a stench like that of the foulest carcass ever found on the face of the earth. Then they ascend with it

---

## Page 1614

and whenever they pass a company of angels, they ask, ‘What is this wicked soul?’ They reply, ‘So-and-so, son of so-and-so,’ using the ugliest names by which he was known in this world. They bring it to the lowest heaven and ask for it to be opened for it, but it is not opened. Then the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, recited:

‘The gates of heaven will not be opened for them, nor will they enter Paradise until a camel passes through the eye of a needle. Thus do We recompense the criminals.’

Then Allah Most High says, ‘Enter his record in Sijjīn—in the lowest earth.’ He said: His soul is then flung down. Then the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, recited:

‘Or the wind sweeps him away to a far-off place.’

He said: His soul is returned to his body, and two angels come to him and sit him up. They ask him, ‘Who is your Lord?’ He replies, ‘Ah, ah! I do not know.’”

He related the hadith as above until he said: “A man with an ugly face and a foul stench comes to him and says, ‘Receive tidings of what will distress you! This is your deed, which you were promised.’ He asks, ‘Who are you? Yours is a face that brings no good.’ He replies, ‘I am your evil deeds.’ Then he says three times, ‘My Lord, do not bring on the Hour!’”

This hadith contains various kinds of knowledge. Among them is that the soul continues to exist after leaving the body, contrary to the misguided speculative theologians; that it ascends and descends, contrary to the misguided philosophers; that it is returned to the body; and that the deceased is questioned and then granted bliss or subjected to punishment, as those who submitted the question had asked about. It also establishes that his righteous or evil deeds come to him in a beautiful or ugly form.

In the two Sahīh collections, Qatādah narrates from Anas ibn Mālik, may Allah be pleased with him, that the Prophet

---

## Page 1615

may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“When a servant is laid in his grave and his companions turn away, he hears the sound of their sandals. Two angels come to him and question him, asking, ‘What did you used to say about this man, Muhammad?’ The believer replies, ‘I testify that he is Muhammad, Allah’s servant and Messenger.’” He said: “Then he says, ‘Look at your place in the Fire. Allah has replaced it for you with a place in Paradise.’” The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “He sees them both.”

Qatādah said: “It was mentioned to us that his grave is made spacious for him by seventy cubits and filled with fresh delights until the Day they are resurrected.” Then we return to the hadith of Anas:

“They come to the disbeliever and the hypocrite and ask, ‘What did you used to say about this man?’ He replies, ‘I do not know. I used to say what people said.’ He is then told, ‘You neither knew nor recited!’ Then he is struck between his ears with iron hammers and lets out a scream that is heard by everyone on earth except humans and jinn.”

Al-Tirmidhī and Abū Hātim, in his Sahīh—with most of the wording being his—narrated from Abū Hurayrah, may Allah be pleased with him, who said:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: ‘When one of you buries a person, two angels, black and blue in appearance, come to him. One is called Munkar and the other Nakīr. They ask him, “What did you used to say about this man, Muhammad?” He answers with what he used to say. If he is a believer, he says, “He is Allah’s servant and Messenger. I testify that there is no god but Allah, and I testify that Muhammad is His servant and Messenger.” They say, “We knew that you would say that.” Then his grave is made spacious for him by seventy cubits and illuminated for him. He is told, “Sleep.” He says, “Let me return to my family and tell them.” They say to him, “Sleep like a bridegroom whom none but his most beloved family member awakens,” until Allah raises him from that resting place. But if he is a hypocrite, he says:

---

## Page 1616

“I do not know. I used to hear people saying something, so I said it.” The two angels say, “We knew that you would say that.” Then the earth is told, “Close in upon him.” It closes in upon him until his ribs interlock, and he continues to be tormented until Allah raises him from that resting place.’”

This hadith mentions his ribs interlocking, along with other details that make clear that the body itself is tormented.

Abū Hurayrah, may Allah be pleased with him, reported that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“When a person is dying, the angels come to him with a white silk cloth. They say, ‘Come out with a fragrance like the finest musk.’ They pass the soul from one to another until they bring it to the gate of heaven. Those there say, ‘How lovely this fragrance is! When did it come to you from the earth?’ They then bring it to the souls of the believers, who rejoice at its arrival more than any of you rejoices when someone absent returns to him. They ask him, ‘What has so-and-so done?’ Then they say, ‘Leave him, for he is in the distress of worldly life.’ When he says, ‘He has come to you,’ they say, ‘He has gone to his mother, the Abyss.’

“When a disbeliever is dying, the angels of punishment come to him with a cloth of wool or hair. They say, ‘Come out, under Allah’s wrath, to Allah’s punishment.’ The soul comes out smelling like the foulest carcass, and they bring it to the souls of the disbelievers.”

This was narrated by al-Nasā’ī and al-Bazzār. Muslim narrated an abridged version from Abū Hurayrah, may Allah be pleased with him. At the mention of the disbeliever and the foul smell of his soul, the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, drew a wrap he was wearing over his nose, like this. A raytah is a thin, soft cloth, like a sheet.

Abū Hātim also included it in his Sahīh, with the wording:

“When death comes to a believer, the angels of mercy attend him. Once his soul is taken, it is placed in a white silk cloth, and they carry it to the gate of heaven. Those there say, ‘We have never encountered a fragrance lovelier than this.’ Then it is said, ‘Leave him

---

## Page 1617

to rest, for he was in the distress of worldly life.’ Then it is asked, ‘What has so-and-so done? What has such-and-such a woman done?’ As for the disbeliever, when his soul is taken, it is brought to the earth. The keepers of the earth say, ‘We have never encountered a stench fouler than this,’ and it is taken down to the lowest earth.”

These hadiths and others like them describe the soul and body sharing in the bliss and torment of the grave. As for what the soul experiences on its own, some of that has already been discussed.

Kaʿb ibn Mālik, may Allah be pleased with him, reported that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The believer’s soul is a bird that feeds among the trees of Paradise until Allah returns it to its body on the day He resurrects him.”

This was narrated by al-Nasā’ī, and also by both Mālik and al-Shāfiʿī. The word “yaʿlaqu,” read with a u vowel, means “to eat.” This has also been reported in other hadiths. These texts tell us that the soul enjoys bliss together with the body in the grave—if Allah wills—whereas it enjoys bliss in Paradise on its own. Both are true.

Ibn Abī al-Dunyā narrated in his book Dhikr al-Mawt (The Remembrance of Death) that Mālik ibn Anas said:

“It has reached me that the soul is released to go wherever it wishes.”

This agrees with the report that “the soul may be in the open spaces around the graves.” As Mujāhid said, “Souls remain at the graves for seven days from the day the deceased is buried, without departing; and the soul may be returned to the body at times other than the questioning.” This is illustrated by the hadith that Ibn ʿAbd al-Barr authenticated, in which the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“No man passes by the grave of a man he knew in this world and greets him with peace without Allah restoring the deceased’s soul to him so that he may return the greeting.”

---

## Page 1618

In the Sunan of Abū Dāwūd and elsewhere, Aws ibn Aws al-Thaqafī reported that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The best of your days is Friday, so send abundant blessings upon me on Friday and the night preceding it, for your blessings are presented to me.”

They asked, “O Messenger of Allah, how will our blessings be presented to you when you have decayed?”

He replied, “Allah has forbidden the earth to consume the bodies of the prophets.”

There are more hadiths and reports on this subject than time presently permits us to examine exhaustively. They make clear that bodies in their graves experience bliss and torment—if Allah wills it—in whatever manner He wills, and that souls continue to exist after leaving the body, experiencing bliss and torment. For this reason, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, commanded that the dead be greeted with peace, as established in the Sahīh and Sunan collections:

“He would teach his Companions, when they visited the graves, to say: ‘Peace be upon you, believing inhabitants of these dwellings. We shall, if Allah wills, join you. May Allah have mercy on those among us and you who have gone before and those who come after. We ask Allah for well-being for ourselves and for you. O Allah, do not deprive us of the reward associated with them, do not subject us to trials after them, and forgive us and them.’”

This has been disclosed to many people, who have heard the voices of those being tormented in their graves and seen with their own eyes those undergoing torment there, as recounted in numerous well-known reports. However, this need not affect the body continuously at every moment; rather, it may occur at some times and not at others.

---

## Page 1619

In the two Sahīh collections, Anas ibn Mālik, may Allah be pleased with him, reported:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, left those slain at Badr for three days. Then he came to them, stood over them, and said: ‘O Abū Jahl ibn Hishām! O Umayyah ibn Khalaf! O ‘Utbah ibn Rabī‘ah! O Shaybah ibn Rabī‘ah! Have you not found what your Lord promised you to be true? I have indeed found what my Lord promised me to be true.’

‘Umar, may Allah be pleased with him, heard the Prophet’s words and asked: ‘O Messenger of Allah, how can they hear when their bodies have putrefied?’

He replied: ‘By Him in Whose hand is my soul, you hear what I say no better than they do, but they cannot answer.’ Then he ordered that they be dragged away and thrown into the well of Badr.”

Both compilers also recorded in their Sahīh collections the report of Ibn ‘Umar, may Allah be pleased with them both:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, stood beside the well of Badr and said: ‘Have you found what your Lord promised you to be true?’ He also said: ‘They can indeed now hear what I am saying.’

This was mentioned to ‘Ā’ishah, who said: ‘Ibn ‘Umar was mistaken. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, only said: “They now know that what I told them is the truth.”’ Then she recited the words of the Most High, ‘You cannot make the dead hear,’ continuing to the end of the verse.”

Scholars of hadith and the Sunnah agree that the reports narrated by Anas and Ibn ‘Umar are authentic, even though neither attended Badr. Anas narrated this from Abū Talhah, who did attend Badr.

Thus, Abū Hātim recorded in his Sahīh, from Anas, from Abū Talhah, may Allah be pleased with him:

“On the day of Badr, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, ordered that twenty-four of the leading men of Quraysh be thrown into one of the stone-lined wells of Badr. Whenever he prevailed over a people, he liked to remain on their grounds for three nights:

---

## Page 1620

so when the third day came, he called for his mount, secured its saddle and set it in motion, then walked on, followed by his Companions. They said: ‘We think he is only going to attend to some need.’ He continued until he stood at the edge of the well, then began calling them by their names and their fathers’ names: ‘O so-and-so, son of so-and-so! Would you be glad if you had obeyed Allah and His Messenger? We have indeed found what our Lord promised us to be true. Have you found what your Lord promised you to be true?’

‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb said: ‘O Messenger of Allah, you are only speaking to bodies with no souls in them.’ The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, replied: ‘By Him in Whose hand is my soul, you hear what I say no better than they do.’”

Qatādah said: “Allah brought them to life so that they heard, to rebuke and humiliate them, to punish them and cause them anguish and remorse.”

‘Ā’ishah interpreted the matter she mentioned, just as she interpreted other similar matters. An authentic statement from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, takes precedence over the interpretation of any of his Companions or anyone else. Nothing in the Quran negates this, for His words,

“You cannot make the dead hear,”

refer only to the ordinary hearing that benefits the hearer. This is a comparison drawn for the disbelievers: they hear sounds, but they do not listen with acceptance, understanding and obedience. As the Most High says:

“The example of those who disbelieve is like that of one who shouts at what hears nothing but calls and cries.”

Likewise, it does not follow that all ordinary hearing—all forms of hearing—must be ruled out for the dead used in this comparison, just as this was not ruled out for the disbelievers. Rather, what is absent in their case is the ordinary hearing from which they would benefit; other hearing is not ruled out for them.

---

## Page 1621

It is also established in the two Sahīh collections and elsewhere that the deceased hears the sound of their sandals as they turn and walk away. This accords with what has been stated, so how can it be rejected?

Some scholars hold that the deceased does not hear in his grave so long as he remains dead, as ‘Ā’ishah stated, citing evidence from the Quran. If Allah brings him to life, however, he hears, as Qatādah said: “Allah brought them to life for that purpose.” Even if they do not hear through that life, just as we do not see the angels and jinn, we do not know what the deceased experiences in his sleep. Likewise, a person does not know what is in another’s heart, although someone to whom Allah has disclosed it may know it.

This is a summary sufficient to answer the questioner’s inquiry, although further explanation and detail would be beyond the scope of this discussion. For the clear evidence we have cited concerning his question is rarely gathered together. Allah knows best. May Allah bless Muhammad, his family and his Companions, and grant them peace.

---

## Page 1622

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah sanctify his soul—said:

Someone asked: “In what language will people be addressed on the Day of Resurrection? Will Allah Most High address them in Arabic? Is it true that the language of the people of Hell is Persian and that the language of the people of Paradise is Arabic?”

After saying, “Praise be to Allah, Lord of all worlds,” I answered him:

It is not known what language people will speak that day, nor in what language they will hear the address of the Lord, Glorious and Exalted. Neither Allah Most High nor His Messenger—peace and blessings be upon him—has informed us of any of this. It has not been authentically established that Persian is the language of the people of Hell, or that Arabic is the language of those enjoying eternal bliss. We know of no disagreement concerning this among the Companions—may Allah be pleased with them. Rather, they all refrained from discussing it, because speaking about such matters is superfluous talk; nor did Allah Most High say anything concerning this to the people of the earth. However, disagreement on this matter arose among later generations.

Some said: “They will converse in Arabic.” Others said: “Except for the people of Hell: they will reply in Persian, which is their language in Hell.”

---

## Page 1623

And others said: “They will converse in Syriac, because it was the language of Adam, from which the other languages branched out.”

Others said: “Except for the people of Paradise, who will speak Arabic.”

The proponents of all these views have no proof for them, whether rational or transmitted. Rather, these are claims devoid of evidence. Allah, Glorious and Exalted, knows best and is most wise.

---

## Page 1624

He was asked:
“Does the Scale mean justice, or does it have two pans?”

He replied:
“The Scale” is that by which deeds are weighed; it is distinct from justice, as the Quran and Sunnah indicate. For example, Allah Most High says:
“Whoever’s scales are heavy,”
and:
“Whoever’s scales are light.”
He also says:
“We shall set up the scales of justice for the Day of Resurrection.”

In the two Sahīh collections, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is reported to have said:
“Two phrases are light on the tongue, heavy in the Scale, and beloved to the Most Merciful: ‘Glory and praise be to Allah; glory be to Allah, the Magnificent.’”

He also said of the two legs of ʿAbd Allāh ibn Masʿūd:
“They are heavier in the Scale than Mount Uhud.”

Al-Tirmidhī and others relate the hadith of the card, which al-Tirmidhī, al-Hākim, and others graded authentic. It concerns a man who is brought forward, and ninety-nine records are spread out for him, each extending as far as the eye can see. They are placed in one pan, and a card is brought for him bearing the testimony that none is worthy of worship except Allah. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:
“Then the records proved light, and the card heavy.”

This and similar reports make clear that deeds are weighed in scales that show whether good deeds outweigh evil deeds or vice versa. The Scale is thus the means by which justice becomes manifest.
