The purpose of weighing is justice, as with the scales of this world. As for the precise nature of those scales, it is on the same footing as the nature of all the other matters of the unseen about which we have been told.

---

## Page 1625

The shaykh said:

Regarding the children of unbelievers, the soundest view is:
“Allah knows best what they would have done.”

This was the answer given by the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, in an authentic hadith. A group of hadith scholars and others said that they are all in Hell. This has been cited as one of Ahmad’s explicit statements, but it is wrongly attributed to him. Another group asserted that they are all in Paradise. Abū al-Faraj ibn al-Jawzī and others adopted this view, citing as evidence a hadith that relates:

“In a dream, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, saw Abraham, the close friend of Allah, with the children of the believers beside him. He was asked, ‘O Messenger of Allah, and the children of the polytheists?’ He replied, ‘And the children of the polytheists.’”

The correct position is to say:
“Allah knows best what they would have done.”

We do not judge any particular child among them to be destined for either Paradise or Hell. Several hadiths relate:
“On the Day of Resurrection, in the gathering grounds, they will be given commands and prohibitions. Whoever obeys will enter Paradise, and whoever disobeys will enter Hell.”

This is the position that Abū al-Hasan al-Ashʿarī reported from Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamāʿah. Being subject to divine commands ends only upon entering the abode of recompense: Paradise or Hell.

---

## Page 1626

As for the gathering grounds on the Day of Resurrection, they will be tested there, just as they are tested in the barzakh (the intermediate state). One of them is asked:

“Who is your Lord? What is your religion? Who is your prophet?”

Allah Most High says in the verse:

“On the Day when a shin will be uncovered and they will be called to prostrate, but will not be able to.”

The hadith concerning Allah manifesting Himself to His servants at the gathering has been established through several chains of transmission in the authentic hadith collections. It relates what happens when it is said:

“‘Let every people follow what they used to worship.’ The polytheists follow their gods, while the believers remain. Then the Lord manifests Himself to them in a form other than the one they recognize, and they do not recognize Him. Then He manifests Himself to them in the form they recognize, and the believers prostrate to Him. The hypocrites’ backs, however, remain stiff like cattle’s horns: they wish to prostrate but cannot. He also mentioned His words in the verse: ‘On the Day when a shin will be uncovered and they will be called to prostrate, but will not be able to.’”

These matters are discussed at length elsewhere. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1627

He was asked about the disbelievers:
“Will they be brought to account on the Day of Resurrection or not?”

He replied:

This “question” has been disputed by later scholars among Ahmad’s followers and others. Those who said that they will not be brought to account include Abū Bakr Abd al-Azīz, Abū al-Hasan al-Tamīmī, al-Qādī Abū Ya'lā, and others. Those who said that they will be brought to account include Abū Hafs al-Barmakī, one of Ahmad’s followers, Abū Sulaymān al-Dimashqī, and Abū Tālib al-Makkī.

The decisive distinction is that “reckoning” may mean having their deeds presented to them and being rebuked for them, or it may mean weighing good deeds against evil deeds. If reckoning is understood in the first sense, there is no doubt that they will be brought to account in this respect. If it is understood in the second sense, and what is intended is that the disbelievers retain good deeds by which they deserve Paradise, this is plainly mistaken. But if what is meant is that their punishments differ in severity, then the punishment of those whose evil deeds are more numerous is greater than

---

## Page 1628

that of those whose evil deeds are fewer; those who have good deeds will have their punishment lightened, just as Abū Tālib’s punishment is lighter than Abū Lahab’s.

Allah Most High says:

“Those who disbelieved and hindered others from Allah’s path—We will increase their punishment upon punishment.”

And He says:

“Postponement of the sacred months is only an increase in disbelief.”

Hell has descending levels. Thus, if some disbelievers suffer more severe punishment than others—because their evil deeds are more numerous and their good deeds fewer—then the reckoning serves to determine the degrees of punishment, not to admit them to Paradise.

---

## Page 1629

Shaykh al-Islām Abū al-ʿAbbās Taqī al-Dīn ibn Taymiyyah—may Allah sanctify his soul—was asked:

“Does a believing servant become a disbeliever by committing a sin, or not?”

He replied:

He does not become a disbeliever merely by committing a sin. For the Quran, the Sunnah, and the consensus of the early generations establish that a person who commits unlawful sexual intercourse without having consummated a valid marriage is flogged, not put to death; one who drinks intoxicants is flogged; one who makes an unsubstantiated accusation of unlawful sexual intercourse is flogged; and a thief has his hand amputated. If they were disbelievers, they would be apostates, and putting them to death would be obligatory. This, however, contradicts the Quran, the Sunnah, and the consensus of the early generations.

---

## Page 1630

He was asked:

“A Muslim man performs a deed that merits having a palace built for him in Paradise and plantings made in his name. He then commits sins for which he deserves Hell. If he enters Hell, how can he be listed as being in Paradise while he is in Hell?”

He replied:

If he sincerely repents of his sins, Allah will forgive him and will not deprive him of what He had promised him; rather, He will grant it to him. If he does not repent, his good and evil deeds will be weighed. If his good deeds outweigh his evil deeds, he will be among those rewarded; if his evil deeds outweigh his good deeds, he will be among those punished.

The reward prepared for him is then nullified by the evil deeds that outweighed his good deeds. Likewise, if he commits evil deeds for which he deserves Hell, then afterwards performs good deeds, those evil deeds are erased. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1631

He was asked about intercession for “those who commit major sins” among the community of Muhammad, peace and blessings be upon him, and whether they will enter Paradise.

He replied:

The hadiths concerning intercession for “those who commit major sins” are firmly established and mass-transmitted from the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him. The righteous predecessors—the Companions and those who followed them in righteousness—and the leading scholars of the Muslims have agreed upon them. Only the adherents of religious innovation, such as the Khawārij, the Mu‘tazila, and others like them, have disputed this.

No one with an atom’s weight of faith in his heart will remain in Hell. Rather, they will all emerge from Hell and enter Paradise, and there will still be room left in Paradise. Allah will then create other beings for it and admit them to Paradise, as is established in an authentic hadith from the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him.

---

## Page 1632

He was also asked:

Will “the children of the believers” remain as they were when they died, or will they grow up and marry? Will the girls also marry?

The answer:

Praise be to Allah. When they enter Paradise, they will enter it just as adults do, in the form of their father Adam, sixty cubits tall and seven cubits wide. They will marry just as adults do. Any woman who dies without having married will be married in the Hereafter. Likewise, any man who dies will marry in the Hereafter. And Allah Most High knows best.

---

## Page 1633

The shaykh—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

Do the people of Paradise have offspring? And the “young boys”—are they the children of the people of Paradise? What is the fate of children? When the souls of the people of Paradise and Hell leave the body, are they in Paradise enjoying bliss, or do they remain in a particular place until Allah resurrects the body? What is the fate of a child born of fornication who dies: will he be among the people of the Heights or in Paradise? What is the correct view regarding the children of polytheists: are they among the people of Hell or the people of Paradise? Are the days in the Hereafter named as they are in this world, such as Saturday and Sunday?

He replied:

The “young boys” who go about attending to the people of Paradise are among the creatures of Paradise; they are not the children of people from this world. Rather, when children from this world enter Paradise, their physical form is perfected like that of the people of Paradise: in the form of Adam, thirty-three years of age and sixty cubits tall. It has also been reported that they are seven cubits wide.

The souls of the believers are in Paradise, and the souls of the disbelievers are in Hell. The souls of the believers enjoy bliss, while the souls of the disbelievers suffer punishment, until they are restored to their bodies.

---

## Page 1634

And if a “child born of fornication” believes and does righteous deeds, he will enter Paradise; otherwise, he will be recompensed for his deeds just as anyone else is. Recompense is based on deeds, not lineage. A child born of fornication is censured only because he is likely to commit evil deeds, as often happens, just as noble lineages are praised because they are likely to foster good deeds. Once a person’s deeds become evident, however, recompense is based on them. The most honored of all creatures in Allah’s sight are the most righteous.

As for “the children of polytheists,” the soundest answer concerning them is the answer of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, as related in the two Sahīh collections:

“Every child is born upon the natural disposition”—and the rest of the hadith.

He was asked, “O Messenger of Allah, what about the children of polytheists who die while still young?” He replied, “Allah knows best what they would have done.”

Thus, no particular child among them is judged to be destined for either Paradise or Hell. It is narrated:

“On the Day of Resurrection, they will be tested on the plains of the Resurrection. Whoever obeys Allah then will enter Paradise, and whoever disobeys will enter Hell.”

Authentic hadiths indicate that some of them will be in Paradise and some in Hell.

In Paradise there is neither sun nor moon, neither night nor day. Morning and evening, however, are known by a light that appears from the direction of the Throne. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1635

And he—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked about a man who was told that it had been reported from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“The inhabitants of Paradise eat, drink, and enjoy themselves, but neither urinate nor defecate.”

He replied, “Whoever eats and drinks urinates and defecates.” Then he was told, “There are birds in Paradise which, whenever one desires them, appear before him in whatever form he wishes, as food or otherwise.” He replied, “This is nonsense.” Does his denial of this make him a disbeliever and necessitate his execution, or not?

He answered:

Eating and drinking in Paradise are established by the Book of Allah, the Sunnah of His Messenger, and the consensus of the Muslims. This is something necessarily known to be part of the religion of Islam. Likewise, there are undoubtedly birds and palaces in Paradise, as described in the authentic hadiths established from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Similarly, the inhabitants of Paradise neither urinate, defecate, nor spit. No believer in Allah and His Messenger has disagreed with this. Anyone who disagrees is one of two kinds: either a disbeliever or a hypocrite. As for the disbeliever, the Jews and Christians deny eating, drinking, and sexual relations in

---

## Page 1636

Paradise, claiming that its inhabitants enjoy only delightful sounds and pleasant scents, together with the bliss experienced by their souls. Nevertheless, they acknowledge the resurrection of bodies together with souls, and their bliss and torment. Certain groups of disbelievers and others—Sabians, philosophers, and those who agree with them—acknowledge only the resurrection of souls, holding that bliss and torment affect souls alone. Other groups of disbelievers, polytheists, and others deny the resurrection altogether, acknowledging the resurrection of neither souls nor bodies. In His Book, through His Messenger, Allah Most High has explained the resurrection of souls and bodies and refuted the disbelievers and those who deny any part of it, with an explanation of the utmost completeness and perfection.

As for the hypocrites of this community who do not acknowledge the well-known wording of the Quran and Sunnah, they distort words from their proper meanings and say, “These are examples given to help us understand the spiritual resurrection.” These include the esoteric Qarmatians, whose doctrine combines the teachings of the Magians and the Sabians; the Sabian adherents of philosophy who profess Islam; and groups who resemble them: writers, physicians, theologians, or Sufis, such as the authors of Rasā’il Ikhwān al-Safā (Epistles of the Brethren of Purity) and others—or a hypocrite. All of these are disbelievers who must be put to death, by the consensus of the people of faith; for Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, has explained this fully and conclusively, leaving no excuse

---

## Page 1637

and this has been transmitted through numerous independent channels among his community, both its learned members and the general populace. One of the Jews debated him on a question of this kind, saying:

“O Muhammad, you say that the people of Paradise eat and drink, but whoever eats and drinks must relieve himself.” The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, replied, “Perspiration like musk.”

It is incumbent upon the ruler to put to death anyone who denies this, even if he outwardly affirms its wording. What, then, of someone who denies it all? Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1638

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

“Do the people of Paradise eat, drink, and have sexual relations with pleasure as in this world? Will these very bodies be resurrected? Is Jesus alive or dead? When he descends, will he judge according to the law of Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, according to his own earlier law, or will a new law be instituted for him?”

He—may Allah be pleased with him—answered:

The people of Paradise eat, drink, and have sexual relations, enjoying all of these, according to the consensus of the Muslims, as explicitly stated in the Quran and the Sunnah. The only people who deny this are those among the Jews and Christians who do so. These same bodies will be resurrected, as explicitly stated in the Quran and the Sunnah. Jesus is alive in heaven and has not yet died. When he descends from heaven, he will judge only according to the Quran and the Sunnah, not by anything that contradicts them. Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1639

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah sanctify his soul—said:

Section:

The greatest of the “prophets” after Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is “Abraham, Allah’s close friend,” as established in Sahīh Muslim through Anas’s report from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, that “he is the best of creation.”

The scholars likewise stated this. Among them was al-Rabī‘ ibn Khuthaym, who said: “I place no one above our Prophet, and after our Prophet, I place no one above Abraham.”

## Page 1640

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

“Is someone sinful for believing that those who are not prophets can attain the rank of the prophets, to the point that they feel secure from Allah’s devising?”

He replied:

Whoever believes that among the friends of Allah there are those who are not obliged to follow and obey the messengers is an unbeliever. He must be called upon to repent; if he repents, so be it; otherwise, he is to be put to death. This includes someone who believes that among the community of Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, there are those who have no need to follow him, just as al-Khidr had no need to follow Moses. Moses’s mission was not universal, unlike that of Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who was sent to everyone. Everyone is therefore obliged to follow his commands. If someone who believes that he is exempt from obeying him is an unbeliever, then what of someone who believes that he is superior to him, or that he can become his equal?

As for someone who believes that certain friends of Allah know that they are among the people of Paradise—just as a number of the Companions were given glad tidings of Paradise, and just as Allah may make known to some of His friends that they are among its people—he is not an unbeliever on that account. Even so, he must still fear Allah Most High. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1641

The Shaykh—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

“A man said that the prophets, peace and blessings be upon them, are protected from major sins but not minor sins. Another man declared him an unbeliever for saying this. Is the person who said this mistaken or correct? Has any of them maintained that the prophets are protected from sin without qualification? What is the correct position on this?”

He replied:

Praise be to Allah, Lord of the worlds. By agreement of the scholars of the religion, he is not an unbeliever. There is likewise no disagreement that this does not fall under those cases of insulting the prophets in which scholars dispute whether the speaker should be called upon to repent. Al-Qādī ‘Iyād and others like him explicitly stated this, despite their stringent positions on prophetic protection from sin and the punishment of those who insult the prophets. Nevertheless, they agree that such a statement does not fall under the rulings concerning insult and punishment, let alone render its speaker an unbeliever or a flagrant sinner.

Indeed, the view that the prophets are protected from major sins but not minor sins is held by the majority of Islamic scholars across all groups. It is even the position of most speculative theologians: “Abū al-Hasan al-Āmidī” mentioned that this is the view of most Ash‘arīs. It is also the position of most scholars of Quranic exegesis and hadith, and most jurists. Indeed, only reports consistent with this view have been transmitted from the early generations, the leading scholars, the Companions, the Successors, and their followers; no reports have been transmitted from them consistent with the view . . . (1)

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) A blank space of six lines.

---

## Page 1642

and in the early period, that view was reported only from the Rāfidīs, then from some Mu‘tazilīs, after which a group of later scholars agreed with them.

What is generally reported from the majority of scholars is that the prophets are not protected from being allowed to persist in minor sins, and that they are not allowed to persist in them. These scholars do not say that minor sins can never occur. Among the groups of this community, the first from whom unqualified protection from sin was reported, and its most emphatic proponents, were the Rāfidīs. They maintain that this protection extends even to matters arising from forgetfulness, inadvertence, or interpretation. They extend it to anyone they regard as an imam, and assert that ‘Alī and the Twelve are protected from sin.

Then the “Ismā‘īlīs,” who ruled Cairo and claimed to be caliphs descended from ‘Alī and Fātimah—though, according to the scholars, they were descendants of ‘Ubayd Allāh al-Qaddāh—and their followers asserted this same protection from sin for their imams and others like them. Yet they were as Abū Hāmid al-Ghazālī described them in the book he wrote to refute them:

“Outwardly, their doctrine is Rāfidism; inwardly, it is pure unbelief.”

“Al-Qādī Abū Ya‘lā” described their doctrines in his books, as did other Muslim scholars. These people and others like them are extremists who uphold protection from sin, and they may declare anyone who rejects it an unbeliever. These extremists are themselves unbelievers by the agreement of the Muslims. Thus, anyone who declares those who allow the possibility of prophets committing minor sins to be unbelievers resembles these Ismā‘īlīs, Nusayrīs, Rāfidīs, and Twelvers.

This is not the position of any of the followers of Abū Hanīfah, Mālik, or al-Shāfi‘ī, nor of the well-known speculative theologians who affiliate themselves with the Sunnah, such as the followers of

---

## Page 1643

Abū Muhammad ‘Abd Allāh ibn Sa‘īd ibn Kullāb, Abū al-Hasan ‘Alī ibn Ismā‘īl al-Ash‘arī, Abū ‘Abd Allāh Muhammad ibn Karrām, and others. Nor is it the position of the leading scholars of Quranic exegesis, hadith, or Sufism.

Declaring someone an unbeliever over this issue is not the position of these scholars. Anyone who makes such a declaration must therefore be called upon to repent. If he repents, well and good; otherwise, he must receive a punishment that deters him and others like him from doing such a thing—unless he manifests something that entails his own unbelief and heresy, in which case the ruling applicable to others of his kind applies to him.

Likewise, anyone who brands others as sinners for holding such a position must receive a discretionary punishment after the proof has been established against him, for this amounts to branding the majority of the leading scholars of Islam as sinners.

As for judging a position on this issue to be correct or mistaken, that is a matter discussed by well-versed Muslim scholars who adhere to the Sunnah and the community. A detailed treatment would require a lengthy exposition beyond the scope of this fatwa. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1644

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked about two men who disagreed concerning Allah’s Prophet, “Jesus son of Mary”—peace be upon him. One said, “Allah caused Jesus son of Mary to die, then raised him to Himself.” The other said, “Rather, He raised him to Himself alive.” Which view is correct? Was he raised bodily or in spirit, or was he not raised? What is the evidence for each view? And what is the interpretation of the Most High’s words:

“I will take you and raise you to Myself”?

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. Jesus—peace be upon him—is alive. It is established in an authentic hadith that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“The son of Mary will descend among you as a just judge and an equitable leader. He will break the cross, kill the swine, and abolish the jizyah tax.”

It is also established in an authentic hadith from him that:

“He will descend at the white minaret to the east of Damascus, and he will kill the Antichrist.”

When someone’s soul has departed from his body, his body does not descend from heaven. Rather, when he is brought back to life, he rises from his grave. As for the Most High’s words:

“I will take you and raise you to Myself and purify you of those who disbelieve,”

this is evidence that He did not mean death by this. Had He meant death, Jesus would have been like all other believers in this respect: Allah takes their souls and raises them to heaven. Thus, it is clear that there would be nothing distinctive about this.

The same applies to His words:

“and purify you of those who disbelieve.”

And if

---

## Page 1645

his soul had departed from his body, his body would have been in the earth, like the bodies of the rest of the prophets or of another prophet.

The Most High has said in another verse:

“They did not kill him, nor did they crucify him, but it was made to appear so to them. Those who differ concerning him are indeed in doubt about him. They have no knowledge of him, but merely follow conjecture. They certainly did not kill him. Rather, Allah raised him to Himself.”

His words here, “Rather, Allah raised him to Himself,” make clear that He raised him in body and soul, just as it is established in authentic hadith that he will descend in body and soul. For had his death been intended, He would have said: “They did not kill him, nor did they crucify him; rather, he died.”

[His words, “Rather, Allah raised him to Himself,” make clear that He raised him in body and soul, just as it is established in authentic hadith that he will descend in body and soul] (*).

For this reason, some scholars explained “I will take you” as meaning, “I will take hold of you”—that is, “I will take your soul and body.” One says, “I have received the amount due in full,” using either tawaffaytu or istawfaytu. The word tawaffī (taking in full) does not, in itself, indicate taking the soul without the body, nor taking both together, except with a separate contextual indication.

It may also refer to the taking that occurs during sleep, as in the Most High’s words:

“Allah takes the souls at the time of their death.”

And His words:

“It is He who takes you by night and knows what you have done by day.”

And His words:

“Until, when death comes to one of you, Our messengers take him.”

They have discussed how the Messiah was taken, as set out in the relevant discussion elsewhere. And Allah Most High knows best.

> **Footnotes:**
> (*) Shaykh Nāsir ibn Hamad al-Fahd stated (p. 33):
> “It appears that the passage in square brackets was inadvertently repeated.”

---

## Page 1646

The shaykh—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

“Is it authentically reported from the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, that Allah, Blessed and Most High, brought his parents back to life for him, so that they embraced Islam through him and then died again?”

He replied:

No hadith scholar has authenticated this report. Rather, those versed in hadith unanimously agree that it is a lie and a fabrication. Although Abū Bakr—meaning al-Khatīb—transmitted a report to this effect in his book al-Sābiq wa-l-Lāhiq (Earlier and Later Transmitters), Abū al-Qāsim al-Suhaylī mentioned it in Sharh al-Sīra (Commentary on the Prophet’s Biography) with a chain of transmission containing unknown narrators, and Abū ‘Abd Allāh al-Qurtubī mentioned it in al-Tadhkira (The Reminder) and similar works, there is no disagreement among those versed in hadith that it is one of the most obvious fabrications, as scholars have explicitly stated.

It is not found in the authoritative hadith books: neither in the Sahīh collections, nor in the Sunan or Musnad collections, nor in other well-known hadith works. Nor have the authors of works on the Prophet’s military campaigns and Quranic exegesis mentioned it, even though they may transmit weak reports alongside authentic ones.

For its obvious falsehood cannot escape anyone committed to religion. Had such an event occurred, there would have been every incentive and reason to transmit it, for it would have been among the greatest departures from the ordinary course of events in two respects:

---

## Page 1647

in bringing the dead back to life, and in embracing faith after death. Such an event would have deserved to be transmitted more than other events. Since no trustworthy narrator reported it, it is known to be a lie.

Al-Khatīb al-Baghdādī’s purpose in his book al-Sābiq wa-l-Lāhiq (Earlier and Later Transmitters) is to mention the earlier and later hadith transmitters who narrated from the same person, regardless of whether what they narrated was true or false. Ibn Shāhīn transmits good and bad material alike. Al-Suhaylī mentioned this report only with a chain containing unknown narrators. Moreover, it contradicts the Quran, the authentic Sunnah, and scholarly consensus.

Allah Most High says:

“Allah only accepts the repentance of those who do evil in ignorance and then repent soon afterwards. It is those to whom Allah will turn in forgiveness, and Allah is ever Knowing and Wise.”

“But repentance is not for those who continue to do evil deeds until, when death comes to one of them, he says, ‘Indeed, I repent now,’ nor for those who die while they are disbelievers.”

Allah Most High thus makes clear that there is no repentance for one who dies as a disbeliever.

He Most High also says:

“But their faith did not benefit them once they saw Our punishment. That is Allah’s established way with His servants, and there the disbelievers lost.”

He thus declares that His established way with His servants is that faith does not benefit them once they have seen His punishment. How, then, could it benefit them after death? Other scriptural texts convey the same meaning.

In Sahīh Muslim, it is reported:

“A man said to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, ‘Where is my father?’ He replied, ‘Your father is in the Fire.’ When the man turned away, he called him back and said, ‘My father and your father are in the Fire.’”

In Sahīh Muslim, it is also reported that he said:

“I asked my Lord for permission to visit my mother’s grave

---

## Page 1648

and He granted me permission. I asked Him for permission to seek forgiveness for her, but He did not grant me permission. So visit graves, for they remind you of the Hereafter.”

In the hadith recorded in al-Musnad and elsewhere, he said:

“My mother is with your mother in the Fire.”

If it is said, “This occurred in the year of the conquest, whereas their revival took place afterwards, during the Farewell Pilgrimage”—which is why those who mentioned it did so, and which was the explanation offered by the author of al-Tadhkira (The Reminder)—this is false for several reasons:

First: Reports about what has happened or will happen are not subject to abrogation, such as His statement concerning Abū Lahab:

“He will burn in a Fire of blazing flame.”

And His statement concerning al-Walīd:

“I shall burden him with a steep ascent.”

The same applies to the statements, “My father and your father are in the Fire,” and, “My mother and your mother are in the Fire.” This is not a report about a Fire from which its inmates will emerge, as those guilty of major sins do. Had that been the case, seeking forgiveness for them would have been permissible. If Allah had known beforehand that they would believe, He would not have forbidden him to do so, for deeds are judged by their endings. Whoever dies as a believer will be forgiven by Allah, so seeking forgiveness for such a person would not be prohibited.

Second: The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, visited his mother’s grave in the year of the conquest because it lay along his route, at al-Hajūn near Mecca. His father, however, was not there, and he did not visit him, since he was buried in Syria, away from his route. How, then, can it be claimed that his father was brought back to life for him?

Third: Had they both been believers whose faith benefited them, they would have been more deserving of renown and remembrance than his two uncles, Hamza and al-ʿAbbās. This is even more far-fetched than the claim made by the ignorant among the Rāfida and others like them

---

## Page 1649

that Abū Tālib embraced faith. They cite the weak hadith in “al-Sīra” (The Prophetic Biography), which states that he uttered indistinct words at the time of his death. Had al-ʿAbbās stated that he had embraced faith, he would not have said to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him:

“Your uncle, that misguided old man, used to help you. Have you done anything to benefit him?”

He replied:

“I found him deep in the Fire, so I interceded for him until he came to be in a shallow part of the Fire. On his feet are two sandals of fire that cause his brain to boil. Were it not for me, he would be in the lowest depth of the Fire.”

This claim is false and contradicts what is recorded in the Sahīh and elsewhere: the last thing he said was that he followed the religion of ʿAbd al-Muttalib, and al-ʿAbbās did not witness his death. Moreover, had that claim been true, Abū Tālib would have been more deserving of renown than Hamza and al-ʿAbbās. It is widely known throughout the Muslim community, through numerous reports handed down from earlier to later generations, that neither Abū Tālib nor the Prophet’s parents are mentioned among his believing family members, such as Hamza, al-ʿAbbās, ʿAlī, Fātima, al-Hasan and al-Husayn—may Allah be pleased with them. This is among the clearest proofs that the claim is a lie.

(Fourth: Allah Most High said, in the verse:

“You have had an excellent example in Abraham and those with him, when they said to their people, ‘We disavow you’”—up to His words—“‘I will certainly seek forgiveness for you, but I cannot avail you against Allah at all.’”

Allah Most High also said:

“Abraham’s seeking forgiveness for his father was only because of a promise he had made to him. But when it became clear to him that he was an enemy of Allah, he disowned him.”

The command, therefore, is to follow the example of Abraham and those with him, except in Abraham’s promise to seek forgiveness for his father. It is also stated that, when it became clear to him that his father was an enemy of Allah, he disowned him. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1650

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked about these hadiths:

“The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, saw Moses, peace be upon him, praying in his grave; he also saw him circumambulating the House and saw him in heaven. The same is reported of some other prophets. When someone dies, does he continue to perform deeds, even though the hadith states that his deeds come to an end? Does he benefit from this prayer and circumambulation? Did the Prophet see the prophets in these places in their bodies or in their souls?”

He replied:

Praise be to Allah, Lord of all worlds. As for seeing Moses, peace be upon him, circumambulating the House, this was a dream, not something that occurred on the night of the Ascension. This is explicitly explained in the report; he likewise saw the Messiah and the False Messiah.

As for seeing Moses and the other prophets in heaven on the night of the Ascension—when he saw Adam in the lowest heaven, John and Jesus in the second, Joseph in the third, Idrīs in the fourth, Aaron in the fifth, Moses in the sixth, and Abraham in the seventh, or in the reverse order—he saw their souls taking the forms of their bodies.

Some have said that perhaps he saw the actual bodies buried in the graves. This view is without foundation.

---

## Page 1651

But Jesus ascended to heaven in both soul and body, and the same has been said of Idrīs. Abraham, Moses, and the others, however, are buried in the earth. The Messiah—may Allah’s peace and blessings be upon him and all the other prophets—will certainly descend to earth at the white minaret east of Damascus. He will kill the False Messiah, break the cross, and kill swine, as established in authentic hadiths. This is why he was in the second heaven, although he is superior to Joseph, Idrīs, and Aaron: unlike the others, he intends to descend to earth before the Day of Resurrection.

Adam was in the lowest heaven because the souls of his descendants are presented to him: the souls of the blessed (whereas the gates of heaven will not be opened to the wretched, nor will they enter Paradise until a camel passes through the eye of a needle). He must therefore be close to them when they are presented to him.

As for the Prophet’s seeing Moses standing in prayer in his grave and also seeing him in heaven, there is no contradiction between the two. Souls are like angels in this respect: in a single instant, they ascend and descend as an angel does; they are not like the body in this regard. Elsewhere, I have discussed at length the states of souls after their separation from their bodies, mentioning some of the hadiths, transmitted reports, and evidence concerning this.

---

## Page 1652

This prayer and similar acts bring enjoyment and delight to the deceased, just as the people of Paradise delight in glorifying Allah. They are inspired to glorify Him just as people in this world are inspired to breathe. This is not an act subject to religious responsibility for which a separate reward is sought; rather, the act itself is part of the bliss in which souls find enjoyment and delight.

The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“When a son of Adam dies, his deeds come to an end except for three: ongoing charity, knowledge from which others benefit, and a righteous child who prays for him.”

By this, he meant deeds for which one receives a reward, not the acts in which one finds enjoyment. The people of Paradise delight in looking upon Allah, remembering Him, glorifying Him, and reciting the Quran. The reciter of the Quran will be told: “Recite and ascend, and recite with measured care as you used to recite in this world, for your station will be at the last verse you recite.”

They also delight in addressing their Lord and speaking intimately with Him. Although these things are deeds that earn a reward in this world, in the Hereafter they are acts that bring greater delight to the one performing them than eating, drinking, or sexual intercourse. These, too, are all acts. In this world, eating, drinking, and sexual intercourse are among the things one is enjoined to do and is rewarded for when accompanied by a righteous intention; in the Hereafter, they are themselves the reward in which one finds delight. And Allah knows best.

This is as much as this sheet could accommodate, for these matters call for lengthy discussion.

---

## Page 1653

The shaykh—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

“Which of the sons of Abraham, Allah’s close friend—peace be upon him—was to be sacrificed: Ishmael or Isaac?”

He replied:

All praise is due to Allah, Lord of the worlds. Scholars hold two well-known views on this question, each reported from a group of the early generations. Abū Ya‘lā mentioned two opinions transmitted from Ahmad on the matter and supported the view that it was Isaac, following Abū Bakr ‘Abd al-‘Azīz, who in turn followed Muhammad ibn Jarīr.

For this reason, Abū al-Faraj ibn al-Jawzī states that Ahmad’s followers support the view that it was Isaac. In fact, it is only these two and those who follow them who support it. This view is also attributed to Mālik himself, though a group of his followers disagreed with him.

Al-Sharīf Abū ‘Alī ibn Abī Mūsā stated that the correct position within Ahmad’s school is that it was Ishmael. This is what ‘Abd Allāh ibn Ahmad reported from his father, saying: “My father’s position was that it was Ishmael.”

In short, the disagreement over this question is well known. Nevertheless, what must be affirmed with certainty is that it was Ishmael. This is what the Quran, the Sunnah, and the well-known proofs establish. It is also what the Torah in the possession of the People of the Book indicates.

---

## Page 1654

Moreover, it states that He said to Abraham:

“Sacrifice your son, your only son.”

In another translation: “Your firstborn.” Ishmael was his only son and his firstborn, as Muslims and the People of the Book agree. However, the People of the Book altered the text by adding Isaac’s name. Those who took this from them transmitted it, and the belief that it was Isaac spread among some Muslims. Its origin lies in the textual distortion by the People of the Book.

Further evidence that it was Ishmael is the account of the son to be sacrificed in Surah al-Sāffāt. Allah Most High says:

“So We gave him good tidings of a forbearing boy.”

These good tidings contained three assurances: that the child would be a boy, that he would reach puberty, and that he would be forbearing. What forbearance could be greater than his when his father told him of the sacrifice and he replied:

“You will find me, if Allah wills, among the steadfast”?

It has been said that no quality is used less frequently by Allah to describe the prophets than forbearance, because it is so rarely found. He did describe Abraham with it in His words:

“Indeed, Abraham was tender-hearted and forbearing.”

“Indeed, Abraham was forbearing, tender-hearted, and ever turning to Allah.”

For this event attested to the forbearance of them both:

“When he was old enough to work alongside him, he said, ‘My son, I have seen in a dream that I am sacrificing you, so consider what you think.’ He replied, ‘My father, do as you are commanded. You will find me, if Allah wills, among the steadfast.’”

—up to His words—

“And We ransomed him with a great sacrifice.”

“And We left this tribute to him among later generations.”

“Peace be upon Abraham.”

“Thus do We reward those who do good.”

“Indeed, he was one of Our believing servants.”

“And We gave him good tidings of Isaac, a prophet among the righteous.”

“And We blessed him and Isaac. Among their descendants are those who do good and those who clearly wrong themselves.”

This account indicates that it was Ishmael in several ways:

- (First: He gave him good tidings of the son to be sacrificed and related his story first. Once He had completed that account, He said:

---

## Page 1655

“And We gave him good tidings of Isaac, a prophet among the righteous.”

“And We blessed him and Isaac.”

He thus made clear that these were two announcements: good tidings of the son to be sacrificed, and a second announcement concerning Isaac. This is plain.

(Second: He relates the account of the son to be sacrificed nowhere else in the Quran. In the other passages, He mentions only the good tidings of Isaac, as in Surah Hūd, where He Most High says:

“And his wife was standing, and she laughed. Then We gave her good tidings of Isaac, and after Isaac, Jacob.”

Had Isaac been the son to be sacrificed, this would have contradicted the promise concerning Jacob.

He Most High also says:

“He felt afraid of them. They said, ‘Do not fear,’ and gave him good tidings of a learned boy.”

“Then his wife came forward with a cry, struck her face, and said, ‘A barren old woman!’”

He Most High says in Surah al-Hijr:

“They said, ‘Do not be afraid. We bring you good tidings of a learned boy.’”

“He said, ‘Do you bring me good tidings when old age has overtaken me? What good tidings, then, do you bring?’”

“They said, ‘We bring you good tidings in truth, so do not be among those who despair.’”

He did not mention that this was the son to be sacrificed. Then, when He mentioned both announcements together—the good tidings of the son to be sacrificed, followed by the good tidings of Isaac—this provided evidence that Isaac was not the son to be sacrificed. This is further supported by His mention of His gift of Isaac and Jacob to Abraham—may Allah have mercy on him—in His words:

“And We granted him Isaac, and Jacob as an additional gift, and made each of them righteous.”

And His words:

“And We granted him Isaac and Jacob, and placed prophethood and Scripture among his descendants. We gave him his reward in this world, and in the Hereafter he will surely be among the righteous.”

Allah did not mention the son to be sacrificed.

The third point: He described the son to be sacrificed as a forbearing boy, whereas elsewhere, when mentioning the good tidings of Isaac, He spoke of a learned boy. There must be wisdom in this distinction,

---

## Page 1656

and this further supports the association of the two qualities. Forbearance is suited to patience, which was the character of the son to be sacrificed. Ishmael is described as patient in His words, Most High:

“And Ishmael, Idrīs, and Dhū al-Kifl—all were among the patient.”

This, too, is a third point, for He relates that the son to be sacrificed said:

“O my father, do what you are commanded. You will find me, if Allah wills, among the patient.”

Allah described Ishmael as being among the patient. Allah Most High also described Ishmael as true to his promise in His words:

“Indeed, he was true to his promise.”

This is because he promised his father that he would patiently endure being sacrificed, and he fulfilled that promise.

The fourth point:

The good tidings of Isaac were miraculous, because the elderly woman was barren. Thus Allah’s intimate friend—peace be upon him—said:

“Do you bring me good tidings when old age has overtaken me? What good tidings, then, do you bring?”

His wife said:

“Shall I bear a child when I am an old woman and this husband of mine is an old man?”

It has already been explained that the good tidings of Isaac came in old age, and that they were given to both Abraham and his wife.

The good tidings of the son to be sacrificed, however, were given to Abraham—peace be upon him—and it was he, rather than the mother who had been given good tidings of the child, who was tested by being commanded to sacrifice him. This accords with what is transmitted from the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—and his Companions in the Sahīh and elsewhere: when Hagar gave birth to Ishmael, Sarah became jealous, so Abraham went

---

## Page 1657

with Ishmael and his mother to Mecca, and there he was commanded to sacrifice him. This further supports the view that this son, rather than the other, was the one to be sacrificed.

Another indication that the son to be sacrificed was not Isaac is that Allah Most High said:

“So We gave her good tidings of Isaac, and after Isaac, Jacob.”

How, then, could He command that Isaac be sacrificed after that? The good tidings of Jacob entail that Isaac would live and that Jacob would be born to him. There is no disagreement among people that the incident of the intended sacrifice took place before Jacob’s birth. Indeed, Jacob was born only after Abraham’s death—peace be upon him—whereas the incident of the intended sacrifice undoubtedly took place during Abraham’s lifetime.

Another indication of this is that the incident of the intended sacrifice took place in Mecca:

“When the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—conquered Mecca, the ram’s two horns were in the Kaaba. The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said to its custodian: ‘I command you to cover the ram’s horns, for there should be nothing in the direction of prayer that distracts the worshipper.’”

For this reason, Minā was made a site for religious rites from the time of Abraham and Ishmael—peace be upon them both—and, as the Quran explicitly states, it was they who built the House. No one, whether from the People of the Book or anyone else, has reported that Isaac went to Mecca. Yet some believers among the People of the Book claim that the incident of the sacrifice took place in Greater Syria. This is a fabrication. For if this had taken place on one of the mountains of Greater Syria, people would have known that

---

## Page 1658

mountain, and it might have been made a site for religious rites, just as the mosque built by Abraham and the sacred sites around it were.

There is further evidence supporting what we have stated on this issue, as well as questions raised by scholars such as Ibn Jarīr, Judge Abū Yaʿlā, and al-Suhaylī. However, there is not enough space here to set out these questions and answer them.

Allah, Mighty and Majestic, knows best. All praise is due to Allah, Lord of the worlds. May Allah send blessings and peace upon Muhammad, his family, and his Companions.

---

## Page 1659

He was also asked—may Allah have mercy on him—about al-Khidr and Elijah: “Have their lives been prolonged? Explain this to us, may Allah Most High have mercy on you.”

He replied:

They are not among the living, nor have their lives been prolonged (*).

Ibrāhīm al-Harbī asked Ahmad ibn Hanbal about the prolonged lives of al-Khidr and Elijah, and the claim that they remain alive, see, and have reports transmitted from them. Imam Ahmad said: “Whoever refers to an absent person has not acted fairly; only a devil put this idea forward.”

Al-Bukhārī was asked about al-Khidr and Elijah: “Are they among the living?” He replied: “How could that be, when the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: ‘At the end of a hundred years, none of those now on the face of the earth will remain’?”

Abū al-Faraj Ibn al-Jawzī said: His saying, exalted is He:

“We did not grant immortality to any human before you.”

And they are not among the living. Allah knows best.

> **Footnotes:**
> (*) Shaykh Nāsir ibn Hamad al-Fahd said (p. 34):
> The quotation from Ibn al-Jawzī at the end of this fatwa is defective, as the context indicates—and Allah knows best. It appears that some text has been omitted here.
> 
> Ibn al-Jawzī, may Allah have mercy on him, wrote a book on this subject entitled Ujālat al-Muntazir fī Sharh Hāl al-Khidr (A Brief Explanation of al-Khidr’s State for the Awaiting Reader). Ibn Kathīr, may Allah have mercy on him, mentioned it in his history (2:265), saying:
> 
> “As for those who held that he had died, they include al-Bukhārī, Ibrāhīm al-Harbī, Abū al-Husayn ibn al-Munādī, and Shaykh Abū al-Faraj ibn al-Jawzī, who defended this position and wrote a book entitled Ujālat al-Muntazir fī Sharh Hāl al-Khidr. Many arguments may be advanced in support of their position, including Allah Most High’s statement: ‘We did not grant immortality to any human before you.’ Thus, if al-Khidr is human, he necessarily falls within this general statement. The presumption is that no exception exists until one is established. No evidence establishing an exception has been cited from an authority protected from error whose report must be accepted.”
> 
> Thus, perhaps Ibn al-Jawzī’s concluding comments on the verse, or something similar, are what has been omitted from the fatwa. Allah Most High knows best.

## Page 1660

The shaykh—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

“Was al-Khidr, peace be upon him, a prophet or a friend of Allah? Is he still alive? If he is alive, what do you say about the saying attributed to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

‘If he were alive, he would have visited me.’

Is this hadith authentic or not?”

He replied:

As for his prophethood, after the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was sent, neither he nor any other person received revelation. As for the period before the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was sent, there is disagreement over whether al-Khidr was a prophet (*).

Those who say that he was a prophet do not say that he was stripped of prophethood. Rather, they say that, like Elijah, he is a prophet, but that he has received no revelation during these times. The withholding of revelation from him for a particular period does not negate his actual prophethood, just as revelation might be interrupted for the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, during his prophetic mission.

Most scholars hold that he was not a prophet, although much of the prophethood among those before us closely resembles the divinely bestowed gifts and perfection found within this community. Nevertheless, every one of the prophets is superior to every

>

>

>

>

He would have been in Mecca and Medina. Nor would he have concealed himself from the best community brought forth for humankind…” He went on to say: “If al-Khidr remains perpetually alive, how is it that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant them peace, never mentioned this or informed his community of it, nor did his rightly guided caliphs?!”

---

## Page 1661

one of those foremost in truthfulness, as the Quran ranks them, and as it is reported that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The sun has neither risen nor set upon anyone superior to Abu Bakr al-Siddīq, apart from the prophets and messengers.”

It is also reported that he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“A man would indeed hear the sound and thereby be a prophet.”

Within this community, however, there are people who hear it and see the light without being prophets. Whatever such a person sees and hears must be assessed against what Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, brought. If it agrees with that, it is true. If it contradicts it, he knows with certainty that what has come from Allah is sure knowledge, untainted by doubt, and needs no corroboration through agreement with anything else.

As for whether al-Khidr is alive: he is alive. The hadith mentioned has no basis, and no chain of transmission is known for it. Rather, what is reported in Musnad al-Shāfi‘ī and elsewhere is that he met the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Anyone who says that he did not meet the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, has spoken without knowledge, for this is a matter that cannot be known exhaustively.

Whoever argues that he has died on the basis of the Prophet’s statement, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Consider this night of yours: at the end of a hundred years, none of those who are on the face of the earth today will remain upon it.”

This provides no proof, for it is possible that al-Khidr was on the face of the earth at that time. Furthermore, the correct view is that the Dajjāl—and likewise al-Jassāsah—was alive and in existence

---

## Page 1662

during the time of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. He remains alive to this day and has not yet emerged; he was on one of the islands of the sea.

Whatever answer is given concerning him also applies to al-Khidr: namely, that the word “earth” may not have been included in this report, or that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, may have meant ordinary, known human beings. Those whose circumstances depart from the ordinary would then fall outside its general scope, just as the jinn do, even if the wording encompasses both jinn and human beings. Exceptions of this kind to such general wording are frequent and customary. And Allah knows best.

## Page 1663

He was also asked:

“Does the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, know the time of the Hour?”

He replied:

As for the report in question—that he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, “knows the time of the Hour”—it has no basis. There is no statement whatsoever from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, specifying the time of the Hour. Rather, Allah Most High has said:

“They ask you about the Hour: when will it take place? Say, ‘Knowledge of it rests only with my Lord. None but He will reveal it at its appointed time.’ It weighs heavily upon the heavens and the earth.”

That is, its time is hidden from the inhabitants of the heavens and the earth. Allah Most High also said to Moses:

“Indeed, the Hour is coming; I almost conceal it.”

Ibn ‘Abbās and others said: “I almost conceal it from Myself; how, then, could it be made known?”

In the two Sahīh collections, on the authority of Abū Hurayrah, and also in Muslim’s Sahīh, on the authority of ‘Umar, it is reported:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was asked, ‘When is the Hour?’ He replied, ‘The one being questioned about it knows no more than the questioner.’”

Thus, he stated that he knew no more about it than the questioner. The questioner appeared in the form of a Bedouin, and he did not know that it was Gabriel until after he had departed. When he answered him, he thought he was simply a Bedouin. So if the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said of himself that he knew no more about the Hour than

---

## Page 1664

a Bedouin, how can anyone else claim to know its appointed time? The Quran and Sunnah have only told us of its signs. These are many: some have already appeared, while others have yet to come.

As for those who have spoken about its precise time—such as the man who wrote a book entitled “Al-Durr al-munazzam fī ma‘rifat al-a‘zam” (The Ordered Pearl on Knowledge of the Greatest), mentioning ten indications by which he specified its time; those who discussed this on the basis of the letters of the alphabet; the one who discussed it in “‘Anqā’ mughrib”; and others like them—although they may enjoy an exalted standing among their followers, most of them are liars and fabricators. It has become clear to them in many ways that they speak without knowledge, even if they claim spiritual unveiling and knowledge of hidden mysteries in these matters. Allah Most High has said:

“Say, ‘My Lord has only forbidden shameful deeds, whether committed openly or in secret, sin, unjust aggression, associating with Allah that for which He has sent down no authority, and saying about Allah what you do not know.’”

---

## Page 1665

Shaykh al-Islām was asked:
“Who is superior: the righteous among the children of Adam or the angels?”

He replied that righteous human beings are superior in terms of their ultimate perfection, whereas the angels are superior in terms of their initial state. The angels are now in the highest company, free from what besets the children of Adam and wholly absorbed in worshipping the Lord. There is no doubt that their present state is more perfect than that of human beings. On the Day of Resurrection, however, after entering Paradise, righteous human beings will attain a more perfect state than that of the angels.

Ibn al-Qayyim said:
“This distinction makes clear the underlying basis of superiority, reconciles the evidence of both sides, and brings them into agreement while acknowledging the truth held by each (*).”

> **Footnotes:**
> (*) Shaykh Nāsir ibn Hamad al-Fahd (p. 37) said:

## Page 1666

He was also asked:
“Are the obedient among the community of Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, superior to the angels?”

He replied:
It is established that ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr said:

“The angels said, ‘Our Lord, You have allowed the children of Adam to eat, drink and enjoy themselves in this world, so grant us the Hereafter as You have granted them this world.’ He said, ‘I will not do so.’ They repeated their request to Him, and He said, ‘I will not do so.’ Then they repeated their request to Him twice or three times, and He said, ‘By My might, I will not make the righteous descendants of the one whom I created with My two hands like those to whom I said, “Be,” and they came into being.’”

ʿUthmān ibn Saʿīd al-Dārimī mentioned this report, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ahmad narrated it in his book al-Sunan (*) as a report attributed to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, with a disconnected chain of transmission.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Salām also said:
“Allah has created no being more honoured in His sight than Muhammad.” He was asked, “Not even Gabriel or Michael?” He replied to the questioner, “Do you know what Gabriel and Michael are? Gabriel and Michael are but created beings subject to His command, like the sun and the moon. Allah has created no being more honoured in His sight than Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.”

I know of nothing reported from any of the Companions that contradicts this. This is the well-known position among those who affiliate themselves with the Sunnah, including the followers of the four imams and others: that the prophets and the friends of Allah are superior to the angels. We have devoted a separate work to this question, in which we presented the evidence on both sides.

> **Footnotes:**
> (*) Shaykh Nāsir ibn Hamad al-Fahd (p. 256) said: “Perhaps it should read: al-Sunnah.”

## Page 1667

The shaykh—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked about Adam:

“When Allah created him, breathed into him of His spirit, and made His angels prostrate to him, did the angels of heaven and earth prostrate, or only the angels of earth? Were Gabriel and Michael among those who prostrated? Was the garden in which he dwelt the Paradise of Eternity, which already exists, or a garden on earth that Allah created for him? When he was sent down, was he sent down from heaven to earth, or from one land to another, as with the Children of Israel?”

He replied:

All praise is due to Allah. Rather, He made all the angels prostrate to him, as the Quran expressly states in His words, exalted is He:

“So the angels prostrated, all of them together.”

These are three expressions affirming universality and exhaustive inclusion. His words “the angels” encompass all the angels, for a collective noun made definite by the definite article denotes universality. Thus, in His words, “Lord of the angels and the Spirit,” He is the Lord of all the angels.

The second expression is “all of them,” which is among the strongest expressions of universality.

The third is His word “together,” which reinforces that universality. Whoever says that not all the angels prostrated to him, but only the angels of earth, has rejected the Quran

---

## Page 1668

with lies and slander. This view and others like it are not the views of Muslims, Jews, or Christians. Rather, they are the views of heretical adherents of philosophy who identify “angels” with the soul’s good faculties and “devils” with its evil faculties. They interpret the angels’ prostration as the faculties’ obedience to reason, and the devils’ refusal as the evil faculties’ disobedience to reason. Such notions are advanced by the authors of the “Epistles of the Brethren of Purity” and others like them among the Qarmatian esotericists, as well as misguided speculative theologians and devotees who follow their path. Similar views may also be found among statements attributed to Quran commentators without any reliable chain of transmission.

The position of Muslims, Jews, and Christians is what Allah has related in the Quran. None of the devils were among those commanded to prostrate; their father, Iblīs, however, was commanded to do so, but refused and disobeyed. Some people counted him among the angels because he was included in the command to prostrate. Others counted him among the jinn because he has a tribe and offspring, and because he was created from fire, whereas the angels were created from light.

The correct understanding is that he was one of them in respect of his form, but not in respect of his origin or his likeness. Not one of the angels failed to prostrate to Adam: neither Gabriel nor Michael nor anyone else. As for the contrary views mentioned by the author of Khawāss al-Qur’ān and others like him, their statements are false. We have explained their unsoundness and falsity in an extensive discussion for which this is not the place. This is among the proofs cited by Ahl al-Sunnah that Adam and others among the prophets and allies of Allah

---

## Page 1669

are superior to all the angels, because Allah commanded the angels to prostrate before Adam in his honour. Thus Iblīs said:

“Do You see this one whom You have honoured above me?”

This indicates that Adam was honoured above those who prostrated before him.

According to the early generations of the Muslim community and Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamā‘ah, the “Paradise” in which Allah settled Adam and his wife is the everlasting Paradise. Whoever says that it was a garden on earth, in India, Jeddah, or elsewhere, belongs to the adherents of philosophy and the heretics, or to their fellows among the speculative theologians who introduce religious innovations. This view is held by some adherents of philosophy and some Mu‘tazilites. The Quran and the Sunnah refute it, and the early generations of the Muslim community and its leading scholars agree that it is false.

Allah Most High said:

“And when We said to the angels, ‘Prostrate before Adam,’ they prostrated, except Iblīs. He refused and was arrogant, and was among the disbelievers.”

“And We said, ‘O Adam, dwell, you and your wife, in Paradise,’”

up to His words:

“And We said, ‘Descend, as enemies to one another. On earth you will have a place of settlement and enjoyment for a time.’”

He thus tells us that He, glorified be He, commanded them to descend, and that they were enemies to one another. He then said:

“On earth you will have a place of settlement and enjoyment for a time.”

This shows that they were not on earth, but were made to descend to it. Had they been on earth and moved to another land—as the people of Moses moved from one land to another—their place of settlement and enjoyment for a time would have been on earth both before and after their descent. Likewise, in Sūrat al-A‘rāf, when Iblīs said:

“I am better than him. You created me from fire and created him from clay,”

Allah said:

“Then descend from it! It is not for you to be arrogant there.”

---

## Page 1670

Thus His words:

“Then descend from it! It is not for you to be arrogant there.”

show that heaven and Paradise are singled out for this ruling. The pronoun in His words “from it” refers to something known, though not explicitly mentioned. This differs from His words:

“Go down to a town, and you will have what you have asked for.”

For there He did not mention the place in which they were made to descend, and here He said, “Descend,” because descent is from a higher place to a lower one. The Children of Israel were near the land of al-Sarāh, facing al-Sarāh, which overlooked the town to which they were descending. Someone who goes down from a mountain into a valley is said to have descended.

(Furthermore, the Children of Israel were travelling from place to place. When a traveller reaches a town, he is said to have “alighted there,” because he ordinarily rides during his journey and dismounts from his animals upon arrival. Thus one says, “The army alighted in such-and-such a land,” or “The caravan alighted in such-and-such a land,” because they dismounted from their animals. The word for alighting is like the word for descending: “descended” is used only for movement from a higher place to a lower one.

And His words in the two verses:

“Our Lord, we have wronged ourselves, and if You do not forgive us and have mercy upon us, we will surely be among the losers.”

“He said, ‘Descend.’”

His statement here, following His words:

“Descend, as enemies to one another. On earth you will have a place of settlement and enjoyment for a time.”

makes clear that they descended to the earth from somewhere other than it. And His words:

“There you will live, there you will die, and from there you will be brought forth.”

are evidence that, before this, they were not in a place where they would live, die, and be brought forth. They came to that place only when they were made to descend from Paradise.

---

## Page 1671

There are many scriptural texts to this effect, as well as statements by the early generations and the imams.

In the two Sahīh collections, Abū Hurayrah, may Allah be pleased with him, reports that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Adam and Moses debated. Moses said, ‘Adam, you are the father of mankind. Allah created you with His Hand, breathed into you of His spirit, and made His angels prostrate before you. Why, then, did you cause us and your descendants to leave Paradise?’

Adam replied, ‘You are Moses, whom Allah chose to bear His message and to whom He spoke. Do you find in the Torah: “Adam disobeyed his Lord and went astray”?’

He said, ‘Yes.’

Adam said, ‘Why, then, do you blame me for something Allah decreed for me before I was created?’”

The Prophet said, “Thus Adam prevailed over Moses in the argument.”

Moses blamed Adam only for the hardship and misery that befell him and his descendants through leaving Paradise. Had it merely been a garden on earth, another earthly garden could have replaced it.

(Adam, peace be upon him, appealed to divine decree because a servant is commanded to bear patiently the calamities Allah has decreed, and to repent to Him and seek His forgiveness for sins and faults. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1672

Shaykh al-Islām said:

Chapter on the well-known question of “The Relative Merit of Angels and Humans”

He said:

The discussion may concern either the relative merit of angels and humans as two kinds of beings, or the relative merit of the righteous among angels and humans.

As for the first question—“Which are superior: angels or humans?”—it admits of four interpretations:

(*)
The first interpretation is to ask: Is every individual human superior to every individual angel?

No rational person would say this. Among humans are disbelievers, the wicked, the ignorant, the arrogant, and believers. Among them are those who resemble beasts and grazing livestock. Indeed, livestock are better off than these people, as the Quran explicitly states in several places, such as His saying, exalted is He:

“Indeed, the worst of living creatures in the sight of Allah are the deaf and dumb who do not use reason.”

And He said:

---

## Page 1673

exalted is He:

“Indeed, the worst of living creatures in the sight of Allah are those who disbelieve, and they do not believe.”

And He said:

“And We have certainly created for Hell many of the jinn and mankind. They have hearts with which they do not understand, eyes with which they do not see, and ears with which they do not hear. Those are like livestock; rather, they are more astray. It is they who are the heedless.”

Dawābb (“living creatures”) is the plural of dābba (“a living creature”), which includes everything that moves in heaven or on earth: humans, jinn, angels, and animals. Thus, five verses in the Quran indicate the superiority of animals over many humans. Ibn al-Marzubān wrote a book entitled The Superiority of Dogs over Many Who Wear Clothes. The transmitted reports on this subject are too numerous for us to count, including the following in Musnad Ahmad:

“Many a mount remembers Allah more than its rider.”

Animals are superior to such people in several respects:

First, an animal has no means of attaining greater perfection or soundness than it already displays, whereas a human being does. If a person fails to attain the soundness and perfection for which he was created, his deficiency and loss become apparent in this respect.

Second, animals have inclinations and desires corresponding to their sensations and awareness, but they have not been endowed with the ability to distinguish and discriminate between what benefits them and what harms them. Human beings, however, have been endowed with this ability.

This is the meaning of the saying: Angels have reason without desires; animals have desires without reason; and human beings have both desires and reason. Whoever’s reason prevails over his desires is superior to the angels, or like them; but whoever’s desires prevail over his reason is inferior to animals.

> 5 - P. 368: He mentions the eighth proof, then says in the twelfth line: “Then he mentioned what al-Khallāl related…” This indicates two things:
> 
> First, that abridgment has taken place, because the “ninth proof” and the “tenth” were not introduced with their numbers, although hadiths were mentioned. Only on p. 370 does he mention the “eleventh proof.”
> 
> Second, that this copy has been altered.
> 
> 6 - P. 369: “Such a statement can only be made on the basis of … [the compiler, may Allah have mercy on him, indicated that there is a blank here in the original] that is clear. Lying about Allah, Mighty and Majestic, is more serious than lying about His Messenger.”
> 
> I say: The wording appears to be: “Only on the basis of clear knowledge,” or something similar.
> 
> 7 - P. 373: “As for the angels, their condition today resembles their condition thereafter, for their reward is continuous, and Paradise is not created, and affirmation…”
> 
> I say: The wording appears to be: “And Paradise is not created for them.”
> 
> 8 - P. 347: “Indeed, seating him upon the Throne is objectionable”—munkaran. The correct form is munkar.
> 
> 9 - P. 387: “It should not be said that, because it was not accompanied by a denunciation, this indicated that it was true. Their statement, ‘This is not a human being,’ was mistaken, and their statement, ‘This is nothing but a noble angel,’ was also mistaken, in their failure to recognize that he was human and their assertion that he was an angel, even though it was not accompanied by a denunciation, [indicated that it was true, and their statement, ‘This is not a human being; this is nothing but a noble angel,’ was mistaken in their denial of his humanity and their assertion that he was an angel, even though it was not accompanied by a denunciation] because they lost their senses upon seeing him. They were therefore not blamed for this in that state.”
> 
> I say: What appears within square brackets seems to be a repetition. Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1674

Third, these people face punishment, exemplary chastisement, and disgrace for the wicked deeds they commit: one is executed, another punished, another subjected to amputation, and another made to suffer and imprisoned. These are punishments prescribed by religious law. As for decreed punishments, some peoples were drowned, others destroyed by various forms of torment, and others afflicted with unjust rulers who subjected them to burning, drowning, mutilation, strangulation, and blinding. Animals are safe from all this.

Fourth, the wicked among the jinn and mankind will face horrors, the Fire, torment, shackles, and other things in the Hereafter from which animals are safe. Comparing their respective conditions makes clear the superiority of animals over these people.

Fifth, all animals believe in Allah and His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace; they glorify Allah with praise and are obedient to Him. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“There is nothing on the face of the earth that does not know that I am the Messenger of Allah, except the wicked among the jinn and mankind.”

The second form of comparison is to say that humanity as a whole is superior to the angels as a whole, without comparing individuals. On the view that righteous human beings are superior to the angels, this claim remains open to question; I do not know whether it is true. For we regard the second generation as a whole as superior to the third generation, although we know that many people of the third generation are superior to many people of the second.

---

## Page 1675

The third form of comparison is this: if we compare the superior members of each kind, then those next in rank with their counterparts in the other kind, which of the two groups is superior?

On the view that righteous human beings are superior, one may say: there is no doubt that the lower-ranking angels are superior to many human beings, while the superior human beings are superior to the superior angels. Yet the difference between “the superior members of the two groups” is greater; as for the difference between “their lower-ranking members,” this is unknown. Allah knows His creation best.

The fourth form is to ask: is the essence of an angel and angelic nature superior, or the essence of a human being and human nature?

This is comparable to our knowing that a living being, insofar as it is alive, is superior to a dead one; that strength and knowledge, considered as such, are superior to weakness and ignorance; that the male essence is superior to the female essence; and that the essence of a horse is superior to that of a donkey. Yet within the inferior kind there may be individuals better than many individuals of the superior kind: as with a donkey, a mouse, and an infirm horse; a righteous woman compared with a wicked man; and a strong, wicked man compared with a weak, infirm one.

The reason this classification is confined to these forms is that anything may be considered in particular, in general, or without qualification. Many important questions receive conflicting scholarly answers, and opinions concerning them become confused, because distinctions and distinctions of superiority are not made.

Thus, if you say: a human being

---

## Page 1676

and an angel: you may mean this particular human being, in which case the reference is particular; or the entire human species, in which case it is general; or the human being without qualification, abstracted from any restriction to the general or particular, its numerical scope being expressed as few or many. The first form concerns superiority both generally and particularly; the second, generally; the third, particularly; and the fourth concerns essence considered without qualification and in abstraction.

We may then say:

As regards the question considered in this way, I know of no earlier statement that explains it. Some people may have argued for the superiority of angels, and others for the superiority of human beings. This question may also have been confused with that of superiority between the righteous and others. Yet what has occurred to me—and Allah knows best what is correct—is that angelic essence is more perfect and more exalted, while human essence is simpler and more comprehensive.

The explanation is as follows:

When we consider the two essences and their intrinsic and consequent attributes, whether inseparable or predominant—life, knowledge, and power in relation to pleasures and desires—we find, first, that the angel is created with a grander form; his abode is higher; his life is more vigorous; his knowledge is greater; his faculties are stronger; his purity and freedom from defilement are more complete; and his attainment of what he seeks is easier and more complete. He is also further removed from whatever is incompatible with him or opposed to him. Yet these attributes are also found in the human being, according to his essence, with a more abundant share of them—of life, physical constitution, knowledge, power, purity, and so forth.

The human being also possesses things that the angel does not: his perception of subtle things through the senses and the intellect, and his enjoyment, in body and heart, of what he perceives. He eats, drinks, has sexual relations, forms wishes, and takes nourishment

---

## Page 1677

and reflects, among other activities in which the angel does not share. Yet the angel has a greater share of what they have in common, and the qualities they share are far superior to those peculiar to the human being.

“An illustration”: one man has a hundred dinars, while another has fifty dirhams, or fifty dinars, or fifty copper coins. Since this is so, the decisive answer is as stated above.

If, however, you seek an unqualified judgment, angelic essence, together with its necessary attributes, is superior to human essence with its necessary attributes. There is no doubt about this. For the life, sensory perception, knowledge, action, attainment of pleasure, and fulfillment of desire necessarily belonging to human essence are of little account. Its forms are merely more numerous in comparison with what resembles angelic essence. It is like comparing someone who knows a little about everything with someone who has mastered knowledge of Allah, His names, and His signs. The condition of someone possessing a dirham is not comparable to that of someone possessing a pearl, nor is the condition of someone who governs all people comparable to that of someone who governs one human being and a horse.

This is clearly indicated by the words of the Most High:

“We have certainly honored the children of Adam, carried them on land and sea, provided them with good things, and favored them greatly above many of those We have created.”

This indicates that they have not been favored above all. His word “mimman” (“of those”) is partitive.
