“I said to my father, ‘Some people say that they love Yazīd.’ He replied, ‘My son, could anyone who believes in Allah and the Last Day love Yazīd?’
“I asked, ‘Father, why, then, do you not curse him?’
“He replied, ‘My son, when have you ever seen your father curse anyone?’”

Muhannā said:
“I asked Ahmad about Yazīd ibn Mu‘āwiya ibn Abī Sufyān. He replied, ‘He is the one who did what he did to Medina.’
“I asked, ‘What did he do?’
“He replied, ‘He killed some of the Companions of Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and did other things.’
“I asked, ‘What else did he do?’
“He replied, ‘He plundered it.’
“I asked, ‘Should hadith be transmitted on his authority?’
“He replied, ‘No hadith should be transmitted on his authority.’”

This is also related by Judge Abū Ya‘lā and others.

It has reached me that, when Abū Muhammad al-Maqdisī was asked about Yazīd, he said, “He is neither to be reviled nor loved.” It has also reached me that our grandfather, Abū ‘Abd Allāh Ibn Taymiyya, was asked about Yazīd and replied, “Neither detract nor add.” This is the fairest and best of the views concerning him and others like him.

---

## Page 1806

As for refraining from reviling and cursing him, this rests either on the view that the sinfulness warranting his being cursed has not been established, or on the view that a particular sinner should not be singled out for cursing, because doing so is either forbidden or discouraged.

It is authentically reported in Sahīh al-Bukhārī, on the authority of ‘Umar, in the account of “Himār,” who repeatedly drank wine and was flogged for it, that when one of the Companions cursed him, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Do not curse him, for he loves Allah and His Messenger.”

He also said:

“Cursing a believer is like killing him.”

This is reported by both al-Bukhārī and Muslim. Yet it is also authentically established that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, cursed wine and those who drink it. Thus, the Prophet cursed wine-drinkers in general, while forbidding, in an authentic hadith, the cursing of this particular individual.

Similarly, the scriptural threats of punishment apply generally to those who devour orphans’ wealth, adulterers, and thieves. We do not, on the basis of these general texts, declare any particular individual to be among the people of the Fire, since a stronger countervailing factor may prevent the threatened consequence from following: repentance, good deeds that erase sins, afflictions that expiate them, accepted intercession, or something else, as we have explained elsewhere. These, then, are three grounds for refraining from cursing him.

Some of those who curse him hold that refraining from doing so is like refraining from other permissible forms of unnecessary speech—not that cursing him is discouraged.

As for refraining from loving him, this is because special love is reserved for the prophets, the steadfastly truthful, the martyrs, and the righteous, and he is not one of them. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“A person will be with those whom he loves.”

Anyone who believes in Allah and the Last Day would not choose to be with Yazīd or with other kings like him who are not just.

---

## Page 1807

There are “two grounds” for refraining from loving him. The first is that he performed no righteous deeds that would warrant loving him, and thus remained merely one of the kings who wielded power. Loving particular individuals of this kind is not religiously prescribed. This ground, together with the position of those who do not consider his sinfulness established, rests on believing that he acted on an interpretation.

The second is that his conduct, including the affairs of al-Husayn and the people of al-Harra, involved deeds indicating his injustice and sinfulness.

As for the scholars who cursed him, such as Abū al-Faraj ibn al-Jawzī, Ilkiyā al-Harrāsī, and others, they did so because of the deeds he committed that permitted his being cursed. They may argue that he was a flagrant sinner and that every flagrant sinner may be cursed. Or they may hold that someone who commits a sin may be cursed even without being judged a flagrant sinner, just as the opposing sides at Siffīn cursed one another in the supplication during prayer. Thus, ‘Alī and his companions cursed particular men from Syria in their prayer supplications, and the Syrians likewise cursed their opponents. Yet when the combatants act on a defensible interpretation—both those in the right and those in rebellion—none of them is thereby deemed a flagrant sinner.

He may also be cursed specifically for his grave sins, even if other flagrant sinners are not cursed, just as the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, cursed certain categories of sinners and particular individuals, without cursing them all. These, then, are (three grounds for cursing him.

As for those who considered loving him permissible, or who loved him, such as al-Ghazālī and al-Dastī, they have two grounds:

---

## Page 1808

The first is that he was a Muslim entrusted with governing the community during the time of the Companions, and those of them who remained followed him. He possessed praiseworthy qualities and acted on an interpretation in the matters for which he is condemned, including the affair of al-Harra. Thus, they say that he exercised independent judgment but was mistaken. They also say that the people of al-Harra were the first to break their pledge of allegiance to him, and that Ibn ‘Umar and others condemned them for this. As for the killing of al-Husayn, he neither ordered it nor approved of it. Rather, he showed grief over his killing and condemned those who killed him. The head was not brought to him; it was brought only to Ibn Ziyād.

(The second ground is that it is established in Sahīh al-Bukhārī, on the authority of Ibn ‘Umar, that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The first army to attack Constantinople will be forgiven.”

The commander of the first army to attack it was Yazīd.

“The sound conclusion” is that both these positions allow scope for independent judgment. Cursing those who commit sins is a matter open to independent judgment, as is loving those who perform both good and evil deeds. Indeed, in our view, there is no contradiction in a person deserving both praise and blame, both reward and punishment. Likewise, there is no contradiction in offering the funeral prayer for him and supplicating for him, while also cursing and reviling him, each on account of a different aspect of his conduct.

For Ahl al-Sunnah agree that flagrant sinners among the Muslims—even if they enter the Fire or deserve to enter it—must eventually enter Paradise. Thus, they receive both reward and punishment. The Khawārij and the Mu‘tazila, however, deny this. They hold that whoever deserves reward does not deserve punishment, and whoever deserves punishment does not deserve reward. This is a well-known issue, and its detailed exposition belongs elsewhere.

---

## Page 1809

As for the permissibility of supplicating both for a man and against him, this issue is discussed in detail in connection with funeral rites. The funeral prayer is offered for deceased Muslims, righteous and sinful alike, even if a sinful person is also cursed individually or as one of a class. Nevertheless, the first position is more balanced and just. This was the answer I gave Būlāy, the Mongol commander, when they came to Damascus during the great upheaval. I had discussions with him and others, and among his questions he asked me:

“What do you say about Yazīd?”

I replied: “We neither revile him nor love him. He was not a righteous man whom we should love, and we do not single out any Muslim for reviling.”

He said: “Do you not curse him, then? Was he not an oppressor? Did he not kill al-Husayn?”

I replied: “When oppressors such as al-Hajjāj ibn Yūsuf and others like him are mentioned, we say what Allah says in the Quran:

‘Unquestionably, the curse of Allah is upon the wrongdoers.’

“We do not like to curse anyone individually. Some scholars have cursed him, and this is a position that allows scope for independent judgment. Nevertheless, the position I have stated is preferable to us and better. As for anyone who killed ‘al-Husayn’, helped to kill him, or approved of it, upon him is the curse of Allah, the angels, and all mankind. Allah accepts neither sarf nor ʿadl from him.”

He said: “Do you not love the Prophet’s household, then?”

I replied: “Loving them is, in our view, a binding obligation for which one is rewarded. For it is established in Sahīh Muslim, on the authority of Zayd ibn Arqam, who said: ‘The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, addressed us at a pool called Khumm, between Mecca and Medina. He said: “O people, I am leaving among you two weighty things: the Book of Allah.” He spoke of the Book of Allah and urged us to adhere to it. Then he said: “And my family

---

## Page 1810

, the members of my household. I remind you of Allah concerning the members of my household; I remind you of Allah concerning the members of my household.”’”

I said to the commander: “And every day in our prayers we say:

‘O Allah, bless Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, as You blessed Abraham. Indeed, You are Praiseworthy, Glorious. And bestow Your blessings upon Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, as You bestowed Your blessings upon the family of Abraham. Indeed, You are Praiseworthy, Glorious.’”

The commander said: “Who, then, hates the Prophet’s household?”

I replied: “Whoever hates them, upon him is the curse of Allah, the angels, and all mankind. Allah accepts neither sarf nor ʿadl from him.”

Then I asked the Mongol minister: “Why did he bring up Yazīd, when he is a Tatar?”

He replied: “They told him that the people of Damascus are Nawāsib—those hostile to ‘Alī.”

I said loudly: “Whoever said that here is lying! Whoever says this, upon him is the curse of Allah. By Allah, there are no Nawāsib among the people of Damascus, and I have never known a single one among them. If anyone were to disparage ‘Alī in Damascus, the Muslims would rise against him. In the past, however, when the Umayyads ruled these lands, some of them harboured hostility towards ‘Alī and reviled him. Today, none of those people remain.”

---

## Page 1811

He was asked:

About a group united in various forms of corruption. Among them is someone who says that religion became corrupt before “this”—namely, when the caliphate was taken from ‘Alī ibn Abī Tālib. Those who took office in his place were not qualified to govern, so their appointment was invalid. Consequently, no contract concluded by Muslims thereafter was valid, whether a marriage contract or any other. Everyone who married after that event therefore has an invalid marriage; likewise, all contracts, appointments to office, and other such matters are invalid.

The person making this claim also alleges that Allah is a cross, with each letter of the Divine Name at the end of one of the cross’s lines. He asserts to people that the Jews and Christians follow the truth, as do the Magians and others.

He replied—may Allah Most High have mercy on him:

This ignorant man resembles, in his ignorance, the Rāfidah, who tell lies and whose superstitions deceive only ignorant people unfamiliar with the foundations of Islam, such as those mentioned in this question. It was stated that they say religion became corrupt when the caliphate was taken from ‘Alī, and that was

---

## Page 1812

from the time of the Prophet’s death, may Allah bless him and grant him peace; that the Rightly Guided Caliphs were unqualified to govern; that Muslims’ contracts are invalid; and that Allah is a cross, while he endorses the religions of the Jews, Christians, and Magians. Such a man is among the worst heretics, of the same kind as the Qarmatian esotericists, such as the Nusayrīs, the Ismāʿīlīs, and their followers.

This is why his statements are contradictory. Someone who endorses the religions of the Jews, Christians, and Magians, yet attacks the faith of the rightly guided Caliphs, who were themselves guided aright, and of the earliest pioneers among the Emigrants and Helpers, can only be among the most ignorant of people and those most deeply steeped in unbelief. If he were among the believers who know that this community is the best community brought forth for mankind, and that its best generation is the first, followed by the next, and then the next, he would not endorse the religion of the disbelievers while attacking the faith of the Emigrants and Helpers.

The refutation of this and similar claims is set out at length elsewhere. In our refutation of the Rāfidah, we have discussed this matter at greater length than space here permits. No one who believes that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah would make such a statement. We therefore answer someone who acknowledges that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah by explaining to him what the Messenger brought that dispels his misconception. As for someone who attacks his prophethood, we address him from another angle: each situation calls for its own manner of discourse.

---

## Page 1813

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

“Do the scholars regard the following claims as authentic: that ʿAlī, may Allah be pleased with him, fought the jinn in the well; that he stretched out his hand on the day of Khaybar and the army crossed over it; that he charged the Confederates, who divided into seventeen groups before him, with a man behind each group striking with a sword and saying, ‘I am ʿAlī’; and that he had a sword called Dhū al-Faqār which could lengthen and shorten?

“That he struck Marhab with it while Marhab had a marble basin on his head, splitting it, him, and his horse with a single blow that continued into the ground, while a voice called out in the air, ‘There is no sword but Dhū al-Faqār, and no valiant man but ʿAlī’; that he launched toward the fortress of al-Ghurāb in a catapult; that he was sent secretly to every prophet and openly alongside the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace; and that he would charge armies of fifty thousand, twenty thousand, and thirty thousand single-handedly?

“That when Marhab came out from Khaybar to face him, he struck him once, cleaving him lengthwise and his horse crosswise, and the sword penetrated two or three cubits into the ground; and that he grasped the ring of Khaybar’s gate and shook it, causing the town to shake and battlements to fall from the wall? Is any of this authentic?”

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. These accounts are fabricated lies, by the unanimous agreement of the people of knowledge and faith.

---

## Page 1814

Neither Alī nor any other Companion fought the jinn, nor did any human fight the jinn, whether in the well of Dhāt al-Alam or elsewhere.

The hadith reporting that he fought the jinn is a fabrication and a lie, by the unanimous agreement of the knowledgeable scholars. During the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, Alī never fought an army of fifty thousand or thirty thousand, let alone charged such an army single-handedly. The campaigns he attended with the Messenger of Allah and in which he fought numbered nine: Badr, Uhud, the Trench, Khaybar, the conquest of Mecca, the battle of Hunayn, and others.

The polytheists were most numerous at the Battle of the Confederates, also known as the Battle of the Trench. They were besieging Medina, but neither they nor the Muslims fought with their entire forces. Only a few Muslims and a few disbelievers fought one another. It was during this battle that Alī killed Amr ibn Abd Wudd al-Āmirī. Whenever Alī fought in single combat, he faced only one opponent, never two.

As for Marhab at Khaybar, it is established in the Sahih that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“I shall give the banner to a man who loves Allah and His Messenger, and whom Allah and His Messenger love. Allah will grant victory at his hands.”

He gave it to Alī. The fighting at Khaybar lasted several days, and some of its fortresses were conquered at the hands of Alī, may Allah be pleased with him. A report relates that he killed Marhab, while another relates that Muhammad ibn Maslama killed him. Perhaps there were two men named Marhab. He killed him in the usual manner: he did not cleave his entire body in two, nor did he cleave the horse, nor did there descend

---

## Page 1815

the sword to the ground. Nor did a sword descend from heaven for Alī or anyone else. He did not stretch out his hand for the army to cross over it; the wall of Khaybar did not shake when the gate was torn off, nor did any of its battlements fall. Khaybar was not a city, but rather a collection of scattered fortresses whose inhabitants had farmland.

What is reported, however, is that he did not tear off the fortress gate until the Muslims had crossed it. Nor did he ever launch anything from a catapult. Many lies have been added to most of these accounts of campaigns involving Alī and others, like the fabrications told in the tales of Antara and other heroes.

Alī, may Allah be pleased with him, took part in only three wars after the death of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace: the Battle of the Camel, Siffīn, and the war against the people of Nahrawān. Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1816

He was asked about someone who said that Alī fought the jinn in the well, and that he charged twelve thousand men and defeated them.

He replied:

No Companion ever charged twelve thousand or ten thousand men single-handedly—neither Alī nor anyone else. Rather, the largest force assembled against the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was the Confederates who besieged him at the Trench. They numbered approximately that many, and Alī killed a man from among them named “Amr ibn Abd Wudd al-Āmirī”.

No human has fought the jinn—neither Alī nor anyone else. Indeed, Alī was of too high a standing for that. The jinn who follow the Companions fight the disbelieving jinn; they do not need the Companions to fight alongside them.

---

## Page 1817

He was asked about the following report:

“Fātimah came to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, Alī prays throughout every night except the night before Friday, when he offers the witr prayer and then sleeps until dawn.’ He replied, ‘Allah raises Alī’s soul every night before Friday, and it glorifies Him in heaven until dawn.’”

Is this authentic? And is it authentically reported that Alī said, “Ask me about the paths of heaven, for I know them better than the paths of the earth”?

He replied:

As for the report mentioned concerning Alī, it is a lie; no scholar has narrated it.

As for his saying, “Ask me about the paths of heaven,” he did say it, but he did not mean by this a path to guidance. Rather, by such words he meant righteous deeds through which one draws near to Allah. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 1818

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked about a man who said of Alī ibn Abī Tālib—may Allah be pleased with him—that he was not a member of the Prophet’s household, that invoking blessings upon him was impermissible, and that doing so was a religious innovation.

He replied:

There is no disagreement among Muslims that Alī ibn Abī Tālib belongs to the Prophet’s household. This is so evident to Muslims that it requires no proof. Indeed, after the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, he is the best member of the household and the best of Banū Hāshim. It is authentically established that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, wrapped his cloak around Alī, Fātimah, Hasan, and Husayn, saying:

“O Allah, these are the members of my household. Remove impurity from them and purify them thoroughly.”

As for invoking blessings upon him individually, this depends on whether blessings may be invoked individually upon someone other than the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace—for example, by saying, “O Allah, bless Umar,” or “O Allah, bless Alī.” Scholars have differed on this matter.

Mālik, al-Shāfiʿī, and a group of Hanbalī scholars held that blessings should not be invoked individually upon anyone other than the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. This accords with the report that Ibn Abbās said: “I do not know of anyone upon whom it is fitting to invoke blessings except the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.”

---

## Page 1819

Imam Ahmad and most of his followers held that there was no harm in doing so, because Alī ibn Abī Tālib—may Allah be pleased with him—said to Umar ibn al-Khattāb, “May Allah bless you.” This view is more correct and preferable.

However, singling out one of the Companions or the Prophet’s relatives, such as Alī or anyone else, for invocations of blessings to the exclusion of others, in imitation of the way blessings are invoked upon the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, so that this becomes a recognized hallmark associated with his name—this is the religious innovation.

---

## Page 1820

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah sanctify his soul—was asked:

Has any scholar or hadith scholar, or anyone regarded as an authority in the religion of Islam, established as authentic the report that the Commander of the Faithful, Alī ibn Abī Tālib—may Allah be pleased with him—said, “When I die, place me on my she-camel and let her go. Wherever she kneels, bury me,” and that she then went off and no one knew where his grave was? Is this authentic or not? Has any scholar known where he was buried or not? Why was he killed? When did this happen? Who killed him?

Who killed al-Husayn, and why was he killed? Is it authentic that the family of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, were taken captive; that they were made to ride camels naked, with nothing to cover them, so Allah Most High created two humps for the camels they were riding, behind which they concealed themselves; and that, when al-Husayn’s head was severed, it was paraded throughout all the lands, taken to Damascus, then taken to Egypt and buried there? And that Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiyah was the one who did this to the Prophet’s family—is this authentic or not?

Is someone who makes these assertions thereby an innovator in Allah’s religion? And what is required of him if

---

## Page 1821

he relates this among the people? If someone objects to these claims, is he considered to be enjoining what is right and forbidding what is wrong, or not? Give us your ruling—may you be rewarded—and provide us with a clear and thorough explanation.

He replied:

Praise be to Allah, Lord of all worlds. As for the claim that the Commander of the Faithful, “Alī ibn Abī Tālib”—may Allah be pleased with him—instructed that, upon his death, he should be placed on his mount, that it should be let loose, and that he should be buried wherever it knelt, and that this was done to him: this is a fabricated lie, by agreement of the scholars. Alī gave no such instructions, nor was anything of the kind done to him. No one known for learning and integrity has mentioned this; it is related only by those who transmit accounts from certain liars.

It is not permissible to do this to any deceased Muslim, nor is it permissible for anyone to leave instructions to this effect. Rather, this is an indignity inflicted on the deceased, and there is no benefit in it. If the purpose is to conceal the location of his grave, then once the she-camel kneels, a grave must still be dug for him and he must be buried in it. A grave could therefore be dug and he could be buried in it without this disgraceful treatment: leaving his dead body on the back of an animal roaming through the wilderness.

The scholars have differed over “the location of his grave.” The generally accepted account among scholars is that he was buried in the governor’s palace in Kufa, and that his grave was concealed so that the “Khārijites,” who declared him an unbeliever and regarded killing him as lawful, would not exhume him. Indeed, the man who killed him was one of the Khārijites, namely Abd al-Rahmān

---

## Page 1822

ibn Muljam al-Murādī. He and two others had pledged to kill Alī, Mu‘āwiya, and Amr ibn al-Ās, for they declared all of them unbelievers, as well as anyone who did not conform to their whims. Reports condemning them have been transmitted from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, through numerous independent chains. Muslim included the hadith concerning them in his Sahīh through ten chains of transmission, and al-Bukhārī through several chains; the compilers of the Sunan and Musnad collections transmitted it through still more.

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said concerning them:

“Any of you would consider his prayer insignificant compared with theirs, his fasting compared with theirs, and his recitation compared with theirs. They recite the Quran, but it does not pass beyond their throats. They pass out of Islam as an arrow passes through its quarry. If I encounter them, I will surely kill them as ‘Ād were killed”—and in another version—“Wherever you encounter them, kill them, for whoever kills them will have a reward with Allah on the Day of Resurrection. They kill the followers of Islam.”

The Companions, may Allah be pleased with them, agreed that they should be fought. It was Alī, may Allah be pleased with him, however, who personally fought them and ordered that they be fought. As related in the two Sahīh collections, Abū Sa‘īd reported that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“A faction will break away at a time when the people are divided. They will be killed by the one of the two parties that is closer to the truth.”

Alī, may Allah be pleased with him, killed them at al-Nahrawān. They had previously gathered at a place called “Harūrā’,” which is why they are called the Harūriyya. Ibn Abbās was sent to them and debated with them until approximately half of them abandoned their position. The remainder then killed “Abd Allāh ibn Khabbāb” and raided the Muslims’ grazing herds, so orders were given

---

## Page 1823

by Alī for the people to go out and fight them. He related to them the Prophet’s command, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, to fight them, and described their identifying sign: among them would be a man with underdeveloped hands, one hand incomplete, and something like a quivering lump of flesh on his breast. When they were killed, the man matching this description was found among them.

When the three Khārijites agreed to kill the three rulers of the Muslims, Abd al-Rahmān ibn Muljam killed “Alī,” may Allah be pleased with him, on Friday, the seventeenth of Ramadan, in the year 40 AH. He lay in wait for him and struck him when he came out for the dawn prayer.

The established practice was that the caliphs and their deputies—the governors who ruled the Muslims—would lead the Muslims in the five daily prayers, the Friday prayer, the prayers of the two Eids, the prayer for rain, the eclipse prayer, and other prayers such as funeral prayers. Thus, the military commander was also the imam who led the prayer.

As for the man who intended to kill “Mu‘āwiya,” they reported that he wounded him. The physician said, “You can be treated, but you will no longer be able to have children.” It is said that from that time onward Mu‘āwiya had an enclosure installed in the mosque. The governors followed his example so that they and their entourages could pray inside it, fearing that someone might attack the Commander of the Faithful and kill him. Nevertheless, impermissible things were done within these enclosures, and some disapproved of praying in enclosures of this kind.

As for the man who intended to kill “Amr ibn al-Ās,” Amr had appointed a man—named Khārija—to take his place that day. The Khārijite mistook him for Amr and killed him. When he discovered his mistake, he said, “I intended Amr, but Allah intended Khārija.” This became a proverb.

---

## Page 1824

It is said that they concealed the graves of “Alī,” “Mu‘āwiya,” and “Amr” out of fear that the Khārijites would attack them. For this reason, they buried Mu‘āwiya inside the southern wall of the congregational mosque, in the governor’s palace known as al-Khadrā’. This is what ordinary people call the grave of “Hūd.” Yet the scholars agree that Hūd never came to Damascus. Rather, his grave is in Yemen, where he was sent as a prophet; it is also said to be in Mecca, to which he migrated. No one has said that it is in Damascus.

As for the “Mu‘āwiya” buried outside “Bāb al-Saghīr,” he is Mu‘āwiya ibn Yazīd, who ruled for about forty days and was known for asceticism and religious devotion. Alī was buried there, but the traces of his grave disappeared, which is why its location is not apparent. As for the shrine at Najaf, those with knowledge agree that it is not Alī’s grave. Rather, it has been said to be the grave of al-Mughīra ibn Shu‘ba. For more than three hundred years, no one identified it as Alī’s grave or visited it, despite the large numbers of Muslims—members of the Prophet’s household, Shia, and others—and their rule in Kufa. It was only made into a shrine during the reign of the non-Arab Banū Buwayh, more than three hundred years after Alī’s death. They related a story claiming that al-Rashīd used to visit that place, along with other accounts that establish no proof.

As for the question about members of the Prophet’s household being taken captive and made to ride camels until the camels grew two humps—these being the bukht camels—so that they could conceal themselves in this way, this is among the most disgraceful and obvious lies. It is among the fabrications of the heretics

---

## Page 1825

and the hypocrites, whose purpose is to malign Islam and its adherents, including the Prophet’s household and others. Anyone who hears such a story, how widely it circulates, and the falsehoods it contains may think or say: “The accounts transmitted to us of the miracles of the prophets and the wonders granted to Allah’s righteous servants are of this same kind.” Then, if it were established that the community had taken its own Prophet’s household captive, this would constitute an attack on the best community brought forth for mankind whose gravity Allah alone knows. Every rational person knows that bukht camels had already been created and existed before Allah sent the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, and before the Prophet’s household existed, just as other camels, sheep, cattle, horses, mules, and goats existed.

This fabrication is merely like their lie that Alī—may Allah be pleased with him—put out his hand at Khaybar, and a mule stepped on it, whereupon he said to it: “May Allah put an end to your offspring!” Every rational person knows that mules have never had offspring. Moreover, they had no mule with them at Khaybar; indeed, the Muslims had no mules at all. The first mule they acquired was the one that al-Muqawqis—the ruler of Egypt—gave to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him. It remained with him until his death.

In Sahīh Muslim, Abū Hurayra reports that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“There are two kinds of people from my community who will be among the inhabitants of the Fire whom I have not yet seen: women who are clothed, straying and leading others astray, with things upon their heads resembling the humps of bukht camels. They will neither enter Paradise nor smell its fragrance. And men carrying whips like cattle’s tails, with which they beat Allah’s servants.”

The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, thus likened those wearing the large head-wraps that would appear after his death to the humps of bukht camels. Had his listeners not already known these camels, they would not have understood. These head-wraps have

---

## Page 1826

appeared long after his death, in this era and the period surrounding it. Moreover, a bukht camel does not conceal its rider if he is naked. Had Allah willed to cover someone unjustly stripped naked, He would have covered him with something suitable, just as He covered Abraham, His intimate friend, when he was stripped and placed in the catapult.

Another indication of the manifest falsehood of this account is that Muslims have continually taken captives from among the unbelievers, both the People of the Book and others. Yet they have never been known to transport women stripped naked, with their bodies exposed. Rather, the most that would be visible of a captive woman was her face, her hands, or her foot.

Nor has it ever been known in Islam that any Muslim took a member of the Prophet’s household captive while knowing that the person belonged to that household. The only possible exception would be someone among the Muslims’ captives whose membership in the Prophet’s household was unknown—for example, a woman captured by the enemy and subsequently rescued by the Muslims. Once it became clear that she was originally free, they would release her. If, among such cases, there were an unknown person who concealed her lineage and deemed lawful with her what Allah had forbidden—a heretic and hypocrite—Allah knows best the truth of that. But nothing of this kind has ever occurred openly in Islam.

Among the claims made by these ignorant people is that al-Hajjāj ibn Yūsuf killed the noble descendants of the Prophet and sought to wipe them out entirely. This reflects ignorance of people’s circumstances. Although al-Hajjāj was a destroyer of lives and a shedder of blood who killed a great many people, he never killed any of the noble members of Banū Hāshim. Rather, his sovereign, Abd al-Malik ibn Marwān, forbade him to attack Banū Hāshim, who are those noble descendants. He stated that he had come to war when they attacked them—meaning when al-Husayn was killed.

---

## Page 1827

Nor is it known that al-Hajjāj killed anyone from Banū Hāshim during the caliphate of Abd al-Malik, when he was his governor in Iraq.

When captivity is mentioned to us, the incident most often cited is the killing of al-Husayn and the transportation of his family to Yazīd. Yet these people are ignorant of what actually happened. Some imagine that his family were taken to Egypt, that they were killed there, and that they numbered a great many people. Indeed, some of them, upon seeing dead bodies bearing signs of violent death, say, “These are among the captives who were killed.” All of this is ignorance and falsehood. Al-Husayn—may Allah be pleased with him, and may those who killed him and those who approved of his killing be cursed—was killed on the day of ʿĀshūrāʾ in the year 61 AH.

The man who urged his killing was al-Shimr ibn Dhī al-Jawshan, who began writing about it to the ruler’s governor in Iraq, Ubayd Allah ibn Ziyād. This Ubayd Allah ordered his deputy, Umar ibn Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqās, to fight al-Husayn, after al-Husayn had asked them for what any ordinary Muslim might ask. He had brought no fighting force with him. He asked them to let him return to Medina, send him to his cousin Yazīd, or allow him to go to the frontier to fight the unbelievers. They refused, insisting that he either surrender to them as a prisoner or face them in battle. They fought him until they killed him, along with a group of his household and others.

Then they took his belongings and his family to Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiyah in Damascus. Yazīd had not ordered them to kill him, nor did he show any pleasure at his killing or approval of it. Rather, he spoke words condemning them. It is reported that he said:

“I would have been satisfied with the obedience of the people of Iraq without al-Husayn being killed.”

He also said:

---

## Page 1828

“May Allah curse Ibn Marjānah (meaning Ubayd Allah ibn Ziyād)! By Allah, had there been any blood relationship between him and al-Husayn, he would not have killed him.” By this he intended to impugn the recognition of his lineage, since his father, Ziyād, had been acknowledged as a son of Abū Sufyān Sakhr ibn Harb and had consequently come to trace his lineage to him. Both Banū Umayyah and Banū Hāshim are descendants of Abd Manāf.

It is reported that when al-Husayn’s belongings and family arrived before Yazīd, weeping and cries arose in his house over what had happened. It is also reported that he treated al-Husayn’s family honorably, provided them with good accommodation, and gave his son Alī the choice of remaining with him or returning to Medina. He chose Medina. The claim concerning the place in the Damascus congregational mosque called “the prison of Alī ibn al-Husayn” is false and has no basis.

Nevertheless, he did not carry out Allah’s prescribed punishment upon those who killed al-Husayn—may Allah be pleased with him—nor did he avenge him. Rather, he killed his supporters to secure his rule. It has also been reported that, concerning the killing of al-Husayn, he quoted verses whose utterance entails outright disbelief, such as:

“When those laden mounts appeared, and those heads rose into view
Toward the heights of Jayrūn,
The raven croaked, and I said, ‘Lament or do not lament;
I have exacted from the Prophet the debts owed to me.’”

This poetry is disbelief.

There is no doubt that people differ greatly over “Yazīd.” One group considers him an unbeliever; indeed, they consider both him and his father unbelievers. Moreover, they also declare Abū Bakr, Umar, Uthmān, and the majority of the Emigrants and Helpers to be unbelievers. These Rāfidah (rejectionists) are among the most ignorant and misguided of Allah’s creatures, and the worst of them in

---

## Page 1829

lying about Allah, Almighty and Majestic, His Messenger, the Companions, the Prophet’s relatives, and others. Their lies about Yazīd are like their lies about Abū Bakr, Umar, and Uthmān; indeed, their lies about Yazīd are far less grave.

Another group regards him as one of the rightly guided leaders, the just caliphs, and the righteous believers. Some even regard him as one of the Companions, while others regard him as a prophet. This too is among the clearest examples of ignorance and misguidance, and among the most outrageous lies and absurdities. Rather, he was one of the Muslim kings, with good deeds and bad deeds, and he should be judged as other kings like him are judged. We have discussed this at length elsewhere.

As for al-Husayn—may Allah be pleased with him—he was killed at Karbalā’, near the Euphrates, and his body was buried where he was killed. His head was brought before Ubayd Allah ibn Ziyād in Kufa. This is what al-Bukhārī reported in his Sahīh, as did other leading scholars.

As for his head being taken to Yazīd in Syria, this has been reported through disconnected chains of transmission, none of which has been established as authentic. Indeed, the reports contain indications that they are fabricated lies. They mention that “Yazīd” began tapping al-Husayn’s front teeth with a rod, and that some of the Companions present—such as Anas ibn Mālik and Abū Barzah—objected to this. This is a deceptive conflation: the person who tapped the teeth with a rod was Ubayd Allah ibn Ziyād. That is how it is recorded in the Sahīh and the hadith collections arranged by Companion. They merely substituted “Yazīd” for Ubayd Allah ibn Ziyād. There is no doubt that Ubayd Allah ordered al-Husayn’s killing and had his head brought before him. Ibn Ziyād was subsequently killed because of this.

---

## Page 1830

And further evidence of this is that the Companions mentioned, such as Anas and Abū Barzah, were not in Syria at the time; they were in Iraq. These liars are simply ignorant of the evidence that exposes their lies.

As for his head being taken to Egypt, everyone agrees that this is false. All scholars agree that the shrine in Cairo, Egypt, known as the “Shrine of al-Husayn” is falsely attributed to him: it contains neither al-Husayn’s head nor any part of his remains. It was established only towards the end of the rule of “Banū Ubayd Allah ibn al-Qaddāh,” who ruled Egypt for two hundred years, until their dynasty came to an end in the days of “Nūr al-Dīn Mahmūd.” They claimed to be descendants of Fātimah and thus claimed noble prophetic descent. Scholars of genealogy, however, say that their claimed lineage is not genuine. It is said that their ancestor was the stepson of a noble descendant of al-Husayn, and that this was the basis of their claim to noble descent.

As for their doctrines and beliefs, scholars of the religion of Islam unanimously regarded them as reprehensible. They outwardly professed Shiism, while many of their leading figures and followers secretly adhered to the esoteric Qarmatian doctrine. This is among the vilest doctrines on earth, more corrupt than those of the Jews and Christians. Hence, most of those who joined them were people of concealed unbelief, hypocrisy, and religious innovation: would-be philosophers, antinomians, the Rāfidites, and others like them, whom people of knowledge and faith have no doubt do not belong to the ranks of knowledge and faith.

This “shrine” was thus established in the fifth century through a transfer from Ascalon. Shortly afterwards, the dynasty of those who had introduced it came to an end with the death of al-Ādid, their last ruler.

---

## Page 1831

The view preferred by scholars concerning the whereabouts of the head of al-Husayn ibn Alī—may Allah be pleased with them both—is that recorded by al-Zubayr ibn Bakkār in his book, “The Genealogies of Quraysh.” Al-Zubayr ibn Bakkār was among the most knowledgeable and reliable authorities on such matters. He stated that the head was taken to the Prophet’s city, Medina, and buried there. This is fitting, for his brother al-Hasan, his father’s uncle al-Abbās, his son Alī, and others like them are buried there.

Abū al-Khattāb ibn Dihyah—who was known as “the man of two lineages, tracing his descent to Dihyah and al-Husayn”—said in his book, “Well-Known Knowledge on the Merits of Days and Months,” after citing al-Zubayr ibn Bakkār’s report from Muhammad ibn al-Hasan:

“He arrived with al-Husayn’s head while the Umayyads were gathered with Amr ibn Saīd. They heard cries and asked, ‘What is this?’ They were told, ‘The women of Banū Hāshim are weeping upon seeing the head of al-Husayn ibn Alī.’” He related: “He brought the head of al-Husayn ibn Alī and took it in to Amr, who said, ‘By Allah, I wish the Commander of the Faithful had not sent it to me.’”

Ibn Dihyah said: “This report indicates that the head was taken to Medina; no other account of its whereabouts has been established as authentic. Al-Zubayr is the foremost authority on genealogy and the most distinguished scholar in this field.”

He continued: “As for the claim that it was in a shrine in Ascalon, this is false and would not be accepted by anyone possessing even the slightest measure of reason and discernment. Given the killing, hostility, and rancour displayed by the Umayyads, it is inconceivable that they would build a shrine over the head for people to visit.

“So much for that. As for what ‘Banū Ubayd’ fabricated during their decline, the onset of their ruin, and their hastened destruction, in the days of the man styled ‘al-Qāsim, Īsā ibn al-Zāfir,’ who was invested with the caliphate

---

## Page 1832

when he was five years and a few days old. He was born on Friday, 1 Muharram 544 AH, and received the pledge of allegiance on the morning his father, al-Zāfir, was killed: Thursday, the last day of Muharram 549 AH. He was then the age we have mentioned, so neither his contracts nor his covenants were valid. He died at the age of eleven years, six months, and a few days, on the eve of Friday, with thirteen nights remaining in Rajab 555 AH.

“During his reign, the construction of the newly established shrine in Cairo and the head’s public arrival with al-Mashhadī al-Asqalānī were contrived, so that what was presented through these outward displays would become firmly rooted in the hearts of the common people. This was something deliberately fabricated or staged to serve a purpose. They carried out their designs to win over the common people for worldly gain. The man who built it was Talā’i‘ ibn Ruzzīk, the Rāfidī. All who have written about the killing of al-Husayn have stated that his honoured head never went west.”

What Abū al-Khattāb ibn Dihyah has stated concerning this shrine—that its claim is a fabricated lie—is a matter of agreement among the scholars.

There is much to discuss concerning this subject and others like it. The killing of ‘Uthmān, the killing of al-Husayn, and similar events gave rise to much strife, falsehood, and partisan passions, into which groups of both earlier and later generations fell. All manner of lies were fabricated about the Commander of the Faithful ‘Uthmān and the Commander of the Faithful ‘Alī ibn Abī Tālib. Some are denied by their partisans and others like them, and some by those who hate them. This was especially so after the killing of ‘Uthmān, when falsehood and partisan passions grew greatly.

---

## Page 1833

And statements were made on both sides about the Commander of the Faithful, ‘Alī ibn Abī Tālib, of which he was innocent. Religious innovations, partisan passions, and falsehood continued to increase until matters arose that would take too long to explain. These include the practices that many in later generations introduced on the day of ‘Āshūrā’. Some turn it into an occasion of mourning, openly displaying wailing and anguish, tormenting themselves, mistreating animals, reviling deceased friends of Allah, fabricating lies about the Prophet’s household, and committing other reprehensible acts prohibited by the Book of Allah, the Sunnah of His Messenger—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—and the agreement of the Muslims.

Allah Most High honoured al-Husayn—may Allah be pleased with him—with martyrdom on this day, and thereby disgraced those who killed him, helped to kill him, or approved of his killing. He had a good example in the martyrs who preceded him. He and his brother are the leaders of the young men of Paradise. Both had grown up when Islam was strong and had not attained through emigration, jihad, and patiently enduring harm for Allah’s sake what the members of their household had attained. Allah Most High therefore honoured them both with martyrdom, completing their honour and raising their ranks. His killing was a tremendous calamity. Allah, glorified is He, has prescribed affirming that we belong to Him and will return to Him when calamity strikes, saying:

“And give good tidings to the patient, who, when a calamity befalls them, say, ‘Indeed, we belong to Allah, and indeed to Him we shall return.’ Upon them are blessings from their Lord and mercy, and it is they who are rightly guided.”

In the two Sahīh collections, the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—is reported to have said:

“No Muslim is afflicted by a calamity and says, ‘Indeed, we belong to Allah, and indeed to Him we shall return. O Allah, reward me for my calamity and grant me something better in its place,’ but Allah rewards him for his calamity and grants him something better in its place.”

Among the finest reports to mention here is one narrated by Imām Ahmad and Ibn Mājah from Fātimah, daughter of

---

## Page 1834

al-Husayn, on the authority of her father al-Husayn—may Allah be pleased with him—who said: The Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“No Muslim suffers a calamity, then remembers it—even long after it occurred—and again affirms that we belong to Allah and to Him we shall return, but Allah records for him a reward like that of the day he was afflicted.”

This hadith was narrated from al-Husayn by his daughter Fātimah, who witnessed his killing. It is well known that the calamity of al-Husayn’s death continues to be remembered despite the passage of time. It is therefore among the beauties of Islam that he himself conveyed this Sunnah from the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace: whenever this calamity is remembered, one affirms that we belong to Allah and to Him we shall return, thereby receiving a reward like that earned on the day the calamity befell the Muslims.

As for anyone who, long after it occurred, does what the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—forbade when a calamity was still fresh, his punishment is more severe. This includes slapping the cheeks, tearing the necklines of garments, and uttering the cries of the pre-Islamic age of ignorance. In the two Sahīh collections, ‘Abd Allāh ibn Mas‘ūd—may Allah be pleased with him—reported that the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“He is not one of us who slaps his cheeks, tears the neckline of his garment, and utters the cries of the pre-Islamic age of ignorance.”

In the two Sahīh collections, Abū Mūsā al-Ash‘arī—may Allah be pleased with him—said:

“I disavow what the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—disavowed. The Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—disavowed women who shave their heads, wail aloud, or tear their garments in mourning.”

In Sahīh Muslim, Abū Mālik al-Ash‘arī reported that the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Four practices of the pre-Islamic age of ignorance remain among my community, which they will not abandon: boasting of noble ancestry,

---

## Page 1835

disparaging people’s lineage, seeking rain through the stars, and wailing over the dead.” He also said:

“If a woman who wails in mourning does not repent before her death, she will be made to stand on the Day of Resurrection wearing a robe of tar and a garment of scabies.”

There are numerous reports concerning this. How much worse, then, when it is compounded by wronging the believers, cursing and reviling them, and helping those who promote discord and religious deviation to achieve their intended corruption of the religion, along with other deeds that Allah Most High alone can enumerate.

Some who profess adherence to the Sunnah transmitted fabricated hadiths, and had such hadiths transmitted to them. On these they based the distinctive observances they adopted on this day to oppose the observances of that other group. Thus they countered falsehood with falsehood and answered an innovation with another innovation, even though one of the two is more corrupting and more helpful to those who promote religious deviation. One example is the lengthy hadith in which it is reported:

“Whoever bathes on the day of ‘Āshūrā’ will not fall ill that year, and whoever applies kohl on the day of ‘Āshūrā’ will not suffer inflammation of the eyes that year.”

There are similar reports about “applying dye on the day of ‘Āshūrā’ and shaking hands on that day,” and other such practices. This hadith and others like it are fabricated lies, by the agreement of those versed in hadith scholarship. Although some hadith scholars have cited it and said, “It is authentic, and its chain of transmission meets the criteria of the Sahīh,” this is unquestionably an error, as has been explained elsewhere.

None of the leading scholars of the Muslims recommended bathing on the day of ‘Āshūrā’, applying kohl or dye on it, or other such practices. Nor was this mentioned by any of the Muslim scholars whose example is followed

---

## Page 1836

and who are consulted concerning what Allah has commanded and forbidden. Nor did the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, do any of this, nor Abū Bakr, ‘Umar, ‘Uthmān, or ‘Alī.

No such hadith is mentioned in any of the collections compiled by hadith scholars: neither in collections arranged by narrator, such as the Musnads of Ahmad, Ishāq, Ahmad ibn Manī‘ [and] (*) al-Humaydī, al-Dālānī, Abū Ya‘lā al-Mawsilī, and similar works; nor in collections arranged by subject, such as the Sahīh and Sunan collections; nor in comprehensive compilations combining hadiths and early reports, such as Mālik’s Muwatta’ and the works of Wakī‘, ‘Abd al-Razzāq, Sa‘īd ibn Mansūr, Ibn Abī Shaybah, and others.

Then those who follow their own desires supposed that whoever engaged in these practices did so to display hostility towards the Prophet’s household and to satisfy their resentment against them. Those who professed adherence to the Sunnah opposed them, responding by explaining that they were innocent of such hostility, that they had a rightful claim to allegiance to the Prophet’s household, and that their claim was stronger than that of others. This is true. Yet confusion and error crept into their thinking when they supposed that these practices were good and religiously recommended. Allah knows best whether the person who first fabricated and introduced these innovations intended hostility towards the Prophet’s household or towards others. For guidance without guidance from Allah—or anything else—is misguidance.

We must follow what has been sent down to us from our Lord—the Book and wisdom—and adhere to the straight path, the path of those whom Allah has blessed: the prophets and the steadfastly truthful

> **Footnotes:**
> (*) The text in square brackets is absent from the printed edition. The correction is taken from Siyānat Majmū‘ al-Fatāwā, p. 256.

---

## Page 1837

and the martyrs and the righteous. We must hold firmly to the rope of Allah all together and not become divided; enjoin what Allah has commanded—“what is right”—and forbid what He has prohibited—“what is wrong”; and strive for sincerity to Allah in our deeds. This is the religion of “Islam.” Allah Most High says:

“Yes, whoever submits himself to Allah and does good will have his reward with his Lord. No fear will be upon them, nor will they grieve.”

And He Most High says:

“Who is better in religion than one who submits himself to Allah, does good, and follows the religion of Abraham, upright in faith? And Allah took Abraham as an intimate friend.”

And He Most High says:

“When they commit an indecency, they say, ‘We found our fathers doing this, and Allah commanded us to do it.’ Say, ‘Allah does not command indecency. Do you say about Allah what you do not know?’”

“Say, ‘My Lord has commanded justice. Devote yourselves to Him at every place of prayer, and call upon Him with sincere devotion to Him alone. As He brought you into being, so you will return.’”

“He has guided one group, while another has incurred misguidance. They have taken devils as allies besides Allah, yet think they are rightly guided.”

And He Most High says:

“O you who believe, fear Allah as He should be feared, and do not die except as Muslims”—up to His words—“On the Day when some faces will brighten and some faces will darken.”

Ibn ‘Abbās said: “The faces of the people of the Sunnah and the community will brighten, and the faces of the people of religious innovation and division will darken.”

And He Most High says:

“Indeed, those who have divided their religion and become sects—you have nothing to do with them.”

---

## Page 1838

And He Most High says:

“They were commanded only to worship Allah with sincere devotion to Him alone, upright in faith, to establish prayer and give zakah. That is the upright religion.”

False attribution is not confined to this “shrine.” Other shrines ascribed to prophets and others are likewise falsely attributed. Examples include the grave called “Noah’s grave,” near Baalbek at the foot of Mount Lebanon, and the grave on the Mecca-facing side of the Great Mosque of Damascus called “Hūd’s grave,” which is actually the grave of Mu‘āwiyah ibn Abī Sufyān. Another is the grave in eastern Damascus called “Ubayy ibn Ka‘b’s grave,” although scholars agree that Ubayy never came to Damascus.

The same applies to the purported graves in Damascus of “the Prophet’s wives,” may Allah bless him and grant him peace, whereas they died in the Prophet’s city, Medina. Likewise, the purported graves in Egypt of “‘Alī ibn al-Husayn,” “Ja‘far al-Sādiq,” and others are falsely attributed, by scholarly agreement: ‘Alī ibn al-Husayn and Ja‘far al-Sādiq both died in Medina.

‘Abd al-‘Azīz al-Kinānī said—in the well-known report—“None of the prophets’ graves has been established with certainty except the grave of ‘our Prophet.’” Another added: “And the grave of ‘the Intimate Friend’ as well.”

The reason scholars differ concerning the locations of graves is that accurately recording this information is not part of the religion. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, prohibited taking graves as places of worship. Since knowing their locations was not part of the religion, keeping an exact record of them was not obligatory.

---

## Page 1839

As for the knowledge with which Allah sent His Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, it is accurately preserved and safeguarded, as He Most High says:

“Indeed, it is We who sent down the Reminder, and indeed, We will preserve it.”

The collections of authentic hadith report that he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“A group of my community will continue to prevail upon the truth, unharmed by those who oppose or abandon them, until the Hour comes.”

These falsehoods originate in misguidance, religious innovation, and polytheism. Those who have gone astray imagined that undertaking journeys to these shrines, praying and making supplications at them, dedicating vows to them, kissing and touching them, and similar practices were acts of righteousness and religious devotion. I have even seen a large book compiled by one of the leaders of the Rāfidah, Muhammad ibn al-Nu‘mān, known as al-Shaykh al-Mufīd, the teacher of the man known as al-Murtadā and of Abū Ja‘far al-Tūsī. He entitled it “Pilgrimage to Visit the Shrines.” In it, he cited reports attributed to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and his household concerning visits and pilgrimages to these shrines, the like of which he did not cite concerning pilgrimage to Allah’s Sacred House.

Most of what he cited consists of the most obvious lies and blatant fabrications. Indeed, I found more lies and fabrications in it than I have found in many Jewish and Christian books. These practices were originally invented and fabricated by hypocrites and heretics to turn people away from Allah’s path and corrupt their religion of Islam. They introduced for them the very foundation of polytheism, which is opposed to devoting worship sincerely to Allah alone, as Ibn ‘Abbās and others among the early generations explained concerning the words of the Most High about Noah’s people:

“And they said, ‘Do not abandon your gods; do not abandon Wadd or Suwā‘

---

## Page 1840

nor Yaghūth, Ya‘ūq, or Nasr.’”

“And they have led many astray.”

They explained: These were the names of righteous men among Noah’s people. When they died, people remained at their graves in devotion and then made statues of them. Al-Bukhārī recorded this in his Sahīh and elaborated on and explained it at the beginning of his book, in the accounts of the prophets and elsewhere.

For this reason, a group of polytheistic Sabian philosophers composed works in support of this polytheism. They joined the esoteric Qarmatians in defying Allah and His Messenger, leading many nations astray and turning them away from Allah’s religion. At the very least, their hallmark became leaving mosques unused and venerating shrines. For they venerate shrines, undertake pilgrimages to them, and engage in polytheistic practices involving them in ways that neither Allah nor His Messenger nor any of the leading religious scholars has commanded. Rather, Allah and His Messenger have forbidden His believing servants to do so.

As for the mosques that Allah has commanded to be raised and His name to be mentioned within, they lay waste to them. At times, they perform neither Friday prayers nor congregational prayers, relying on one of the branches of hypocrisy they have established as a principle: that prayer is valid only behind an infallible person, along with similar errors of theirs.

The first to introduce the doctrine of ‘Alī’s infallibility and his explicit designation for the caliphate was the leader of these hypocrites, “‘Abd Allāh ibn Saba’,” who had been a Jew, then professed Islam outwardly, intending to corrupt the religion of Islam just as Paul corrupted the religion of the Christians. The Commander of the Faithful, ‘Alī ibn Abī Tālib, intended to kill this man after learning that he was reviling Abū Bakr and ‘Umar, until the man fled from him.

---

## Page 1841

just as ‘Alī burned the extremists who claimed that he was divine, and said concerning those who held him superior:

“Anyone brought before me who considers me superior to Abū Bakr and ‘Umar, I will flog as a slanderer is flogged.”

Through such lies and slander, these misguided fabricators—hypocrites who follow heretics—abandon Islam’s distinctive observances and fail to uphold its pillar, foremost among these observances being the practices of guidance established by the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Thus, they perform neither Friday prayers nor congregational prayers.

Someone who holds this belief may equate shrines with mosques, treating acts of worship—such as prayer, supplication, recitation, remembrance of Allah, and other acts—as prescribed at graves just as they are prescribed in mosques. He may even, through his conduct or words, give preference to worship at graves and shrines over worship in the houses of Allah, which are the mosques. You may therefore find that when one of them wishes to devote himself earnestly to supplication, repentance, or similar acts, he goes to the grave of someone he venerates, whether his shaykh or someone else. There he exerts himself in supplication, humble entreaty, reverence, and tenderness of heart to a degree he does not match in mosques, in the hours before dawn, or when prostrating to Allah, the One, the All-Subduing.

Many of their ignorant followers have gone so far as to invoke the dead and seek their aid, just as Christians seek the aid of the Messiah and his mother. They ask the dead to relieve distress, facilitate the fulfillment of their requests, grant victory over enemies, remove calamities and afflictions, and accomplish other such things that none but the Lord of the earth and heaven can accomplish. Indeed, when one of them wishes to perform Hajj, his chief concern is not the obligation imposed by

---

## Page 1842

Allah upon him—“pilgrimage to Allah’s Sacred House,” the distinguishing rite of the upright, monotheistic faith of Abraham, the leader of those who follow Allah’s religion. Instead, he heads for Medina.

Nor does he seek what the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, encouraged: prayer in his mosque. He said in an authentic hadith:

“One prayer in this mosque of mine is better than a thousand prayers elsewhere, except in the Sacred Mosque.”

He pays no heed to Allah’s commands to invoke blessings and peace upon His Messenger wherever he may be, to obey his commands, follow his Sunnah, support him, and revere him. This entails loving him more than one’s family, wealth, and all other people—indeed, more than oneself. Instead, by visiting his grave or someone else’s, he seeks things that neither Allah nor His Messenger commanded, that his Companions never practised, and that the leading scholars of the religion never approved.

His desire to visit the Prophet’s grave during Hajj may exceed his desire to perform Hajj itself, or he may give the two aims equal weight. All of this is a departure from the religion, by the agreement of the Muslims. Indeed, undertaking a journey specifically to visit any grave—whether that of a prophet or anyone else—is prohibited according to the majority of scholars. They do not even permit intending to pray there, on the grounds that it is a journey undertaken in disobedience to Allah. This is based on his statement, authentically recorded in the two Sahīh collections:

“No journey is to be undertaken except to three mosques: the Sacred Mosque, al-Aqsā Mosque, and this mosque of mine.”

He was the most knowledgeable of people concerning such a matter. Every hadith narrated concerning visiting the grave is weak—indeed, fabricated. Indeed, even

---

## Page 1843

Mālik and other leading scholars of Medina disapproved of saying, “I visited the Prophet’s grave, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.” Rather, the Sunnah is simply to greet him with peace when coming to his grave, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, as the Companions and their successors used to do when they came to his grave, as explained elsewhere.

Another such practice is circumambulating something other than the Kaaba. Muslims agree that circumambulation is prescribed only around the much-frequented House. It is therefore impermissible to circumambulate the Rock in Jerusalem, the chamber of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, the dome on Mount ‘Arafāt, or anything else. Likewise, Muslims agree that ritual touching and kissing are prescribed only for the two Yemeni corners: the Black Stone is touched and kissed, while the Yemeni Corner is touched.

It has also been said that the Yemeni Corner should be kissed, but this is a weak opinion. As for anything else, neither ritual touching nor kissing is prescribed, including the sides of the House, its two Syrian corners, the Station of Abraham, the Rock, the Prophet’s chamber, and all other graves of prophets and righteous people.

In the two Sahīh collections, Abū Hurayra, may Allah be pleased with him, narrated that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“May Allah destroy the Jews and Christians: they took the graves of their prophets as places of worship.”

In a version recorded by Muslim:

“May Allah curse the Jews and Christians: they took the graves of their prophets as places of worship.”

---

## Page 1844

Also in the two Sahīh collections, ‘Ā’isha and Ibn ‘Abbās narrated:

“When Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was in the throes of death, he began placing a cloak over his face. Whenever it made breathing difficult, he would uncover his face. While in that state, he said: ‘May Allah curse the Jews and Christians: they took the graves of their prophets as places of worship.’”

He was warning against what they had done.

Also in the two Sahīh collections, ‘Ā’isha said:

“During the illness from which he never recovered, Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: ‘May Allah curse the Jews and Christians: they took the graves of their prophets as places of worship.’ Were it not for that, his grave would have been left exposed, but he feared that it might be taken as a place of worship.”

In Sahīh Muslim, Jundub ibn ‘Abd Allāh said:

“I heard Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, say five days before his death: ‘I declare before Allah that I take none of you as an intimate friend, for Allah has taken me as an intimate friend just as He took Abraham as an intimate friend. Were I to take anyone from my community as an intimate friend, I would take Abū Bakr. Indeed, those before you took the graves of their prophets as places of worship. So do not take graves as places of worship, for I forbid you to do so.’”

In Sahīh Muslim, Abū Marthad al-Ghanawī narrated that Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Do not sit on graves or pray towards them.”

Abū Sa‘īd al-Khudrī, may Allah be pleased with him, narrated that Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The entire earth is a place of prayer except graveyards and bathhouses.”

This was reported by the compilers of the Sunan collections;

---

## Page 1845

such as Abū Dāwūd, al-Tirmidhī and Ibn Mājah. Some considered it defective because it was reported directly from a Successor without an intervening Companion, while the hadith master authenticated it (*).

In the two Sahīh collections, ‘Ā’isha, may Allah be pleased with her, said:

“When the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, fell ill, one of his wives mentioned to him that she had seen a church in Abyssinia called Māriya. Umm Salama and Umm Habība had both been to Abyssinia, and they described its beauty and the images inside it. He raised his head and said: ‘When a righteous man among those people died, they would build a place of worship over his grave and make those images inside it. Those are the worst of creation in Allah’s sight.’”

Ibn ‘Abbās, may Allah be pleased with him, said:

“Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, cursed women who visit graves and those who establish places of worship over them and place lamps upon them.”

This was reported by the compilers of the Sunan collections, such as Abū Dāwūd, al-Nasā’ī and al-Tirmidhī. Al-Tirmidhī graded the hadith good; some copies read “authentic.”

In Mālik’s Muwatta’, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is reported to have said:

“O Allah, do not make my grave an idol that is worshipped.”

In Sunan Abī Dāwūd, he is reported to have said:

“Do not make my grave a place of festivity, and do not make your homes graveyards.”

As for acts of worship in mosques, such as prayer, recitation, supplication and the like, Allah Most High has said:

“Who is more unjust than those who prevent Allah’s name from being mentioned in His mosques and strive for

their destruction.” Allah Most High also says:

In al-Tirmidhī, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is reported to have said:

“If you see a man frequenting the mosque, bear witness to his faith, for Allah Most High says: ‘The mosques of Allah are maintained only by those who believe in Allah’—and the rest of the verse.”

Allah Most High says:

“Say: ‘My Lord has commanded justice,’ and turn your faces toward Him at every place of worship”—and the rest of the verse.

Allah Most High also says:
